# 03 · Component library

Every component in the project. Build once, reuse everywhere: a page section is composed from these, never from one-off markup.

All of them are rendered live and in isolation at **`/styleguide`**, which is the place to look at one without loading a whole page.

**Before changing any of these, Claude asks whether to update the existing component or create a new one** — see rule 3 in [01-ways-of-working.md](01-ways-of-working.md).

Status legend: **Built** · **Not built**

---

## Atoms and molecules

### Button — Built
`components/Button.tsx` · recipe: `button`

Pill button. Renders as a link when given `href`, otherwise a real `<button>`.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `variant` | `primary` (solid orange) or `secondary` (outline). Default primary |
| `href` | Makes it a link. Omit for a button |
| `disabled` | Button form only |

Secondary uses `currentColor`, so it reads correctly on light and dark sections with no extra prop. Every state keeps the 44px tap target.

**Used by:** Header, MobileNav, hero, final CTA band.

### Eyebrow — Built
`components/Eyebrow.tsx` · recipe: `eyebrow`

The small uppercase label above a heading.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `tone` | **Required, no default.** `dark` (`#ABB5C0`) or `light` (`#17191E`) for an eyebrow paired with a heading; `muted` (grey, dims against the section) for a standalone label |
| `as` | The HTML tag. Defaults to `<p>`; use `span` when nesting inside other text |
| `size` | Overrides the default Overline size. Only for a confirmed real-design difference — e.g. Hero's mobile eyebrow measures ~16px against Figma, not Overline's fixed 20px |

**Standing sitewide rule (owner call, 2026-08-24): an eyebrow paired with a heading is always `#ABB5C0` on a dark/black section and always `#17191E` on a light/white section, no exceptions.** This replaced an earlier `accent` (orange) default that had never actually been confirmed against Figma for most sections using it — Hero's own eyebrow included, which was accent orange until this rule was applied and changed it to `#ABB5C0`. `tone` has no default now specifically so a caller can't silently inherit the wrong one — every section states which background it's on. `muted` is unrelated and untouched: a standalone label not paired with a heading (Marquee's ticker label, LogoRow's label), dimmed to whatever colour the section already sets.

**Used by:** SectionHeading, LogoRow, Marquee.

### Chip / Tag — Built
`components/Chip.tsx` · recipe: `chip`

Category or filter pill. Becomes a link with `href`, a button with `onClick`, or a plain static label with neither.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `active` | Filled orange treatment for the selected chip |
| `href` / `onClick` | Which interactive form to render |

Interactive forms carry the 44px tap target; a static label does not, since it is not a tap target. No Figma component defines a chip, so its type style (Button Small, the smallest in the scale) is a role match, not a Figma fact.

### SectionHeading — Built
`components/SectionHeading.tsx` · recipe: `sectionHeading`

Eyebrow plus H2. No intro line by design — that is the copy rule for this component.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `eyebrow` | The small uppercase label |
| `heading` | The H2 text |
| `eyebrowTone` | **Required.** `"dark"` or `"light"` — see the standing rule on `Eyebrow`'s own entry above |
| `eyebrowSize` | Overrides the eyebrow's default Overline size — see below |
| `align` | `"left"` (default) or `"center"` — see below |
| `headingClassName` | Extra classes for the `<h2>` itself, e.g. a `max-width` to force a confirmed Figma wrap point |

The heading takes no colour of its own, so it inherits dark or light from the section around it. Type styles are Figma's own "Section Header" pairing — Overline over Heading 1 — read from Figma, not chosen. The element stays an `<h2>` regardless, since Heading 1 is a type style, not a document heading level.

**`align="center"`**, added 2026-08-23 for Certified & Compliant: confirmed via `get_design_context` that its heading is genuinely centred (both eyebrow and H2, text and block), unlike Trust Signals and What We Make which are genuinely left-aligned — a real variant, not a one-off hack. Implemented as `sectionHeading.alignCenter` (`items-center text-center`) composed onto `root` only when requested; every existing left-aligned usage is unchanged.

**Confirmed against real Figma 2026-08-23** (What We Make, node 366:130/366:162 — the first real section to use this component), correcting two things that had shipped as unverified guesses:
- `root`'s eyebrow-to-heading gap was a flat, unconfirmed 12px — corrected to the real 16px mobile / 24px desktop.
- The heading matches `text-h1` exactly on desktop (54px/500/64px leading), but **not** on mobile: the token's fluid low anchor already gets the *size* right (30px), but not the weight or line-height — Figma's real mobile instance is 460/34px, not the token's 500/~35.6px. Scoped as a one-off in `sectionHeading.heading` (fixed mobile values, `xl:text-h1` above) rather than changing the shared `--text-h1` token, since there's no confirmation yet that other `text-h1` headings (e.g. Hero's) share this same mobile discrepancy — worth re-checking if one is confirmed later.

The `eyebrowSize` prop exists because What We Make's real mobile eyebrow is 16px, not the default Overline 20px — the same value Hero's own mobile eyebrow already used (`hero.eyebrowSize`). Overriding a type token's *size* alone silently drops whatever else that token bundles — Overline's paired 600 weight, in this case — so both `hero.eyebrowSize` and this component's `eyebrowSize` explicitly re-set `font-semibold` for mobile too. This was a real, live bug in Hero's mobile eyebrow (rendering at the browser's default 400 weight, not 600) until caught here and fixed retroactively.

**Threshold moved from `xl:`/`max-xl:` (1280px) to `md:`/`max-md:` (768px), homepage-wide (owner, 2026-09-04): "should we use the font sizes used on the desktop [at tablet]... increase the font sizes as per best practices for tablet"** — every homepage heading/eyebrow following this component's `max-xl:MOBILE xl:DESKTOP` shape rendered at its mobile size all the way through 1279px tablet width, then jumped hard to desktop size only at 1280px. Verified mathematically before changing anything: `text-h1`'s fluid `clamp()` (`app/globals.css`) already evaluates to ~39px at exactly 768px — well above the 30px mobile value it replaces and still growing continuously to 54px at 1440px — so moving the threshold is a pure, non-regressive change, not a new value. `sectionHeading.heading` and `hero.eyebrowSize`/`whatWeMake.eyebrowSize` (identical shape) all moved `max-xl:`→`max-md:`, `xl:`→`md:`. The same treatment (same reasoning, values unchanged) was applied at the same time to `whatWeMake.groupTitle`/`groupBody` (both fixed values, desktop always larger) and `capabilityCard.text` (shared by Our Services/How It Works, and also `ProductCustomizeSteps` on the PDP — a deliberate spillover, not an oversight, since the same "desktop size always ≥ mobile" reasoning holds there too). `FinalCta`'s own heading/subline pair needed no change — its desktop/mobile split is two entirely separate DOM blocks (not a single class with two prefixes), and that structural split was already moved to `md:` in an earlier fix this same session. Confirmed live: 44.8px (growing toward 54px) at 1024px where 30px rendered before; pixel-identical to the original values at 1440px and 375px; no horizontal overflow or unexpected 3-line heading wraps at 768px (the narrowest, highest-risk tablet width); full 30-test screenshot suite passes. `TrustSignals` and `Stats` were explicitly left out of this pass — their desktop/mobile split is the same two-separate-DOM-blocks shape as `FinalCta`'s, but hasn't had its own `md:` structural extension yet (unlike `FinalCta`), so a pure font-size change there would have no visible effect until that layout-level fix lands too.

### Card — Built
`components/Card.tsx` · recipes: `card`, `capabilityCard`, `cardMedia`

Two exports:

- **`Card`** — category tile: square image plus a centred label, the whole tile a link. Matches the "What We Make" tiles. Media radius is overridable via `mediaRadius` (`lg` default, `none` — What We Make's own confirmed 0px, 2026-08-26, same additive prop pattern as `CapabilityCard`'s `mediaRadius` below).
- **`CapabilityCard`** — image, title, body, not a link. Matches the "Our Services" tiles. Media aspect ratio is overridable via `mediaAspectClassName` (default `aspect-video`/16:9, a placeholder guess from before any real section used it); Our Services passes its own confirmed 7:5 mobile / 8:5 desktop ratio.

Both share an internal media area. With no `image` prop it renders a placeholder box (no border — sitewide owner call, 2026-08-24, see MediaPlaceholder below); passing `image` swaps in a real optimised image with no other change, so photography can land later without a rebuild. `Card`'s label uses Heading 5 (confirmed an exact match against real Figma 2026-08-23, no longer a guess) — sized up to 30px on mobile only (owner call, 2026-08-26; desktop stays the real 24px), see the design-system note in [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md). `CapabilityCard`'s title follows Figma's "Feature Card" — Heading 3.

`card.root`'s image-to-label gap was also corrected 2026-08-23, from an unconfirmed 12px (`gap-3`) to the real 24px (`gap-6`) — same story as `sectionHeading.root` above: nothing had used `Card` in a real section yet, so its numbers were reasonable guesses until What We Make gave them a real Figma frame to check against.

**`CapabilityCard`'s own body text and media-to-text gap were corrected 2026-08-25** (Our Services, node `415:5449`/`415:5484` — this component's first real usage, same "confirmed against its first real section" story as `Card` above): body was `text-body-lg text-muted` (18px-ish, dimmed) → real design is plain near-black, 20px/28px leading desktop, 18px/24px leading mobile, no dimming. The media-to-text gap was a flat 16px → real design is 16px mobile, 24px desktop.

**`CardMedia`'s radius is now an explicit, mutually-exclusive prop (`radius: "lg" | "none"`), not a class baked into `cardMedia.base`** (corrected 2026-08-25, Our Services' media placeholders are confirmed 0px radius on both breakpoints). `cardMedia.base` no longer carries `rounded-lg` at all — appending a second `rounded-*` class alongside it would risk the exact cascade-order bug this project has hit before (two same-specificity utilities, order in the generated stylesheet decides the winner, not order in the class string), so each caller now states its radius explicitly instead. `Card` (What We Make's tiles) keeps `radius="lg"` (the default, unchanged); `CapabilityCard` exposes it as `mediaRadius` (default `"lg"`, Our Services passes `"none"`).

**`CapabilityCard` gained full-replacement size overrides (`rootClassName`, `bodyClassName`, `titleClassName`)** for its second real usage, How It Works (2026-08-25) — that section's confirmed title size and gaps differ from Our Services' (28px desktop/24px mobile title vs Our Services' flat 30px, 32px/16px gaps vs 24px/16px). Each prop defaults to Our Services' existing values (that section is unaffected) and replaces the whole class string rather than appending a second one, same cascade-order reasoning as the radius prop above. The 24px mobile title was itself sized up to 30px on 2026-08-26 (sitewide mobile-title bump), so mobile now matches Our Services' 30px while desktop keeps its own 28px.

**`FeatureNumbered` was removed (2026-08-25)** — its only anticipated usage was always How It Works, built around a step-number-on-the-media-box guess that neither real Figma frame (desktop `430:1929`, mobile `430:1961`) actually shows. That section uses plain `CapabilityCard`s instead, same as Our Services.

**Used by:** How It Works.

### StatBlock — Built
`components/StatBlock.tsx` · recipe: `stat`

Big number plus caption. Built for the dark Stats band only, so it hardcodes light text rather than adapting — there is no light-section use for it. Type styles are Figma's own "Stat Card" pairing: Heading 1 for the value, Body Large for the caption — not the Stat Number / Stat Label styles, which exist in the scale but no component reads them.

### MediaPlaceholder — Built
`components/MediaPlaceholder.tsx` · recipe: `media`

The shared section-level media box, standing in for photography and video until assets land.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `label` | Text shown in the empty box; becomes the alt text once an image is set |
| `ratio` | `16:9` (hero video, service tiles), `4:5` (portrait product), `1:1` (category tiles) |
| `radius` | `lg` or `xl` |
| `tone` | `light` or `dark`, for placement on ink sections |
| `overlay` | Something rendered on top, e.g. the hero play button. The label drops to the bottom so the two never collide |
| `showLabel` | Default true. Set false when the overlay already carries its own caption (the Hero's "Play Video" label under the play button) — otherwise the placeholder's own caption renders as a second, redundant one. `label` still supplies the image alt text either way |
| `image` | Swaps the placeholder for real photography |

The shell carries the real radius; the placeholder fill, overlay and real image all take `rounded-[inherit]` so they match it exactly, rather than showing square corners inside a rounded box (see the radius note in [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md)). **Neither tone has a border** — dark-tone placeholders never did (confirmed against Figma 2026-08-23); light-tone placeholders had one for definition on a pale background until 2026-08-24, when the owner asked for it to be removed sitewide, on every section, not just the one being worked on at the time.

### LogoRow — Built
`components/LogoRow.tsx` · recipe: `logoRow`

A wrapping, centred row of logos. Without image files each item falls back to a labelled outline box of the same size.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `logos` | The list. Each item is a name, optionally with an image |
| `label` | Optional eyebrow above the row |
| `greyscale` | Client logos rest greyscale and come to colour on hover. Set `false` for certification badges, which are meaningful marks |

### Marquee — Built
`components/Marquee.tsx` · recipe: `marquee` · animation in `app/globals.css`

Scrolling ticker. Zero client JavaScript: the track holds the item list twice and travels exactly -50%, so the loop is seamless at any content width without measuring anything.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `items` | The running list |
| `label` | Static label before the ticker |
| `durationSeconds` | One full pass. Longer lists need longer to keep the pace even |
| `tone` | `light` or `dark` band |
| `labelVariant` | `eyebrow` (default, muted uppercase), `bold` (bold sentence-case — the real "Fully Custom Offerings" ticker, confirmed against Figma), or `title` (a real `<h2>`, Heading 5 — Client Logos' desktop "Trusted by top brands worldwide," added 2026-08-26, referencing tedy.app, not Figma-confirmed) |
| `separator` | `slash` (default), `sparkle` (offerings ticker), or `none` (client logos ticker — no glyph between items, just space) |
| `layout` | `stacked` (default): label on its own row, ticker below, 32px gap — the real offerings ticker, at both breakpoints. `inline`: label beside the track, side by side — used by Client Logos' desktop title (2026-08-26); previously an unused guess, now a real usage |
| `gap` | `default` (40px, offerings ticker) or `loose` (72px, client logos ticker) — both confirmed against their own real Figma spacing, not interchangeable |
| `itemSize` | `body` (default, 18px — offerings ticker) or `lg` (20px, `#F2F2F7` — the compliance ticker's own confirmed size and colour, 2026-08-24) |
| `divider` | Default true: a bottom border marking the end of the band (offerings ticker). The client logos ticker sets `false` — no border in that real design |
| `pauseOnHover` | Default true (pauses on hover/focus-within, so a reader can stop the track). Client Logos' desktop/mobile tickers and Hero's "Fully Custom Offerings" ticker all set `false` (2026-08-26) — decorative content, not something to read, shouldn't stop moving on hover |
| `edgeFade` | Default false. Fades the track's left/right edges to transparent via a `mask-image` gradient (works over any background). Client Logos' desktop and mobile tickers (2026-08-26, referencing tedy.app). The fade width is a **fixed 20px** (`.marquee-edge-fade` in `app/globals.css`), not a percentage — a percentage-based fade widens on every viewport bigger than the one it was tuned on, hiding more logos than intended; a fixed px width stays exactly 20px regardless of the track's own width, keeping the maximum number of logos visible (corrected 2026-08-26 from an initial `8%`/`92%` percentage version) |
| `padded` | Default true: the standard 40px-top/32px-bottom internal padding (`marquee.basePaddingDefault`). Client Logos' mobile marquee sets this false (`marquee.basePaddingNone`) since its own wrapper already supplies the exact vertical rhythm and would otherwise double up with this padding |

**The `title` label variant's box-sizing bug, and what actually fixed it (2026-08-26)**: neither `w-fit` nor `display: table` shrinks a wrapping block down to its widest *rendered* line once the text no longer fits on one line — both simply resolve to the full available/max width instead (confirmed live and in isolation, including on a bare `display:table; max-width:320px` div with no other CSS at all). `display: table` is also blockified back to plain `block` when the element is itself a flex item, per the CSS Display spec, so it wouldn't have worked on the label directly anyway. What actually solved it: pick a deliberately tight, chosen `max-width` (200px) rather than trying to auto-compute one, on an `inline-block` (not a bare `span` — a plain inline element ignores `max-width`/`width` entirely) nested *inside* the real flex-item `<h2>` wrapper (`marquee.labelTitleWrap`), so the inline-block itself never has to fight blockification.

Item text is Body (18px, 400 regular) — corrected 2026-08-23 from a bolder caption style first guessed. The separator icon (when not `none`) leads every item, including each pass's first — Figma's static screenshot shows no icon before the very first "Design", but the two duplicated passes must render at identical widths for the `-50%` loop transform to land exactly on the seam; omitting the icon from only one pass's first item made that pass narrower, breaking the loop itself, not just the visible gap (fixed 2026-08-24). Whatever the gap value, it's carried as trailing space on every pass (`pr-10` / `pr-[72px]`), not just a gap between items, since the track has no gap of its own between the two `<ul>`s — the seam needs the same spacing as everywhere else, or the loop repeats with a visible jump.

Pauses on hover **and** on keyboard focus, so a keyboard user can read a moving item. Under reduced-motion it stops at its start frame and becomes a normal scrollable strip. The moving track is hidden from screen readers and the items are exposed once instead, so nothing is read twice.

**Desktop only.** On the real homepage, this ticker is hidden below `xl` (1280px) — the real mobile design isn't a narrower marquee, it's a plain vertical list (see Hero below), a genuinely different layout, not a mode of this component.

### TextReveal — Built
`components/TextReveal.tsx` (client component) · recipe: `textReveal` · animation in `app/globals.css`

Word-by-word entrance reveal: each word sits in its own `overflow-hidden` mask and slides up into place, staggered per word, when the element scrolls into view. Owner reference, 2026-08-27: `afternow.co/services/`.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `text` | The full sentence. Split on `"\n"` into lines first, then on spaces into words within each line — a literal `"\n"` renders as a real `<br />` between lines, not a character inside a word's own mask |
| `as` | Tag to render, e.g. `"h1"` for Hero's headline. Defaults to a plain `span` |
| `className` | Passed straight to the rendered tag — same styling contract as any heading |

**Masked per word, not per rendered line, unlike the reference.** The reference site measures which words land on the same visual line with JS, re-run on every resize (line breaks depend on viewport width), then masks per line. Masking per word instead gets the identical visual language — words rising out of a clipping box, staggered — with no resize-recalculation edge case.

**Why the mask doesn't clip a word's own descenders**: `overflow-hidden`'s auto height comes from the word's own normal layout box — `transform` never affects layout size, so the mask never grows or shrinks during the animation, and there's nothing to clip once the word is at rest. This is the exact bug already hit and fixed once on Client Logos' marquee title (a negative margin moved a box with its own background over a neighbour); this component was designed from the start to avoid that class of bug rather than needing a second fix.

**Real bug, found 2026-08-30 (`FabricOptions`' 2-line heading, owner report: "fabric section title should be in 2 lines")**: the original version split words with a bare `text.split(" ")` — a `"\n"` embedded in the source text doesn't contain a space, so it survived whole inside one word's own token (e.g. `"the\nbig"`), embedded raw inside that word's `overflow-hidden` animated mask span. That's not merely "failed to add a line break" — an embedded newline inside a masked, animated inline-block visibly scrambled the rendered word order across the intended break (`"The fabrics behind the brands" / "big"` instead of `"The fabrics behind the" / "big brands"`). Fixed by splitting on `"\n"` first into lines, rendering a real `<br />` between them, then splitting each line into words as before — the stagger animation's `--reveal-index` keeps incrementing globally across the break, so timing is unaffected. This is a shared, sitewide component (Hero and every other reveal heading route through it) — the fix applies anywhere a forced line break is ever needed through `TextReveal`, not just this one heading.

**Scroll-triggered via `IntersectionObserver`, not a play-on-mount CSS animation** (corrected 2026-08-27, the day this shipped — the first version animated unconditionally on mount, a plain `@keyframes` play, matching `Marquee`'s own zero-client-JS motion pattern). That worked for Hero (in view at load) but broke for every other usage: a below-the-fold section mounts at the same instant Hero does, so its animation had already finished — words sitting at their final resting state — long before a reader scrolled down to actually see it (owner report: "still only shows on the top banner text," confirmed live). `TextReveal` is now a small `"use client"` component (same category of component as `ScrollSpotlightList`, this project's other scroll-driven visual): each instance observes its own rendered tag, and the first time it's ≥30% in view, adds a `reveal-active` class and disconnects (one-shot, never re-triggers on scrolling back up). `.reveal-word`'s hidden resting state (`opacity: 0`, `translateY(110%)`) is now the unconditional default in `app/globals.css`; the `@keyframes` animation itself only runs under `.reveal-active .reveal-word`. Hero still animates immediately on page load in practice, since it's already in the viewport the instant the observer attaches.

**Accessibility**: the rendered tag carries the full sentence as `aria-label`; each per-word span is `aria-hidden`, so nothing is read twice or split word-by-word by a screen reader — the same pattern the reference itself uses. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables the animation entirely and shows the final position immediately, matching `Marquee`'s own existing reduced-motion handling.

**Used by:** Hero's H1 (`components/sections/Hero.tsx`) — same existing classes (`hero.heading` + `text-display`), only the children changed from a plain text node to the word-reveal markup. Scoped to the headline only, not the eyebrow or CTAs, since the request was specifically about the banner's headline text.

**Extended 2026-08-27 to every other section's eyebrow + title** (owner request: "apply the same to these section eyebrow and titles only"): What We Make, Certified & Compliant, Inside the Factory, How It Works (both breakpoints of each), plus Final CTA's title (no eyebrow there). Not applied via a new prop on `SectionHeading` itself — that would also animate Exhibitions' and Our Services' headings, which weren't asked for — each target section instead wraps its own `content.eyebrow`/`content.h2` directly: `eyebrow={<TextReveal text={content.eyebrow} />}`, `heading={<TextReveal as="span" text={content.h2} />}`, passed straight into `SectionHeading`'s existing `eyebrow`/`heading` props (both already accept `ReactNode`, not just strings, so this needed no `SectionHeading` changes at all).

How It Works' mobile heading is a special case: its own `renderMobileHeading` forces Figma's real 3-line break points via literal `<br/>`s around separately-rendered line strings (not one plain string), so each line gets its own `<TextReveal as="span">` instead of one call across the whole heading — the stagger restarts per line rather than continuing across all three, a simplification accepted since three independently-staggered groups still reads as one cohesive reveal. This surfaced a real accessibility bug, since fixed: each line's own `aria-label` fully replaces that line's accessible name, and `<br/>` alone doesn't reliably contribute a space between two `aria-label`-carrying siblings — the two lines' accessible names ran together ("to" + "shipped" → "toshipped", confirmed live via `textContent` before the fix). Fixed with a plain trailing space text node after each line's `TextReveal` (not part of any `aria-label`, so it can't be swallowed by one).

**`useRevealOnView` extracted 2026-08-27**: the `IntersectionObserver` logic above is now a small exported hook (`useRevealOnView<T extends HTMLElement>(threshold = 0.3)`, returning `{ ref, active }`), co-located in this file since it's the component that first needed it — same precedent as `useDesktopChevronScroller` (`components/DesktopChevronScroller.tsx`), a hook that lives with its first real usage and gets imported elsewhere once a second one exists. `RevealBox` (below) is that second usage.

### RevealBox — Built
`components/RevealBox.tsx` (client component) · classes: `reveal-box`/`reveal-box-active` · animation in `app/globals.css`

Generic "reveal once it's in view" wrapper for non-text content — a box animates in from a smaller, inward `clip-path` inset (not a plain fade) to its full size, the first time it scrolls into view. Owner reference, 2026-08-27: `cuberto.com`'s own hero video reveal — inspected live via `getComputedStyle` rather than guessed: its resting state is `opacity: 1`, `transform: translate(0,0)`, `clip-path: inset(0% round 2rem)`, confirming the "unfold from a cropped state" quality rather than a generic fade.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `children` | The content to reveal, e.g. a `MediaPlaceholder` |
| `className` | Passed straight to the wrapping `<div>` |

**A plain CSS transition, not `@keyframes`**, unlike `TextReveal`'s word-by-word stagger: there's no per-item stagger here, just one box toggling between two states, so a transition triggered by the `.reveal-box-active` class (set once `useRevealOnView`'s observer fires) is simpler than defining keyframes for a single state change. The clip-path animates inset only, no rounding — Hero's own video block keeps its existing square corners (`radius="none"`, a confirmed Figma call, unrelated to this reveal); a caller on a rounded box would need its own rounded `inset(... round Npx)` value, not currently parameterized since only one usage exists. `transition-delay: 0.2s` trails Hero's own headline stagger slightly, so the two don't pop in at the same instant.

`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` shows the box in its final position immediately, matching `TextReveal`'s own reduced-motion handling.

**Used by:** Hero's video block (`components/sections/Hero.tsx`) — wraps the existing `<MediaPlaceholder>` with no changes to `MediaPlaceholder` itself, since this reveal is opt-in per caller rather than a new default behaviour for every media block sitewide.

### PageTransition — Removed
Built 2026-08-29 as a sitewide page-to-page crossfade on real client-side navigation (owner reference: `cuberto.com/services`), using React's native `<ViewTransition>`. Corrected twice on timing/easing (owner reports: "very fast and jerky," then "not accurate" — the second correction was backed by frame-extracting an actual screen recording the owner supplied, `reference/Page animation.mov`), but the owner ultimately asked to remove it entirely rather than continue tuning it ("still the same problem, remove it"). Removed completely: `components/PageTransition.tsx` deleted, the `.page-fade-in`/`.page-fade-out`/`site-header` view-transition CSS removed from `app/globals.css`, `Header.tsx`'s `viewTransitionName` style removed, and both `app/page.tsx`/`app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx` unwrapped back to plain `<main>`/`<Footer>` with no transition wrapper.

**A real, separate bug surfaced while this was live, and initially looked like part of the same problem but wasn't** — see `ScrollReset`'s own entry below for what it actually was and why removing `PageTransition` didn't fix it on its own.

### ScrollReset — Built
`components/ScrollReset.tsx` (client component), rendered once in `app/layout.tsx`

**A real bug, found live 2026-08-30**: navigating via `<Link>` between the homepage and any other route (either direction) left the reader scrolled to wherever they'd been on the *previous* page, clamped to the new page's own shorter height — landing on its footer instead of its top ("stuck on the footer"). First assumed (reasonably, since it appeared at the same time) to be caused by `PageTransition`'s `<ViewTransition>` wrapper — but confirmed the two are actually independent: removing `PageTransition` entirely did **not** fix the scroll bug (re-tested and reproduced the exact same clamped-scroll symptom with zero transition code left anywhere in the app). The real, narrower cause: Next's own built-in scroll-to-top-on-navigation only fires reliably when the outgoing and incoming routes share a common nested layout segment (confirmed: `/activewear/leggings` → `/activewear/shorts`, both under the same `[category]/page.tsx` parent, already resets correctly on its own) — crossing the top-level boundary between `/` and any other route does not reset scroll on this app, independent of `PageTransition` or any other custom code here.

Confirmed live that a plain `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` after such a navigation has *no effect at all* until called with an explicit `behavior: "instant"`, and even then only from a `useLayoutEffect`, not a plain `useEffect` (the deferred timing of a plain effect left a window where the browser had already clamped the stale scroll offset to the new, shorter page before the reset could run).

Scrolls to the top on every real link-driven (push) navigation, but deliberately not on browser back/forward (pop) — those already get the browser's own correct native scroll restoration, which this must not override. Distinguishes the two via a `popstate` listener setting a ref that the next pathname-change effect checks and clears.

Rendered once in `app/layout.tsx` (a single instance for the whole app, reacting to every `usePathname()` change), next to the existing `AppEntryMarker` — same "small, render-nothing, side-effect-only client component mounted once in the root layout" shape. Has no dependency on `PageTransition` at all — confirmed both directions (`/` → `/activewear/leggings` and back) reset to `scrollY: 0` with `PageTransition` fully removed.

Removed entirely rather than left half-right: `components/PhotoCollage.tsx` deleted, `hero.bannerTextCol`/`.bannerPhotoCol`/`photoCollage` recipe keys removed from `components/ui/styles.ts`, the `.photo-collage-*` rules removed from `app/globals.css`, `content/home.ts`'s `hero.factoryShots` removed, and `Hero.tsx`'s banner reverted to its original single-column layout. ### Accordion — Built
`components/Accordion.tsx` · recipe: `accordion`

FAQ disclosure list. One open at a time.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `items` | Question and answer pairs |
| `defaultOpen` | Index open on load. Omit to start collapsed |

Real buttons inside headings, so Tab, Enter and Space all work with no custom key handling. The panel stays permanently mounted (never `display:none`) so its open/close can animate — `aria-hidden` keeps a collapsed answer out of screen readers instead.

**Used on the real homepage** (FAQ, section 13). Corrected on this first real use against Figma node `438:2150` (desktop) / `438:2192` (mobile) — every guessed value below turned out wrong, the same "correct on first real use" pattern already applied to `Card`/`SectionHeading`, plus a further owner pass after that correction landed:

- **Question size**: 30px on desktop, 22px on mobile. Neither is the guessed 24px this text never actually was, so the sitewide mobile-title 24→30px bump (2026-08-26) doesn't apply here — it was never a 24px title. **Weight is regular (400)** — Figma's own export literally showed medium/500, matched at first, then set to regular on direct owner instruction (2026-08-26): the visual call overrides the literal export here. `flex-1` on the question span so it fills the full width left by the icon before wrapping — without it the span shrinks to its own preferred width and wraps a line early, most visible on mobile's narrower column (a real bug, found and fixed the same day).
- **Answer**: 18px mobile / 20px-with-28px-leading desktop, regular weight, the **same muted `#838d97` colour on both breakpoints** — shipped once with full white on desktop (matching Figma's own literal export) before the owner asked for the mobile tone everywhere; no token matches this hex, same one-off already used for Hero's mobile ticker item.
- **Hover colour**: the trigger's hover state is also `#838D97`, not the brand accent orange — an accent hover read as a link/CTA, which a disclosure trigger isn't.
- **Open/close is animated**, not an instant snap: `grid-template-rows` transitions between `0fr`/`1fr` (300ms ease-in-out) on the panel wrapper, with an inner `overflow-hidden` div clipping content during the transition — the classic CSS-only accordion technique, avoiding the `hidden`-attribute snap the first pass shipped with.
- **Padding model**: padding wraps the whole item (question row + answer together), not just the trigger button, so opening/closing an item never moves the border — desktop `py-8` (32px), mobile `py-6` (24px). Desktop has no horizontal item padding (the section's own `container-p` already insets it); mobile adds `px-5` (20px) per item, since the mobile section itself is edge-to-edge with no outer horizontal padding (a real gap found and fixed after the first pass shipped with no mobile `px` at all, letting text touch the viewport edge). Gap between question and answer is `mt-6` (24px) desktop, `mt-4` (16px) mobile, on the **answer text itself**, not a flex `gap` on the item.
- **Real bug, found 2026-08-30 (reported on both the homepage and the Activewear PLP)**: "it looks uneven there too, from the top it looks lesser than the bottom of the question. When the question is expanded then it looks fine." The panel stays mounted at zero height when collapsed (`grid-rows-[0fr]`), but this spacing originally lived as a flex `gap` between the trigger and that always-present panel `div` — a flex `gap` reserves its full value even when the sibling is zero height, so a collapsed item measured `32px` above the question (padding-top only) but `57px` below it (padding-bottom + the gap the invisible panel still claimed). Fixed by moving the value onto `answer`'s own `mt-4`/`xl:mt-6` instead: it now lives inside `panelInner`'s `overflow-hidden` wrapper, where it's genuinely clipped to zero at collapsed (zero) height rather than merely invisible, and renders exactly as before once expanded. Collapsed items now measure `32px`/`33px` (rounding) above/below; expanded items are unaffected (`32` → `24` → `33`).
- **Border**: every item gets a bottom border, including the last (Figma shows a trailing rule); none above the first (dropped the earlier `first:border-t`). Genuine one-off colour per breakpoint, no matching token: `#29292e` mobile, `#3c3c43` desktop, via mutually exclusive `max-xl:`/`xl:` classes.
- **Icon**: a real plus glyph closed, a real minus glyph open (`lucide-react`'s `Plus`/`Minus`, swapped by `isOpen`) — not a plus rotated 45° into an X, which is what the pre-real-design guess used.

### Logo — Built
`components/Logo.tsx` · sized via `header.brandLogo`/`header.brandLogoDesktop` where it's used

The Capriowear wordmark — "CAPRIO" plus a smaller "WEAR" mark (Figma node `680:394`) — inlined as an SVG component rather than a static file in `/public`. Replaces the earlier CAPRIO-only mark (Figma node `316:1331`, 137x26) sitewide, per owner request. Inlining is what lets it pick up `currentColor` (the source file hardcoded a white fill), matching the same pattern `Button` secondary, `Chip` and `Eyebrow` muted already use for light/dark portability. Takes standard SVG props (`className` for sizing) plus two booleans, `stacked` and `footer`; no colour or variant props otherwise.

**Three geometries, one component:**
- **Default** (`stacked`/`footer` both unset) — a `217.2x22.76` combined viewBox, "WEAR" set *beside* "CAPRIO" via a horizontal `transform="translate(151 6.379)"`. Built 2026-09-02 as an approximation decoded from a screenshot, not a direct Figma export. `IntroLoader` is the only place still using it — every real page mark has since moved to `stacked` or `footer` (see below), so treat this as the retiring geometry rather than the "normal" one.
- **`stacked`** — the real Figma geometry (node `680:394`), a combined viewBox with "WEAR" set *below* "CAPRIO" (not beside it) via a vertical `transform="translate(...)"`, Figma's own measured offset. Owner, 2026-09-06: "update the caprio logo in the nav bar, not in the footer. Only for desktop" — first wired to `Header.tsx`'s desktop (`xl:`) brand mark only, via `header.brandLogoDesktop`. Extended three times more the same day: (1) owner: "Update the mobile nav caprio logo: Use the same logo size, not in the footer only header" (Figma node `587:6968`, confirmed the same `680:394` base component scaled for the mobile instance) — now also used by the header's own mobile/drawer mark via `header.brandLogo`; (2) Footer's own desktop mark (`footer.desktopBrandLogo`, Figma node `587:5755`) — **since superseded, see `footer` below**; (3) Footer's own mobile mark (`footer.mobileBrandLogo`, Figma node `590:1421`, "Update the mobile footer logo") — confirmed with the owner that each extension was intentional, not a stray edit, when the codebase kept being found already ahead of what this doc had recorded. `Logo`'s default geometry now has no real-page consumer left at all; `IntroLoader` is its sole remaining usage.
  **Re-measured 2026-09-06** (owner: "updated the desktop nav logo, update it again", same Figma link) — node `680:394`'s own frame had been nudged smaller in Figma since the first pull (`141x38` → `137.29x37`); the viewBox/paths/translate offset were re-exported to match. Same shape (WEAR below CAPRIO, still ~3.7:1 wide vs. the default geometry's ~9.5:1), just current numbers — re-check this node before trusting the dimensions here if the geometry is ever questioned again.
- **`footer`** — Footer's own desktop mark, re-fetched as its own real node for the first time (2026-09-07, owner: "updated the footer caprio logo. Update it", `587:5755`'s own `Logo` child, node `787:1073`). Until this point Footer's desktop mark reused `stacked` on the assumption the footer and header marks were the same asset; `get_metadata` on `787:1073` now returns a genuinely different bounding box (`224x57.69`, a different aspect ratio from `stacked`'s `137.29x37` for header node `680:394`, not just a different scale) — the two nodes have diverged in Figma, confirmed by pulling this node directly rather than assuming it still matches. Only Footer's desktop mark (`footer.desktopBrandLogo`, now `h-[57.69px]`, was `h-[55px]`) uses this; Footer's mobile mark still uses `stacked`, unchanged — this request named the footer's own Figma link specifically, not the mobile one, and no Figma frame confirms mobile ever matched this node either. Re-check node `787:1073` (not `680:394`) before trusting these dimensions if the footer logo is ever questioned again.

Path data for all three geometries is this file's own direct copy of Figma's SVG export, not hand-approximated.

**Used by:** Header (`stacked` geometry at both breakpoints — see Header's own entry below), Footer (`footer` geometry desktop, `stacked` mobile), `MobileNav`'s drawer (inherits whatever `Header` passes as `logo`), `IntroLoader` (default geometry only).

### IntroLoader — Built, no longer live
`components/IntroLoader.tsx` · recipe: `introLoader` · styleguide only (`app/styleguide/IntroLoaderDemo.tsx`) — removed from `app/page.tsx` 2026-09-06 (owner request); component and its `AppEntryMarker`/`lib/pageEntry.ts` support left in the library, not deleted

A brief, one-time entrance animation: the CAPRIO wordmark wipes into view over a full-screen black overlay, holds briefly, then fades to reveal the homepage underneath (owner request, 2026-08-27). No props — self-contained.

- **A pure visual overlay, not a content gate.** The homepage's real HTML (Header, Hero, `<h1>`, everything) renders and is present in the server response exactly as it always was — confirmed via `curl`, the `<h1>` and its full text sit in the markup regardless of this component. The overlay only affects what a real browser visually shows for well under a second; nothing here can cost SEO or Core Web Vitals.
- **Reveal mechanism**: `clip-path: inset()` sweeping left to right (`inset(0 100% 0 0)` → `inset(0 0 0 0)`, 600ms ease-out), not an SVG stroke-dasharray draw — `Logo.tsx`'s wordmark is filled shapes with real counters (the holes in "A"/"P"/"R"), so a literal stroke-draw would need per-path stroke lengths worked out across several paths for no real visual gain over a wipe. Same clip-path-transition technique already proven twice this session (`MobileNav`'s drawer reveal, the desktop mega menu's own open animation).
- **Shown once per browser session, and only on a genuine fresh entry to "/"** — never on a reload once already seen, and never when navigating here client-side from another page (owner requirement: "not going from one page to another"). Two independent gates: a `sessionStorage` flag (`cw-intro-seen`) for "already seen this session", and `lib/pageEntry.ts`'s `captureInitialPath`/`getInitialPath` for "was this page load's actual entry route the homepage" — the second one matters because Next.js App Router doesn't remount the root layout on client-side route changes, so a plain `sessionStorage`-only check would still wrongly fire the first time a visitor's *browsing session* happened to land on the homepage via an internal link, not just via a real navigation. `components/AppEntryMarker.tsx`, rendered once from `app/layout.tsx` on every route, is what actually captures the entry path — a module-level variable captures it exactly once per real page load (reset by a true reload, untouched by a client-side transition), which `IntroLoader` (homepage-only) reads back.
- **Hydration-safe**: both server and client's first render show the overlay (`visible` starts `true` in both places, so there's no mismatch); a `useLayoutEffect` corrects it synchronously before paint for every case that shouldn't actually show it, so ineligible/repeat visits never flash it.
- **A real bug found and fixed during verification**: React's Strict Mode (on by default in Next.js dev) double-invokes effects on mount. The very first version read+wrote the `sessionStorage` flag directly inside the effect body, so the phantom second Strict Mode invocation read back the value the first invocation had *just written*, always concluding "already seen" and cancelling the intro on every single fresh load. Fixed by memoizing the whole decision (read, write, and the boolean outcome) in a module-level `decideOnce()` function — computed exactly once regardless of how many times the surrounding effect runs.
- **Skippable**: a click anywhere on the overlay, or any keypress, jumps straight to the fade-out.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`**: never shown at all, marked as seen immediately.
- Locks background scroll while visible, same one-line pattern already used by `MobileNav`/`HeaderOverlayNav`.

### Header — Built
`components/Header.tsx` · recipe: `header`

Sticky site header on the ink surface: brand, nav, secondary link, primary CTA. A nav link with `megaMenu` becomes a mega-menu trigger; one without is a plain link. Nav data is passed in from `content/home.ts`, never hardcoded, so every page reuses it.

**`sticky` prop** (default `true`) — set `false` to render a plain, non-sticky header that scrolls away with the page (`header.baseStatic`: same colours/border as `header.base`, no `position`/transform), and skip the hide-on-scroll-down/reveal-on-scroll-up effect entirely. Added 2026-08-29 for a one-time trial on the Activewear PLP (owner: "let's try one time gymshark approach and see how it looks") — `gymshark.com`'s own header is non-sticky, which is why their sticky filter sidebar locks flush at the bare viewport top instead of docking below a persistent nav. **Reverted 2026-08-30** (owner: "apply the same nav top behavior as applied on the home. Always apply to every new page") — the PLP's own `Header` usage no longer passes `sticky={false}`, and `CategoryFilters`' own sticky `top`/`max-h` were reverted alongside it (back to `top-[111px]`/`max-h-[calc(100vh-135px)]`, clearing the persistent header again instead of locking flush at the bare viewport top).
>
> **Standing rule:** every new page uses the sitewide sticky, hide-on-scroll-down/reveal-on-scroll-up header — don't pass `sticky={false}` on a new page without an explicit owner request. The prop itself stays (documented above) for a future one-off trial, but it's opt-in per page, not a pattern to default into.

**Nav CTA size, vs. banner CTAs (owner question, 2026-09-02: "what's the cta size in the nav, is it the same as other on banner?")** — height is identical (54px, the shared `Button` base `min-h-[54px]`) at both; font size differs (nav CTA is 16px via `header.actionButton`'s `text-button-sm`, banner CTAs use the base 18px `text-button`). **A real bug found while checking**: `header.actionButton`'s own `px-6` (24px) side-padding override was losing to `Button`'s base `px-8` (32px) in Tailwind's generated stylesheet order — same specificity, same property, no `!` — so the nav CTA rendered with the SAME 32px padding as a banner button despite the recipe's own stated intent. Fixed with `!px-6` (same override pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase for this exact class of same-specificity conflict); confirmed live afterward (`paddingLeft`/`paddingRight: 24px`).

**A floating frosted-glass "pill" nav variant (Figma node `670:355`) was tried and reverted the same day (2026-09-02)** — owner: "I have tried a new nav bar which has a frost effect ... Let's try it for desktop first", then "Does not like it revert back" after seeing it live on the homepage. Built as an opt-in `variant="frost"` prop with dedicated `header.frost*` recipe tokens; fully removed rather than left disabled/dead in the codebase. Worth knowing before re-proposing something similar: getting it right required fixing three real bugs (a `px-8`/`px-[8px]` Tailwind-token mixup that overflowed the pill at 1440px, the mega-menu chevron pushing the row 36px past the required 1280px minimum, and the transparent header exposing `Footer`'s own `sticky bottom-0 z-0` "reveal" text through the top of the page until the header switched from `sticky` to `fixed`) — a real design/engineering cost even before the owner's own visual call.

**Desktop-only logo correction (owner, 2026-09-06: "update the caprio logo in the nav bar, not in the footer. Only for desktop")** — the nav bar's logo (mobile and desktop alike) had been rendering `Logo`'s default, approximated geometry (WEAR beside CAPRIO), not Figma's real one (WEAR below CAPRIO, taller and narrower — see Logo's own entry above for the full geometry comparison). Fixed for the desktop mark only, via the new `desktopLogo` prop (`<Logo stacked className={header.brandLogoDesktop} />`), rendered as a sibling of `logo` inside the same brand `<Link>`, each self-hiding via its own recipe class (`brandLogo` gained `xl:hidden`; `brandLogoDesktop` is `hidden xl:block`) — the same "two parallel elements, each hides itself by breakpoint" pattern already used elsewhere on this site (e.g. `Faq`'s `desktopOuter`/`mobileSection`), so no JS/conditional rendering needed. All 8 pages that render the real `Header` were updated to pass both props (styleguide's exploratory `HeaderOverlayNav` demo was left on the old geometry — it isn't the real header).

**Mobile follow-up (same day, owner: "Update the mobile nav caprio logo: Use the same logo size, not in the footer only header")** — the mobile bar/drawer mark (`logo`, via `header.brandLogo`) was deliberately left on the old, unstacked geometry by the fix above; this closes that gap. `header.brandLogo` now passes `stacked` too (`<Logo stacked className={header.brandLogo} />`, same 8 page call sites plus both styleguide demos) and its own height moved from `h-5` (20px) to `h-[28px]`, matched to `footer.mobileBrandLogo`'s already-established 28px value. The old unstacked geometry is ~9.5:1 wide, so a naive height bump on it would have overlapped the "Menu" pill (confirmed live pre-fix: only ~22px of slack at 375px); the stacked geometry is ~3.7:1 wide, so 28px tall renders narrower despite the taller height, leaving clear room. `MobileNav`'s drawer needed no change — it renders whatever node `Header` passes as `logo`, so it picked up the new mark automatically. (Note: a separate, later same-day request also brought Footer's own *desktop* mark onto `stacked` — Figma node `587:5755` — so "Footer untouched" only ever applied to Footer's mobile mark; see Logo's own entry above for the current, accurate split.) PLP/PDP/styleguide spot-checked; `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests) green. A separate, pre-existing 1280px-width overflow was found on the leggings PDP during the desktop-only check above, confirmed unrelated to either logo fix — flagged as a separate task, not fixed here.

**Geometry re-measured again (owner, 2026-09-06: "updated the desktop nav logo, update it again", same Figma link)** — re-fetching node `680:394` found its own frame had been nudged smaller in Figma since the first pull (`141x38` → `137.29x37`, same WEAR-below-CAPRIO shape). `header.brandLogoDesktop`'s height corrected `h-[38px]` → `h-[37px]` (renders ~137px wide at native size, still narrower than `brandLogo`'s own ~190px-wide default-geometry render and still shorter than the desktop nav's 48px-tall trigger pills, so the "no overflow, no row-height change" reasoning from the first pass still holds); `header.brandLogo` stays at its own fixed `h-[28px]` (a deliberately fitted size, not native, so it didn't need a numeric change — only the underlying path data did). `Logo.tsx`'s `stacked` branch and every consumer (`Header` mobile+desktop, `Footer` desktop) picked up the corrected paths automatically, being one shared component. Confirmed live: desktop measures `137.29x37` at `x:80`; mobile measures the same `137.29x37` viewBox at 28px tall; Footer desktop measures the same viewBox at its own fixed 55px; Footer mobile and `IntroLoader` unchanged (still the default, unstacked geometry). `npx tsc --noEmit` surfaced one pre-existing, unrelated error (`components/HeaderOverlayNav.tsx`'s own stale `header.brandNavGroup` reference, from an untouched file — confirmed via `git diff`) — flagged as a separate task, not fixed here; `npx eslint .` full-repo clean.

**Real bug fixed (owner report, 2026-08-30, "fix the nav" — turned out not to be `CategoryBanner` at all): `baseStatic` gained `relative z-40`** (was plain `position: static`, no z-index). A `position: static` element paints in an earlier CSS stacking bucket than any positioned one, *regardless of z-index* — so on this page only, the header was painting BEHIND `Footer`'s own `sticky bottom-0 z-0` reveal trick (`components/sections/Footer.tsx`), which computes as pinned near the viewport top at scroll position 0 on every page (confirmed live: `getBoundingClientRect` puts Footer at `top: 0` on both this page and the homepage). The homepage's own header (`base`, `sticky z-40`) was never affected — a positioned element with a positive z-index always paints after Footer's z-0 one — only this page's plain-`static` variant was exposed, showing Footer's own content bleeding through the top ~87px of the page (cut-off logo, a stray white band) at certain viewport heights. `relative` (not `sticky`) keeps this variant's own scroll-away layout completely unchanged; it only opts the element into normal stacking-context ordering so its own `z-40` (matching `base`'s value, same reason) can do its job.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `brand` | Always required, even with `logo` set — it becomes the home link's accessible name |
| `logo` | The real mark, e.g. `<Logo stacked />`. Replaces the text brand block entirely when given; `brandParent` goes unused. Rendered below `xl:` (mobile bar/drawer, `header.brandLogo`, `h-[28px]`) — see `desktopLogo` |
| `desktopLogo` | The `xl:`+ mark, e.g. `<Logo stacked className={header.brandLogoDesktop} />` — added 2026-09-06 for the nav-bar-only logo correction (see Logo's own entry above). Falls back to `logo` if omitted, but `logo`'s own recipe is `xl:hidden`, so a caller that only passes `logo` shows nothing at `xl:`+; every current page passes both |
| `brandParent` | Text-only fallback subline, e.g. "by Caprio Sports". Not shown when `logo` is set |
| `links`, `cta`, `secondaryCta` | Desktop nav content, from `content/home.ts` |
| `mobileLinks`, `contact` | The mobile drawer's own content — see MobileNav below |
| `social` | `content/site.ts`'s `ORGANIZATION.sameAs`, forwarded to the mobile drawer's social row |

**Real usage** (Figma node 316:1331, homepage): the CAPRIO WEAR logo, no text brand, four plain links (Activewear, Teamwear & Uniforms, Services, Our Factory) — resolved 2026-08-22, see open question 6 in [04-product.md](04-product.md). Nav link type is Figma's own measured style, **Body** weight (400, not uppercase) at **16px** (`navLink`/`navTrigger`'s own `text-[1rem]`, owner correction 2026-09-02: "make the activewear, teamwear etc 16px" — was 18px/`text-body`, the shared body-copy token, kept untouched since it's used well beyond nav links).

**Desktop nav appears at `xl` (1280px)**, Tailwind's standard breakpoint — not a custom value. The real nav's intrinsic width (logo, 4 short links, two actions) is roughly 1117px, which fits comfortably inside `xl` with room to spare. (An earlier pass, before the real logo and real nav existed, needed a bespoke 1420px breakpoint because the wireframe-era placeholder nav — six links, a mega menu, bold caption-style type — was considerably wider; that need went away once the real content replaced it.) All four required desktop viewports (1280, 1366, 1440, 1920) now show the full desktop nav; the drawer covers every mobile and tablet size.

**Mega menu, corrected on first real use** (Figma node 493:3140, 2026-08-27) — the earlier `w-80 rounded-lg` dropdown card (a flat link list with an "All <section>" lead link) was a pre-real-design guess and never used by real content; the real design is a full-width panel dropping below the header with 5 category columns. Only Activewear currently has real category content (`content/home.ts`'s `activewearMegaMenu`, shared verbatim with the mobile drawer's own mega menu built the same day) — Teamwear & Uniforms keeps navigating as a plain link until its own category breakdown exists; wiring Activewear's categories under a different label would be a content error, not a faithful build.
- **A single panel, not one per trigger**: rendered once directly inside `<header>` (not nested in each trigger's own `<li>`), showing whichever link's `megaMenu` matches `openMenu` — required for a true full-bleed panel, since an element positioned relative to one `<li>` can never paint past that `<li>`'s own inline position. The trigger row and the panel share one `onMouseLeave`/`onBlur` zone (a wrapping div around both), so moving the pointer from a trigger down into the panel no longer closes it — only leaving both together does.
- **Active trigger**: `font-semibold` plus a 2px white underline **8px below** the trigger text (`navTriggerActive`/`navUnderline`, `-bottom-2`) — this request's own explicit spec (reverses the 2026-08-27 call below, which had instead pushed the line flush with the header's own bottom border; that read as detached from the selected word, owner correction 2026-09-07). Not a literal copy of Figma's own underline placement (which sits oddly far below the trigger, near the header's own outer bottom edge) — that reads as a byproduct of how the Figma frame was assembled on a separate, taller mockup canvas, not a deliberate typographic choice.
- **Promo strip removed** (owner, 2026-09-07: "let's remove this" — the "Fully Custom Manufacturing" heading, description and three sparkle-bulleted stats that used to sit under every mega menu's categories). `megaPromo`/`megaPromoHeading`/`megaPromoRow`/`megaPromoText`/`megaPromoBullet`/`megaPromoIcon` deleted from `components/ui/styles.ts`, the `megaMenuPromo` prop removed from `Header` entirely (and every one of its 9 call sites), and `home.nav.megaMenuPromo` deleted from `content/home.ts`. Its divider ("a line where the menu ends," added 2026-08-27) went with it, so the panel is un-bordered below the category columns now. The category columns are the last thing in the panel; `megaPanelInner`'s own `pb-10` (40px) is now the real, and only, gap to the panel's bottom edge (also the owner's explicit spec, same request: "finish the mega menu 40px from the bottom of the content").
- **Group labels** (TOPS, BOTTOMS, etc.) are `font-medium` (owner call, 2026-08-27), overriding `text-button-sm`'s own bold default for this one instance — the same "override weight on one usage, not the shared token" pattern `Marquee`'s `labelVariant` already established.
- **Panel border**: only the top edge (`megaPanel`'s `border-t`).
- Still opens on hover for pointer users and on click/Enter for keyboard users, closes on Escape, and closes when focus or the pointer leaves the group (now the whole header+panel group, not one `<li>`). Exercised in `/styleguide` via `megaMenuDemoLinks`, updated to the new grouped `megaMenu` shape.
- **Teamwear & Uniforms got its own real mega menu the same day** (Figma node `485:2992`) — two groups (UNIFORMS, 8 items; OTHERS, 2 items), reusing the exact same panel/animation the mechanism above already built for Activewear, no new UI. `content/home.ts`'s `teamwearMegaMenu` is a separate array from `activewearMegaMenu` (genuinely different categories), fed to both `nav.links[1].megaMenu` (desktop) and `mobileLinks[1].megaMenu` (mobile drawer) the same way Activewear's is.

**Mobile drawer trigger is a bordered pill, not a plain icon button** (corrected against real Figma, node 465:2862/465:2871 in the "Caprio Website" file, 2026-08-27) — a `1.5px border-current` pill containing the real exported glyph (`components/icons/MenuIcon.tsx`, three bars, the outer two shorter than the middle one — not lucide's equal-width `Menu`, which doesn't match) plus a visible "Menu" label (`text-button-sm`, 16px/700). The visible label is now the button's accessible name, so the earlier `aria-label="Open menu"` was dropped as redundant. Mobile only (`xl:hidden`); desktop is untouched.

**Mobile header total height is a fixed 72px** (owner call, 2026-08-27, revised up from an initial 62px same day) — the new pill (51px, its own padding taken verbatim from Figma) is taller than the old icon-only button (44px), so `header.inner`'s existing `py-4` (correct for desktop, unrelated to this change) started overshooting on mobile once the pill replaced it. Split to `max-xl:py-2.5 xl:py-4` so only mobile's total height was corrected (72px exactly, live-measured); desktop's own 87px is untouched.

**Hides on scroll down, reveals on scroll up** (owner request, 2026-08-26 — "you decide" on the exact threshold): a `scroll` listener (rAF-throttled) compares the current `window.scrollY` against the last sample to get direction, and translates the header fully off-screen (`header.hidden`, `-translate-y-full`, `transition-transform duration-300`) once the page has scrolled down past the header's own *measured* height (`headerRef.current.offsetHeight`, not a hardcoded pixel guess — stays correct at any breakpoint's real header height). Any upward scroll reveals it immediately, at any scroll position. Never hides while a mega menu or the mobile drawer is open — both force `navHidden` back to `false` on every scroll sample, so an open menu can't be yanked off-screen mid-interaction. The header keeps its `sticky top-0` positioning throughout; only its rendered offset moves (`translate`, not `display`/`visibility`), so the transition has something to animate and the header's own layout box never disappears from the flow. Verified via a real Playwright session (not this project's own synthetic-event-limited browser tool — see `DesktopChevronScroller`'s entry for that limitation): header's `boundingBox()` moves from `y:0` to `y:-87` (its own height) scrolling down past that threshold, and back to `y:0` on any upward scroll; stays at `y:0` for a small scroll (40px) well under the threshold.

### Breadcrumb — Built
`components/Breadcrumb.tsx` · recipe: `breadcrumb` · icon: `components/icons/BreadcrumbArrowIcon.tsx`

`Home › Activewear › Leggings` (Figma node `406:3078`, 2026-08-28) — a foundational component built ahead of the inner pages that will use it, category/product pages aren't built yet. Not wired into the homepage, whose own breadcrumb is a single "Home" crumb with no arrows to show.

Props: `items: { label: string; href: string }[]`, `tone?: "light" | "dark"` (default `"light"`), `className?: string` (added 2026-08-31, merged onto the `<nav>` — a call site's own spacing, or a CSS-only visibility override; never a markup change, the trail itself always renders). Same `items` shape `lib/schema.ts`'s `breadcrumbSchema()` already takes — this component is that data's **visible** counterpart, not a separate model; a page that renders a real, multi-level breadcrumb should feed the same array to both.

- `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">` around an `<ol>` — standard breadcrumb semantics.
- Every item but the last renders as a real `next/link` (`breadcrumb.link`, `#727272`, hover to `text-ink`, same "hover to full contrast" pattern as `megaItem`). The last item is a plain `<span aria-current="page">` (`breadcrumb.current`, `font-semibold text-[#21272a]`). **Corrected on first real `tone="light"` use** (PDP breadcrumb, Figma node `634:4952`, 2026-08-31) — the original `#838d97`/`font-medium text-ink` was built ahead of any light-background design to check it against (only `tone="dark"`, inside `CategoryBanner`, had shipped before this); these are Figma's own real values, same "corrected on first real use" pattern used throughout this project.
- `tone="dark"` swaps in `linkDark` (`hover:text-paper`) and `currentDark` (`#abb5c0`, Figma's own real value for a dark-background current item) — `tone="light"`'s `text-ink`/`hover:text-ink` are both near-black, invisible against an ink surface.
- Arrow separator between every pair, `aria-hidden` (decorative; `<ol>`/`<li>` order already conveys sequence to assistive tech) — `BreadcrumbArrowIcon`, the real exported Figma asset's own vector path inlined as `currentColor`, same pattern as `ChevronArrowIcon.tsx`/`MenuIcon.tsx`.
- Type (`breadcrumb.link`/`.current`: `text-[1rem] leading-[18px]`) has no match in the existing scale — written as a literal Tailwind value straight from Figma, same precedent as `megaPromoHeading` and other one-off promo text, per the project's documented typography exception.

## Activewear PDP (product detail page)

New route, `app/activewear/[category]/[product]/page.tsx` (2026-08-31), built the same "one template, all copy from data" way as the PLP: `generateStaticParams` flat-maps every category's own `styleCards`, no product copy typed into the page itself. Reuses `Header` exactly as every other page does (default `sticky={true}`, none of the PLP's own non-sticky trial).

Built section by section, breadcrumb first (owner: "Let's start with the Breadcrum, use the same Nav as goloabal"): `<Breadcrumb items={...} className="hidden md:block" />` — hidden below `768px` via CSS only (`display: none`, never conditionally unmounted), with `breadcrumbSchema(...)` rendered as a sibling `<JsonLd>` built from the same `breadcrumbItems` array, so the visible strip's mobile hide can never touch the schema (confirmed live at 390/768/1440, not just assumed). A minimal placeholder body — the rest of the PDP isn't designed yet — adds one explicit, always-crawlable link back to the parent PLP ("Back to all Custom Leggings") so hiding the breadcrumb never removes the page's only upward link; this is a stand-in for the eventual real Related Styles section, not the real design.

New `StyleCard.pdpTitle` (optional, `content/activewear/types.ts`) — the PDP's own product name where it genuinely differs from the grid's shorter `cardTitle` (Figma: "High-Waisted Compression" vs. the card's own "High Waist Leggings"). Falls back to `cardTitle` wherever read, so cards without a real PDP frame yet need no placeholder text.

### ProductGallery — Built
`components/sections/ProductGallery.tsx` · recipe: `productGallery` (Figma node `634:4961` desktop / `638:860` mobile, 2026-08-31)

The PDP's hero image gallery. Desktop: a fixed-height scrollable thumbnail rail (5 visible, chevron-driven "show more"/"show less") beside a large main image with its own prev/next chevrons; hover/focus/click on a thumbnail swaps the main image. Mobile: one main image with a swipeable thumbnail strip pinned over its bottom edge. Both breakpoints share one `activeIndex` state.

**Main-image swap mechanism replaced (owner report, 2026-09-07: "blinky, jerky" — "on PDP, sliding the image with thumb or otherwise, the animation is not smooth").** The original build used `key={activeIndex}` on a single `MediaPlaceholder` plus a CSS fade-in keyframe (`gallery-fade-in`, `app/globals.css` — since removed) — every change unmounted the whole image and mounted a fresh one, which blinked (the old node vanishes and the new one starts at `opacity:0` before the fade visibly starts) on every trigger; worse, mobile swipe was a bare `touchstart`/`touchend` threshold check with **no `touchmove` handling at all**, so the image gave zero visual feedback while the finger dragged and only jump-cut on release.

Replaced with the same native scroll-snap pattern `CardCarousel.tsx` already established for finger-swipeable content elsewhere on this site (Our Services, How It Works, Inside the Factory) — real browser-native touch scrolling, not hand-rolled drag tracking:
- Two permanently-mounted horizontal tracks (`mainTrack`/`mainSlide` desktop, `mobileTrack`/`mobileSlide` mobile — `overflow-x-auto snap-x snap-mandatory`, `no-scrollbar`), each holding one full-width slide per image, no `key` remounting.
- `goTo(index)` (thumbnail click/hover, chevron `stepMainImage`) imperatively `scrollTo`s both tracks to the target slide; a direct swipe is detected the other way, via a `scroll` listener (rAF-throttled, same pattern as `CardCarousel`'s own) computing the nearest slide from `scrollLeft`/`clientWidth`.
- `stepMainImage`'s wrap case (last → first, first → last) scrolls instantly (`behavior: "auto"`), not smoothly — a smooth scroll across every intermediate slide on wrap would itself look broken for a gallery with more than a couple of images; every other step still slides.
- A `matchMedia("(min-width: 1280px)")` `change` listener re-syncs both tracks (instant) whenever the viewport crosses this component's own `xl` breakpoint — needed because `goTo` multiplies by a track's own `clientWidth`, which is `0` while that breakpoint's track is `display:none`, so the *hidden* track's target position silently zeroes out on every interaction until it's re-synced the moment it becomes visible. Tried `window`'s own `resize` event and a `ResizeObserver` on the tracks first — both are more indirect ways to ask the same question and both failed to fire reliably in live testing; `matchMedia` on the exact query this component cares about is the direct, correct signal.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint .` clean. Thumbnail click/hover, chevron next/prev (including both wrap cases), and `activeIndex`/counter/active-border sync all confirmed correct via live DOM inspection. Real finger-drag smoothness confirmed via a one-off Playwright script dispatching genuine CDP touch events (not reproducible through this session's own Browser-pane tool, which throttles animated `scrollTo` while its panel isn't actively rendered) — `scrollLeft` climbed proportionally with the simulated drag position frame-by-frame, then the native snap correctly completed the gesture to the next slide on release, with `activeIndex`/the counter syncing correctly. `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` unaffected (static screenshots don't exercise the animation; layout/box sizing confirmed unchanged).

### ProductInfo — Built
`components/sections/ProductInfo.tsx` · recipe: `productInfo`

The PDP's product info text block (Figma node `634:4988` desktop / `638:2541` mobile, 2026-08-31): a SKU row, then the real on-page H1 + a description paragraph. Owner: "This text part comes next to the product images, use the same spacing." Identical copy, type sizes (16px semibold SKU, 24px/30px-line-height semibold H1, 18px/28px-line-height regular description), and internal spacing (20px SKU→content gap, 8px H1→description gap) at both breakpoints — Tailwind's own `text-2xl`/`leading-[30px]` and `text-lg`/`leading-7` happen to match Figma's literal values exactly. The only real difference between the two Figma frames is the mobile one's own 20px page inset, supplied by the caller's plain `container-p` wrapper (its own 20px side padding matches exactly), not a class inside this component.

Props: `sku?: string`, `heading: string`, `description: string`. When `sku` is unset, the whole SKU row is omitted rather than rendering an empty placeholder.

Three new optional `StyleCard` fields feed it (`content/activewear/types.ts`), same "only the first real Figma frame gets real copy, others fall back" pattern as `pdpTitle`: `sku`, `pdpHeading` (the real H1 — different, longer copy than `pdpTitle`, which is only the breadcrumb's own short trailing crumb), `pdpDescription` (falls back to `cardSubline`). Set for `high-waisted-compression` only (renamed from `high-waist-leggings`, see the PDP route's own entry below). Replaces the page's earlier placeholder `<h1>` — still exactly one `<h1>` on the page.

**A real bug caught before shipping**: an initial pass tried `xl:container-p` to scope the wrapper's padding to desktop only. `container-p` is a hand-written CSS class (`app/globals.css`), not a Tailwind utility — Tailwind cannot generate a responsive variant of a class it doesn't recognize as its own. Fixed by applying plain `container-p` unconditionally (correct at both breakpoints) and putting only the real top-padding difference (`pt-5`/`xl:pt-16`) on actual Tailwind utilities alongside it.

Sits beside `ProductGallery` (`StyleCard.images`, Figma node `634:4961`/`638:860`) in a row on the page — gallery above on mobile, beside it on desktop, same "no combined two-column frame exists yet to measure the real split against" reasoning as `ProductGallery`'s own entry.

**Tablet width (768–1279px), owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet treatment to the PDP — reverted 2026-09-07.** The 2026-09-04 pass moved the row from `flex flex-col xl:flex-row xl:items-start xl:gap-[66px]` to switching at `md:`, with `ProductGallery`'s own `desktopRoot` compressed to a judgment-call `md:w-[320px]` (no tablet Figma frame exists for this page) so the fixed `700px` gallery + `66px` gap + fixed `514px` text column, which totals 1280px, would fit a 768–1279px container. That squeeze left only ~195px for the actual main product image (the 109px rail + its own 16px gap ate most of the 320px) — the owner reported this live, 2026-09-07: "the PDP top section, above the specifications should follow the mobile layout, not desktop as the main product image size is very small." Reverted, back to the original single `xl:` (1280px) tier, both in the page files (`app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`, mirrored in `app/teamwear/[sport]/[style]/page.tsx`) and `productGallery`'s own recipe:
- The row is back to `xl:flex-row xl:items-start xl:gap-[66px]` only — stacked (mobile layout) through the full 768–1279px tablet range, not just real phone widths.
- `productGallery.desktopRoot`/`mobileRoot` moved `md:`→`xl:` gating back, dropping the tablet-specific `320px` cap entirely — the desktop column (`700px` sticky rail + main image) only renders from 1280px up again; `mobileRoot`'s full-width stacked image (fixed `h-[450px]`) now covers all of 768–1279px too.
- The text column drops its now-redundant `md:min-w-0 md:flex-1` pair (nothing left to override below `xl` once the fixed `xl:w-[514px] xl:flex-none` is the only sized tier) — it's `w-full` at every width under `xl`, same as the mobile layout always was.
- `productCtas.desktopRow`/`mobileBar` moved `md:`→`xl:` gating back in the same pass, so the CTA row switches in step with the gallery instead of a breakpoint earlier on its own (see that component's own entry below). `productCtas.mobileButton`'s own `md:` tablet-width button-cap fix was deliberately left unchanged — see its own comment for why it's now newly relevant across the wider range `mobileBar` covers, not superseded by the revert.
- `ProductInfo.heading`'s own `md:text-2xl` size bump was left as-is — pure typography, not part of the layout squeeze the owner flagged, and larger heading text at tablet width remains desirable regardless of which gallery layout is showing.
- `ProductRelatedStyles`' two page-level instances (desktop position inside this text column, mobile/tablet position after `TrustPoints`) were separately corrected the same day (owner, on that section's own spacing: "make it 72px only on both mobile and tablet") from `md:flex`/`md:hidden` back to `xl:flex`/`xl:hidden`, restoring the coupling to this same column split — see that entry's own note below for the full reasoning; mirrored here to the teamwear PDP template, which hadn't been updated yet.

Confirmed live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression`: full-width stacked mobile layout (image + thumbnail strip, sticky CTA bar) renders unchanged at 768px, 1024px, and 1279px; the desktop side-by-side layout (700px gallery, 514px text column, inline CTA row) only appears from 1280px; `xl:` desktop (1440px) and real mobile (375px) both pixel-identical to before. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, and the full 30-viewport screenshot suite all clean.

**Breadcrumb-to-image gap corrected at tablet width, same day (owner, 2026-09-07: "breadcrumbs on the tablet pdp should have the same gap to the image as it has from the top")** — `Breadcrumb`'s own recipe (`breadcrumb.nav`, `"container-p pb-2 pt-8"`) is unconditional, so it already gave a flat 32px gap above the breadcrumb (header → breadcrumb) at every width the breadcrumb is visible (`hidden md:block`). But the gallery/info row's own top padding, `pt-0` with only `xl:pt-6` overriding it, meant the gap below the breadcrumb (breadcrumb → image) was just `pb-2`'s 8px at tablet width — a real, visible 32px/8px mismatch — while desktop already matched (8px + `xl:pt-6`'s 24px = 32px). Added `md:pt-6` to the row wrapper (both PDP page files, alongside the tablet-width revert above), giving 8px + 24px = 32px below the breadcrumb at tablet width too, matching the 32px above it; `xl:pt-6` (now redundant with `md:pt-6` but kept, matching this project's existing pattern of layering breakpoint values) and real mobile (breadcrumb still hidden, `pt-0` still zero) are both unaffected. Confirmed live: 32px/32px at 768px, 1024px, and 1279px; unchanged 32px/32px at 1440px `xl:`; unchanged 0px gap (breadcrumb hidden) at real mobile (375px).

**`ProductGallery` alt-wiring fix (SEO audit, 2026-09-02)**: its own `ProductGalleryImage` type used to drop `src` entirely (alt-only), so even once real gallery photography lands with a real `src`, no `<Image>`/`alt` attribute would ever have rendered — only the captionless placeholder box, every time. Fixed: `src?: string` added to the type, and all 4 `MediaPlaceholder` call sites (desktop rail, desktop main, mobile main, mobile strip) now pass `image={{ src, alt }}` whenever `src` is set. No visual change today — every `images[].src` sitewide is still empty, confirmed via a live screenshot pixel-matching the pre-fix render.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`.

Two groups today ("Fabric options", "Customization"), but the component is generic over `groups: {heading, items}[]` rather than two hard-coded props, so a future third group is a data change, not a component change.

**Pills reflow via plain `flex flex-wrap`, not a hand-coded per-row grouping**: Figma's own "rows" (2×2 for the 4 fabric pills, 3+1 for the 4 customization pills at 456px; a different, narrower break at mobile's ~263-271px column) are just where a flex-wrap row happens to break at each frame's own width, not a fixed layout — letting the real pill text reflow naturally reproduces both screenshots without hardcoding either one, and adapts automatically if a category's own `fabricPills` count ever changes.

**Content, two different scopes**: "Fabric options" is category-level (`Category.fabricPills`, new field, `content/activewear/leggings.ts`) — a short PDP-facing pill label per fabric, distinct from `fabricOptions[].fabric`'s own full descriptive string on the PLP (e.g. "Nylon or polyamide + elastane (70 to 85% / 15 to 30%)" → "Nylon spandex"), not derived from it at render time since the two don't share a mechanical transformation. "Customization" is shared across every PDP regardless of category (`pdpCustomizationPills`, `content/activewear/pdpShared.ts`) — generic manufacturing-capability tags, same "one shared place" rule as `pdpFaqOperational`/`pdpSpecHighlights`.
