### ProductCtas / ProductCtasMobileBar — Built
`components/sections/ProductCtas.tsx` · recipe: `productCtas`

The PDP's request/download CTAs (Figma node `634:5065` desktop / `638:1645` mobile, 2026-09-01) — two genuinely different treatments per breakpoint, not one layout reflowing, now two separate components rendered at two different places in the page:

- **`ProductCtas` (desktop)**: an inline primary+secondary `Button` pair (`components/Button.tsx`, reused verbatim — the same pill already used for `Header`'s own `cta`/`secondaryCta`) in normal flow, fourth child in the PDP's shared text column. Owner spec: "the top space should be 32px" — that column's own existing `gap-8` already supplies it (confirmed live: exactly 32px from the last "Customization" pill to this row). `hidden xl:flex` — desktop only.
- **`ProductCtasMobileBar` (mobile)**: the secondary button drops entirely; the primary button becomes a bottom bar, "always visible" (owner spec) while scrolling the page's own content. Rendered separately, as the literal last child of `<main>` (`app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`), not alongside `ProductCtas` in the text column.

**Real bug fixed (owner report, 2026-09-02): "the fixed cta, overlapping the social icons on the footer, it should not do that."** The bar originally used `position: fixed` (pinned to the viewport, `z-30`, explicitly documented as outranking both `<main>`'s own `z-10` and `Footer`'s own `sticky bottom-0 z-0`) — but since it was a *descendant* of `<main>`, and `<main>`'s own higher stacking already always outranks `Footer`'s reveal trick (`Footer.tsx`'s own comment) regardless of the bar's own internal z-index, the bar kept floating on top of Footer forever, including over the social icons, once scrolled that far.

**Fix**: the bar is now `position: sticky bottom-0` (`productCtas.mobileBar`), rendered as `<main>`'s literal last child. This mirrors `Footer`'s own sticky-reveal trick, but in reverse: placed at the very end of `<main>`, it sticks to the viewport bottom throughout the page's own scroll range (identical "always visible" behavior to before), then releases itself the instant `<main>`'s own box ends — exactly where Footer begins — so the two can never overlap, no z-index games needed. Being `sticky` (not `fixed`) also means it now occupies its own real ~60px of space in normal flow, so the old `bg-ink` spacer div that used to reserve room for the out-of-flow fixed bar is gone — the bar's own box does that job itself.

`w-full` inside the bar's own `px-5`, not Figma's literal `320px` (that's just `360 − 20×2` at one specific frame width, not a deliberate fixed button width).

**Desktop row moved `md:`→`xl:` and back (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet treatment to PLP/PDP; reverted 2026-09-07 alongside `ProductGallery`'s own revert — see that component's own entry above)** — `productCtas.desktopRow`/`mobileBar` moved `xl:flex`/`xl:hidden` → `md:flex`/`md:hidden` on 2026-09-04, then back to `xl:flex`/`xl:hidden` on 2026-09-07 so this row switches in step with the gallery beside it (the owner's complaint was specifically that the tablet-width side-by-side layout squeezed the main product image too small; this row has no fixed-width elements of its own and so needed no independent fix, just to stay paired with the gallery's threshold rather than switch a breakpoint earlier on its own). `mobileButton`'s own `md:` tablet-width button-cap (below) was deliberately left unchanged — it's now newly relevant across the wider 768–1279px range `mobileBar` covers again, not superseded by this revert. Confirmed live: sticky mobile CTA bar shows through 1279px; desktop inline button pair only from 1280px.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 375px: bar stays pinned to the viewport bottom at scrollY 2000 (mid-page), and at full-page scroll the bar has released above the footer's social icons with zero overlap (confirmed via bounding boxes and a screenshot).

**Also slides away automatically once the CTA section is reached (owner follow-up, same day: "when it gets to the cta section, remove the fixed cta automatically")** — FinalCta already carries its own big CTA button, so this bar is redundant clutter once that section is on screen, and the sticky/DOM-position release above only happens much later, at the Footer boundary. `ProductCtasMobileBar` is now a client component: it watches an invisible marker (`FINAL_CTA_MARKER_ID`, rendered immediately before `<FinalCta>`) via `IntersectionObserver`, and slides itself out (`translate-y-full`, `productCtas.mobileBarHidden`) as that marker approaches. `translate-y-full`, not `hidden`/`display:none` — the bar's own 60px keeps reserving its space in flow for the length of the slide animation, so nothing below it jumps; a `collapsed` state (`productCtas.mobileBarCollapsed`) then removes that reserved space ~300ms later, once the bar has actually finished sliding off-screen, so it doesn't leave a second unwanted gap sitting between FinalCta and Footer forever after. Note: Tailwind v4's `translate-y-*` utilities set the CSS `translate` property, not `transform` — check `getComputedStyle(el).translate`, not `.transform`, when verifying this live.

**Two real bugs in getting the hide condition right (found live, same day)**: (1) keying off `entry.isIntersecting` alone made the bar reappear once the marker scrolled back out of the viewport above (i.e. once the user was well past FinalCta, under the FAQ CTA) — backwards, since it should stay hidden for the rest of the page once reached; (2) a first fix comparing `entry.boundingClientRect.top < window.innerHeight` directly looked right but is wrong in practice — `IntersectionObserver` only invokes its callback at threshold crossings, so that raw `top` value is stale between crossings and doesn't get re-checked until the next one, far later, leaving the bar visible on screen the whole time in between. Fixed by keying off `entry.isIntersecting || entry.boundingClientRect.top < 0` — true from the moment the section is approached (accounting for the observer's own `rootMargin`), and stays true for every scroll position at or beyond that.

**Now used on both the PDP and the PLP** (owner, 2026-09-02: "it should be applied to both plp and pdp") — the PLP (`app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`) previously hand-duplicated the sticky-bar markup directly from the `productCtas` recipe rather than importing this component, so it had none of the auto-hide behavior; it now renders `<ProductCtasMobileBar primaryCta={home.nav.cta} />` verbatim (component reused, not reimplemented) plus its own `FINAL_CTA_MARKER_ID` marker before its own `<FinalCta>`.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`, `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`, `app/activewear/running-wear/page.tsx`.

**Button capped at tablet width, not stretched full-bleed (owner, 2026-09-03)**: `productCtas.mobileButton` was `w-full`, correct at real mobile width (matches the bar's own full-bleed `px-5` inset at any phone width) but a jarring stretched pill once the same bar rendered at 768–1279px tablet width. `w-full` now scoped to `max-md:`; from `md:` up the button caps at `md:w-auto md:min-w-[280px] md:mx-auto` — a normal CTA width, centered in the bar. The bar itself (`mobileBar`, full-bleed/sticky) is unchanged at any width — only the button inside it stopped growing to fill it.

### ProductRelatedStyles — Built
`components/sections/ProductRelatedStyles.tsx` · recipe: `productRelatedStyles`

The PDP's "Related styles" pill/tag row (Figma node `634:5070` desktop / `643:2660` mobile, "Browse More", 2026-09-01) — a border-top divider, then a heading and a wrapped row of pill tags, each with a trailing chevron. Owner: "This text part comes next to the product images, use the same spacing" / "32 px space from the top" — that 32px is this section's OWN top padding (border to heading), not the gap from the previous sibling (`ProductCtas`), which the shared text column's own `gap-8` already supplies (same "no extra pt of its own" pattern as `ProductHighlights`/`ProductOptions`/`ProductCtas`).

Not the same thing as the page's separate, plain "Related styles" block further down the page (sibling PDP links + a back-link to the parent PLP, satisfying SEO rule 6's crawl-loop requirement) — this is Figma-designed browsing-by-attribute copy (e.g. "Flare & Wide-Leg"), not a second list of the same sibling-PDP links.

Props: `heading?: string` (default `"Related styles"`), `tags: {label, href}[]`. Each tag carries its own real `href` (owner correction, 2026-09-01: "related styles should be linked and take user to the relevant page") — wherever this category's `styleCards` has a genuine matching sibling PDP the tag links straight to it (`Flare & Wide-Leg` → `flare-leggings`, `Capri & Cropped` → `capri-leggings`), a tag with no matching card yet (and the trailing "See All") links to the parent category PLP instead — always a real, working page, never an invented per-attribute filter URL.

**Hover states, added same day per owner spec**: text + chevron go `text-ink` (near-black) on hover — the chevron has no colour of its own, inheriting `currentColor` from the chip. Background tints to `#fafafb` on hover (a same-day `#fff6f3` trial was reverted back to this). The chevron also nudges `translate-x-0.5` (2px) on hover via `group-hover`, `chip` carrying the `group` class.

New `StyleCard.relatedStyleTags` field (`content/activewear/types.ts`), optional, same "only the first real Figma frame gets real copy" pattern as `sku`/`pdpHeading`.

**Renders twice on the PDP (owner correction, 2026-09-02)**: props gained a `className` override so the page can render two instances that hide themselves at opposite breakpoints — the original position (inside the gallery/info row's own text column) is now `hidden xl:block` (desktop only); a second instance right after `TrustPoints` is `block xl:hidden` (mobile only). The two breakpoints genuinely sit in different places in the page flow, not one node repositioned via CSS. (`xl:block` was later corrected to `xl:flex` the same day — see `topRule`'s own recipe comment, `productRelatedStyles.root` needs a flex container for its own `gap` to do anything.)

**Breakpoint moved `md:` → `xl:` and back again.** 2026-09-04 (tablet-width pass, see `ProductInfo`'s own entry above): both instances' visibility gates moved from `xl:flex`/`xl:hidden` to `md:flex`/`md:hidden`, coupling this section to the PDP's own tablet-width two-column layout switch, so the bordered in-column instance would appear as soon as that column split did (768px), not wait for `xl` (1280px). **Reversed 2026-09-07** (owner: "make it 72px only on both mobile and tablet") — back to `xl:flex`/`xl:hidden`, so the 72px/no-divider instance (`topRuleNone`, placed after `TrustPoints`) now covers the full mobile-through-tablet range, and the bordered `topRuleDefault` instance only shows at real desktop widths. The gallery/text column's own `md:` split (and everything else from the 2026-09-04 pass) is untouched — this was the one piece the owner wanted reversed. Confirmed live at 375px, 768px and 1440px: exactly one instance renders at each width, with the expected recipe (`pt-0 pb-[72px]` below `xl`, the bordered `py-8` at `xl`).

**`topRuleNone`'s own bottom padding also narrowed to 48px on mobile, same day** (owner, first: "make it 48px for mobile" on `TrustPoints`' own bottom gap above this section; then, separately: "make it 48px from bottom too" for this section's OWN bottom gap, the one nothing below it supplies) — `topRuleNone` was `pt-0 pb-12 md:pb-[72px]` (was a flat `pb-[72px]`), tablet kept at 72px since there was no Figma frame confirming any other value.

**Later the same day, gained a real top gap of its own at tablet width, and the 72px tablet-only bottom override was dropped**: a follow-up to the request above (owner: "for PDP tablet, follow 120px from top of each section, 0px from the bottom" for `ProductSpecifications`/`ProductCustomizeSteps`/`TrustPoints`) zeroed `TrustPoints`' own tablet bottom padding, which this instance had been passively relying on for its entire top gap (`pt-0` here always meant "borrow whatever the previous section's bottom happens to be") — without a fix, this instance would have sat flush against `TrustPoints` with a 0px gap at tablet width. Owner, once this was flagged: "add 48px top gap for related style and remove any other gaps it has it must be 48px from the top and bottom only." `topRuleNone` is now `pt-0 md:pt-12 pb-12` — `md:pt-12` (48px) gives this instance a real top gap of its own at tablet for the first time, and the old `md:pb-[72px]` tablet-only override is dropped entirely, since the unprefixed `pb-12` (48px) already covers tablet mobile-first once nothing overrides it — a clean, self-owned 48px top / 48px bottom at tablet, replacing the old asymmetric 48px-top (borrowed)/72px-bottom split. Real mobile is unaffected (`pt-0` + `TrustPoints`' own unchanged `pb-12` still nets 48px above; this instance's own `pb-12` unchanged below). Confirmed live at 768px: 48px above (now genuinely owned, not borrowed), 48px below.

Also gained a `topRule?: "default" | "none"` prop for the same reason: the desktop instance keeps `topRuleDefault` (border-top divider + 32px inset, its original treatment); the mobile instance passes `topRule="none"` (owner correction, 2026-09-02: "remove the top separator and space should be 72px from the top") — no divider, a plain 72px top gap instead, this project's standing mobile inter-section gap (see `productSpecifications.section`'s own comment for the same value).

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`.

### ProductCategoryLinks — Built
`components/sections/ProductCategoryLinks.tsx` · recipe: `productCategoryLinks`

SEO audit finding (2026-09-02, rule 6): despite this file and `docs/05-plan.md` previously describing a PDP "sibling styles + Back to all [Category]" crawl-loop block as already built, it didn't exist anywhere in `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx` — the PDP breadcrumb's own upward link is also `hidden md:block` (owner spec, 2026-08-31), so mobile visitors/crawlers had no visible link back to the parent category at all. Built for real this time.

Renders a real `<a href="/activewear/[category]">Back to all [Category]</a>`, plus (only when they exist) links to any other **published** sibling styles in the same category — `category.styleCards.filter(c => c.status === "published" && c.slug !== product.slug)`, computed straight from the existing registry, no new data. Draft siblings are excluded on purpose: no PDP route is generated for a draft slug, so linking to one would be a dead link. Plain, undesigned markup (no Figma frame for this block) — same lean-placeholder approach `ProductInfo`/`ProductGallery` used before their own frames landed.

Not the same as `ProductRelatedStyles` above (a Figma-designed browsing-by-attribute pill row) — this is a functional crawl-link list, always rendered, not gated on optional per-style tag data.

Rendered as `<main>`'s second-to-last child, right before `ProductCtasMobileBar`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at both 375px and 1440px: real `<a href="/activewear/leggings">` confirmed present and clickable (no sibling list rendered — only one published style exists sitewide today, so the "More Leggings" block correctly doesn't appear).

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`.

Heading/subline are shared PDP copy (`content/activewear/pdpShared.ts`'s `pdpSpecificationsCopy`).

**No exact Figma metadata for the heading/subline size**: this session's Figma Dev Mode MCP connection was unreachable (confirmed via repeated `get_screenshot`/`get_design_context` failures even after the user re-enabled it and reopened the file) — built from a pasted screenshot instead. Rather than guess a new pixel value, the heading reuses `sectionHeading.heading`'s own literal H2 scale (54px/500 desktop, 30px/460 mobile) and the subline reuses `trustPoints.subline`'s own literal values verbatim, both already-established tokens for "H2 + gray subline" pairs elsewhere on this same page.

Owner, reviewing PLP/PDP screenshots of this and two sibling sections (`TrustPoints`, `WhatWeCover`): "does not need an image placeholder... follow the website style" — tablet should follow desktop (no image) rather than showing a mobile-sized one. `xl:hidden` → `md:hidden`. Real mobile (`<md:`) unaffected.

**Placement**: node ID order places this between `ProductRelatedStyles` (`634:5070`, the pill row inside the text column) and `ProductCustomizeSteps` (`634:5153`) — inserted as a full-width sibling directly after the gallery+text-column row, before `ProductCustomizeSteps`. Only renders for a style that supplies `specifications` rows.

**Threshold moved xl:/max-xl: → md:/max-md: (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — `heading` reuses `sectionHeading.heading`'s own `text-h1` scale, whose fluid clamp already evaluates well above the 30px mobile value at 768px (confirmed on the homepage pass); `subline`/`value` are fixed literals, desktop always larger. Confirmed live: heading measures ~44.8px at 1024px (was 30px), unchanged at 375px/1440px.

**`section`'s own tablet top gap corrected to 120px (owner, 2026-09-07: "for PDP tablet, follow 120px from top of each section, 0px from the bottom" — for this section, `ProductCustomizeSteps`, and `TrustPoints` specifically)** — was `pt-[72px]` at tablet, only becoming `120px` at `xl:` (desktop); real mobile's 72px value came from this project's own standing mobile-only inter-section gap and was never meant to also cover tablet. `md:pt-[120px]` now cascades the existing desktop value down to tablet too (the redundant separate `xl:pt-[120px]` tier was dropped, not kept alongside an equal `md:` one) — real mobile (72px) and desktop (unchanged, still 120px) are both unaffected. Bottom was already 0 at tablet (only `xl:pb-[40px]` adds one, at desktop) — already matched the "0px from the bottom" half of the request, no change needed there.

`components/sections/ProductCustomizeSteps.tsx` · recipe: `productCustomizeSteps`

Owner: "already built the same component on homepage" — this is a near-literal copy of the homepage's `HowItWorks.tsx` structure: `CapabilityCard` rows + `useDesktopChevronScroller`/`DesktopChevron` for the desktop cursor-tracking carousel, `CardCarousel` for the mobile swipeable one, not a new carousel implementation. Inserted as a full-width sibling directly after the gallery+text-column row, before `TrustPoints`.

The chevron's fixed `CARD_WIDTH + CARD_GAP` pitch needed no change. Confirmed live: 32px left inset, no overflow, card measuring 469px wide at 1024px; unchanged at 1440px `xl:` and 375px real mobile.

Scoped to exactly one style, not sitewide, until confirmed: `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx` passes `align="left"` only when `data.product.slug === "high-waisted-compression"`, every other PDP gets `"center"` (the prop's own default) unchanged. Mobile is untouched either way — the trial is desktop-only, not part of the owner's request. Confirmed live: eyebrow/heading both left-aligned (flush at the container's own 80px inset) on the leggings PDP at 1440px; a second PDP (`/activewear/bodysuits/tank`) confirmed still centred, unaffected. **Once confirmed good, roll out sitewide by dropping the conditional back to a plain `align="left"` (or flipping the prop's own default) rather than leaving the one-slug check in place.**

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`.

### PDP route (`app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`) — Built, reusable template
Recipe: none of its own (composes existing sections/components)

The Activewear PDP (product detail page): one dynamic route renders every real style from every real category's own `styleCards` array (`generateStaticParams`, SSG — this catalogue changes only occasionally, so static is right for both SEO and speed). Segment renamed `[product]` → `[style]` (owner spec, 2026-09-01) — no behavior change, just the URL param's own name, matching the owner's exact requested route path.

**Gallery is sticky on desktop** (`productGallery.desktopRoot`, owner reference, 2026-09-02: nike.ae's own PDP) — confirmed live against that site's own computed style (`position: sticky` on its images column) before matching it: `xl:sticky xl:top-[24px] xl:self-start`. `self-start` stops the column stretching to match `ProductInfo`'s height, which would break sticky (nothing left to stick against). Releases naturally near the bottom of its own flex row, same standard sticky-column behavior Nike's own reference shows — not a bug, expected once the row's remaining content is shorter than the gallery itself.

**Real bug: `top` offset assumed the sitewide `Header` stays visible, but this page's own header hides on scroll** (owner report, live: "gap on top of the image ... adds more gap [when scrolling further]"). First pass used `top-[111px]` (87px header + 24px gap — this project's own established offset for a sticky column beside a header that's *always* visible, the same value `CategoryFilters`' own sidebar used before its non-sticky-header trial). But `Header.tsx` doesn't just stay sticky, it also hides itself on scroll-down past its own height — once it hides, the 87px it used to occupy is gone, while the gallery's `top` offset stayed fixed at 111px regardless, so the empty space where the header used to be read as a gap that kept growing the further down the page you scrolled. Corrected to a flat `top-[24px]`, correct whether the header is currently visible or hidden — sticky only actually engages well after the header has already crossed its own hide threshold on a normal downward scroll, so there's no overlap in practice.

**Adding a new PDP is adding one entry, nothing else** (owner spec): a new style is one object in a category's own `styleCards` array (`content/activewear/<category>.ts`) — no new route, no new schema code, no new metadata code. A whole new category is its own content file added to `content/activewear/categories.ts`'s registry, same as the PLP.

**Metadata**: title form `"Custom [Style] Manufacturer"` (`StyleCard.pdpMetaTitle`, falls back to `` `Custom ${pdpTitle ?? cardTitle} Manufacturer` `` — no `" | Capriowear"` suffix, same reasoning as `Category.metaTitle`'s own comment, the root layout's `title.template` applies automatically to this nested route). Description: `pdpMetaDescription ?? pdpDescription ?? cardSubline`. Self-referencing canonical. OpenGraph/Twitter images: the gallery's first real `src` once photography lands, falling back to `DEFAULT_OG_IMAGE` (`content/site.ts`) until then — never a broken or empty image tag.

**Structured data**, all generated in `lib/schema.ts`, never hand-written per page: `breadcrumbSchema()` (full 4-level trail, Home > Activewear > category > style, fed by the same array the visible `Breadcrumb` renders — see its own entry below for the mobile-hide rule), the new `productSchema()` (name/description/brand/manufacturer/material, **no `offers`/price** — a made-to-order B2B product with no public price, so this stays schema-valid without an Offer rather than publishing invented pricing, same precedent `collectionPageSchema()` already set on the PLP), and `faqSchema()` fed the exact merged FAQ array the page renders (see the FAQ section below) — schema can never describe content the page doesn't show.

**FAQ section**: reuses the PLP's own `Faq`/`Accordion` component verbatim, fed a merged array: the shared entity question first (`pdpFaqEntity`, `content/activewear/pdpShared.ts`), then this style's own 2 to 3 questions (`StyleCard.faqs`), then the shared operational block last (`pdpFaqOperational` — MOQ/grading, sample time, shipping, NDA, get-started). Deliberately different content from the PLP's own FAQ: the PLP answers category/range questions, the PDP answers this-style questions. `content/activewear/pdpShared.ts` is written once and reused unchanged on every PDP — a style file never carries a second, duplicated copy of the shared questions.

**Related styles**: see `ProductCategoryLinks`'s own entry below — that component is what actually renders sibling `styleCards` links plus the "Back to all [Category]" link (this paragraph previously claimed it as already built here; it wasn't, until the 2026-09-02 SEO audit found the gap and built it for real — see that entry and `docs/05-plan.md`'s `5cu`).

**Breadcrumb, mobile rule** (owner spec): the exact same `Breadcrumb` markup renders at every viewport — `hidden md:block` (a CSS-only display toggle via the component's own `className` override, see `Breadcrumb`'s entry) hides it visually below `md`, but it's never conditionally removed from the DOM by screen size, and its `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD sits outside that hidden wrapper entirely (built straight from the same `breadcrumbItems` array, not reading from the hidden DOM), so hiding the visible strip on mobile can never touch the schema. `ProductCategoryLinks`'s own "Back to all [Category]" link (plus the sitewide footer) keeps at least one crawlable upward link on the page at every viewport, so hiding the breadcrumb never drops the only parent link.

**First worked example**: `content/activewear/leggings.ts`'s first style card, slug/href renamed from `high-waist-leggings` to `high-waisted-compression` (the owner's own requested first PDP URL) — safe as a plain rename since no PDP was ever live at the old slug (confirmed via grep before renaming); a rename after a real PDP has shipped would need a 301 redirect instead, per the owner's own standing rule.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` (regenerates Next's route types after the segment rename) all clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression`: fetched the rendered `<head>` and all JSON-LD `<script>` blocks directly (not just visually) — unique title/description/canonical/OG confirmed, `BreadcrumbList` shows the real 4-level trail, `Product` has no `offers`/price field anywhere, `FAQPage`'s `mainEntity` matches the page's own rendered entity → per-style → operational order exactly, `Organization`'s description reflects the sitewide wording update below. `/sitemap.xml` includes the new PDP URL. Scoped Playwright overflow check clean at 360/768/1440px — not yet added to the permanent `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` suite, same standing convention that file's own header comment documents for the PLP (a page still being built section by section stays out of the always-must-pass suite until it's actually done, verified instead via scoped ad-hoc checks as each section ships).

### productSchema() (`lib/schema.ts`) — Built
New builder alongside `breadcrumbSchema()`/`collectionPageSchema()`/`faqSchema()`, same "pure function, fed only by data the calling page already renders" pattern as every other one in this file. Takes `{name, description, image?, material?}`, returns a `Product` with `brand`/`manufacturer` fixed to `ORGANIZATION.name`/`ORGANIZATION.legalName` (Capriowear / Caprio Sports) — **no `offers`/`priceSpecification` field, ever**, matching the PDP's own no-invented-pricing rule. `image`/`material` are omitted from the object entirely when absent, same "no placeholder value in schema" rule `collectionPageSchema()`'s own `image` field already sets.

### Sitemap (`app/sitemap.ts`) — Updated
Gained a second enumeration block: every category's own `styleCards`, each as its own PDP URL, read from the exact same registry `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`'s own `generateStaticParams` reads from — a new style entry is in the sitemap automatically, never a second, hand-typed list of routes to keep in sync. Same pattern the file's own existing category-enumeration block already used.

### Entity intro (`content/site.ts`, `content/activewear/pdpShared.ts`) — Built
One stored source, four variants (owner spec, 2026-09-01) — every surface on the site that names the Capriowear entity references one of these, never retypes its own copy. Three exact strings, `site.ts`: `companyIdentity` (108-char one-liner — the footer's own "who we are" line, and anywhere a single identity sentence prints inline), `companyIntroShort` (304-char, 2-sentence — `ORGANIZATION.description`/`organizationSchema()`, and the About/Our Story page's intro once that page exists; no longer rendered as a visible homepage paragraph — removed 2026-09-06, owner request — though the same sentence still appears inside the homepage FAQ's "What services do you offer?" answer in `content/home.ts`), `companyIntroMeta` (142-char, meta-length — `DEFAULT_DESCRIPTION`, both the homepage's own meta description and the root layout's sitewide fallback for any page without a more specific one of its own, and any future hub page's meta description with the same "no more specific description" gap).

The fourth variant — the 3-sentence PLP/PDP entity FAQ answer ("What does Capriowear manufacture?") — is deliberately not a stored constant: `categoryEntityFaq(category)` (`pdpShared.ts`) builds it per category from that category's own `menuLabel` and up to 4 real style names from its own `styleCards`, ending with `companyIdentity` appended verbatim. Both `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx` and `.../[style]/page.tsx` call it and prepend the result to their own FAQ list at render time, so the visible accordion and `faqSchema()` can never drift apart, and no two categories can ever hand-type slightly different versions of the same answer.

**Four optional overrides, added for Sports Bras (2026-09-02)** — `Category` gained `manufacturerNoun`/`productNounPlural`/`entityExampleStyles`/`entityFabrics`, all read by `categoryEntityFaq()` and all falling back to the exact prior behavior when unset (`menuLabel.toLowerCase()` for the first two, the joined first-4-`cardTitle`s list for the third, no clause at all for the fourth) — confirmed live that Leggings' own rendered entity answer is byte-identical before and after this change. Needed because a single `menuLabel`-derived noun can't always serve both roles the template needs: "leggings" reads correctly as both "a custom leggings manufacturer" and "private label leggings" (same word), but "Sports Bras" needs "sports bra" (singular) in the first and "sports bras" (plural) in the second — two different words. `entityExampleStyles`/`entityFabrics` exist for the same reason on the styles clause: Sports Bras' own card titles are the long PDP-style form ("Custom High-Impact Racerback Sports Bra"), so joining 4 of them verbatim would read as a keyword-stuffed list rather than the owner's own requested categorical phrasing ("high-impact, medium-support, and light-support styles in nylon or recycled polyester blends").

**Fifth optional override, added for Rash Guards & Fight Wear (2026-09-06)** — `Category` gained `audienceClause`, read by `categoryEntityFaq()` and falling back to the exact prior hardcoded text ("for activewear brands and teamwear suppliers worldwide") when unset — confirmed live that Leggings' own rendered entity answer is still byte-identical after this change. Needed because the entity sentence's "for [X]" audience clause was never a per-category field, only ever the one generic activewear/teamwear-brand phrase — correct for every category so far, but wrong for a combat-sports category, whose real buyers ("combat-sports brands, academies and teamwear suppliers worldwide") are a genuinely different audience. Same task also fixed `fight-wear.ts`'s own `manufacturerNoun` from `"Rash Guard & Fight Wear"` to `"Rash Guard and Fight Wear"` — the ampersand read correctly in `h1`/`menuLabel` (separate, explicit fields, untouched) but produced a literal `&` inline in the auto-built sentence ("a custom rash guard & fight wear manufacturer") where prose needs "and".

No About/Our Story page or Activewear/Capabilities/Our Factory hub pages exist yet — the rules for those live as comments next to the relevant `site.ts` constant, for whoever builds them.

### MobileNav — Built
`components/MobileNav.tsx` · recipe: `drawer`

The drawer behind the Header's menu button: a full-screen black takeover, not a partial sliding panel. Locks background scrolling, traps Tab inside itself, closes on Escape or the Close button, and returns focus to the menu button.

Rendered through a portal onto the page body, because the Header's sticky layer would otherwise trap it underneath anything else on the page.

**Corrected on first real use** (Figma node `465:2817`, "Caprio Website" file, 2026-08-27) — built before any open-state design existed, so the earlier shape (a right-sliding `max-w-96` panel with a translucent scrim, dividers between links, 30px/medium link text sized up from the sitewide mobile-title-bump rule, and CTA buttons in a footer) was a reasonable pre-design guess, not what the real design turned out to be:

- **Full-screen panel, no scrim** — `fixed inset-0`, not `inset-y-0 right-0 max-w-96`. A full-screen panel has no "outside" to click, so the separate translucent scrim is gone; Escape and Close remain the only ways out.
- **Head row reuses `header.inner`/`header.menuButton` verbatim** — the drawer's own logo + "Close" pill sits in exactly the same row layout as the real closed header's logo + "Menu" pill (same `container-p`, same mobile padding, same pill recipe), so opening the drawer reads as the same bar flipping state, not a re-guessed layout. `Header` now passes its own `logo`/`brand` down to `MobileNav` for this.
- **Flat list, no dividers, 28px/regular** (`drawer.link`) — corrects the guessed 30px/medium, which came from the sitewide mobile-title-bump rule; this text was never a 24px title, so that rule never applied here, only a real design could settle it.
- **No expand/collapse — the chevron is decorative.** `home.nav.links` (the shared, desktop array) has never had `children`, so the old subList/expand machinery never actually rendered in real content. The real design shows a chevron on exactly two rows (Activewear, Teamwear & Uniforms) with no expanded state anywhere in it, so `MobileNav` now takes its own `home.nav.mobileLinks` dataset (`{label, href, chevron}`) — a separate array from desktop's `links`, so this can't accidentally wake up desktop's mega-menu machinery (which keys off `children`). The chevron glyph itself is a real exported asset (`components/icons/ChevronArrowIcon.tsx`, solid fill, not lucide's stroke-based chevrons — doesn't match), rotated 180° to point right.
- **New content**: a "Get in touch" contact block (`home.nav.contact`, same email Footer already uses) and a social icon row (`LinkedinIcon`/`InstagramIcon`/`FacebookIcon`, reused from `components/icons/SocialIcons.tsx`, inside a new `size-[50px]`/`#292929`-bg wrapper — a different treatment from Footer's own 60px/light-bg social buttons, so a separate set of `drawer.social*` recipe keys, not shared ones). Facebook renders unlinked, matching the sitewide rule (no real Facebook URL yet in `ORGANIZATION.sameAs`). Both sit in `bottomWrap`, gapped down from the last link by a fixed `mt-[132px]` -- Figma's own real, confirmed gap (`get_metadata`: "Our Story"'s bottom edge to this block's top). This replaced an initial `mt-auto` build (flex auto-margin, meant to "hold up at real device heights") that turned out to be the actual bug: on a viewport short enough that the list + header + this block already filled the screen with no leftover space, `mt-auto` collapsed to 0 and the last link touched "Get in touch" directly (found live, 2026-08-27). A fixed gap is correct at every height and harmless on a tall one, since `panel` already scrolls.
- **CTA buttons removed** — not present in this design at all.
- **Open/close transition matches the owner's own reference recording** (`reference/Nav.mov`, afternow.co/services') — a single `clip-path` inset sweeping up from the bottom, not a per-item stagger: opening runs `inset(100%→0%)` from the top edge on `<nav>` (`drawer.navReveal`/`navRevealOpen`/`navRevealClosed`), so the bottom rows (Contact, the social icons) appear first and the top row (Activewear) resolves last; closing is the exact mirror. The header row (logo + Menu/Close pill) never animates — only the content below it does. `clip-path` only affects paint, not layout, so none of the exact spacing above needed to change. `MobileNav` now stays mounted for 500ms after `open` goes false (`rendered` state, `CLOSE_TRANSITION_MS`) so the closing transition has time to play before the portal unmounts.

**Activewear and Teamwear & Uniforms mega menus, a second in-drawer screen** (Figma nodes `473:2919` and `485:2992`, both 2026-08-27) — tapping either link no longer navigates directly; it pushes a full category breakdown (Activewear: five groups — TOPS, BOTTOMS, SETS & ONE PIECES, OUTWEAR & SUITS, BASE LAYERS; Teamwear & Uniforms: two groups — UNIFORMS, OTHERS) inside the same drawer, with a "Back" row to return. `MobileNavLink` gained an optional `megaMenu` field for exactly this; both links now use it, so `chevron: true` is functional on both rows, not decorative.

The push/back transition is a second, independent motion layered *inside* the existing 900ms open/close `clip-path` reveal, not a replacement for it: `nav`'s content is now two screens (`drawer.screen`, each exactly half of a `flex w-[200%]` track, `drawer.screens`) that slide between `translateX(0)`/`translateX(-50%)` on a 350ms `transition-transform` — deliberately quicker than the outer reveal, since this is a lighter "push between two already-open screens," not the same kind of transition. The Back row reuses the existing `ChevronArrowIcon` unrotated (already confirmed to point left by default — the exact direction needed, no new asset) and only clears the active megaMenu (`activeMegaMenuHref`), it never calls `onClose`; category item links do call `onClose`, closing the whole drawer on navigation like every other real link here. `activeMegaMenuHref` resets to `null` in the same delayed effect that unmounts the panel on close, so a reopened drawer always starts at the main screen — resetting it the instant `open` goes false instead would visibly snap the still-closing drawer back to the main list mid-animation.

Group labels (TOPS, BOTTOMS, etc., `drawer.megaMenuGroupLabel`) are `font-medium` (owner call, 2026-08-27) — matching the same weight change made to the desktop mega menu's own group labels (`Header`'s `megaGroupLabel`), applied here as its own separate recipe key since it's a different component.

**Bug found live the same day and fixed**: the 200%-wide `screens` track was first clipped by putting `overflow-x-hidden` directly on `nav` — but `nav` also carries `container-p`'s own side padding, so that clipped at the *padding* edge, not the *content* edge. Since each screen is sized to `nav`'s content box, the off-screen screen's own far edge (where a right-aligned glyph sits — the Back icon at rest, or both list-row chevrons once the megaMenu is active) landed exactly inside that still-visible padding zone and peeked through as a stray chevron: one extra chevron visible on the main screen at rest (the Back icon), and two extra (Activewear's and Teamwear's own list chevrons) visible once the megaMenu was active. Fixed by moving the clip to a new, unpadded wrapper (`drawer.screensClip`, a plain block child of `nav`) whose own box exactly matches the screens' content edges — verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()`: the clip wrapper now spans exactly `x:20` to `x:355` (the real content box on a 375px viewport), and both screens' far edges land at or beyond that boundary in every state, with zero pixels bleeding through.

One content note: Figma's own list-item text layers in this frame are tagged `Inter`, unlike every other layer in it (and everywhere else on the site, all `Figtree` — the project's only loaded typeface per `CLAUDE.md`). Treated as a Figma authoring oversight rather than a deliberate second-typeface request; `megaMenuItem` intentionally leaves font-family unset so it inherits the page's real Figtree.

**Item type corrected to 20px/36px** (owner correction, 2026-08-30, re-checked directly against node `473:2919`) — `drawer.megaMenuItem` was `text-[1.5rem] leading-[44px]` (24px/44px), a misread on first build; line height iterated `40px` → `38px` → `36px` in quick follow-ups the same session. One shared class covers both mega menus (Activewear's groups and Teamwear & Uniforms' groups both render through the same `megaMenuGroup`/`.megaMenuItem` markup), so each edit updated both, with no second place to drift out of sync.

### HeaderOverlayNav — Built (exploratory, styleguide only)
`components/HeaderOverlayNav.tsx` · recipe: `headerOverlay`

**Not wired into the live page** — `Header.tsx`/`MobileNav.tsx` are untouched and stay the real site nav. This is a second, standalone nav component for side-by-side comparison in `/styleguide` only (`Header — full-screen overlay nav (exploratory)`, index 10), following the same "build the alternate for real, don't touch the live one" pattern already used for Client Logos' mobile marquee variant.

Owner reference, 2026-08-27: `afternow.co/services/`. Confirmed live (DOM/computed-style inspection, not guessed) that its nav is a sticky top bar — logo, plain inline links, a CTA, and a small always-visible toggle — where the toggle opens a full-screen panel *below* the still-visible top bar (logo and toggle stay put, the toggle's own icon swaps to a close state, the inline links/CTA fade out). No design exists for this on Capriowear, so it's an explicit exploration: real nav content (`home.nav`), real social links (`content/site.ts`'s `ORGANIZATION.sameAs`), but every type size and colour reused verbatim from this project's own tokens per the owner's "keep my visual language" instruction — `text-h1` for the big link list, `bg-ink`/`text-paper` for the panel (the same "dramatic full-screen dark moment" already established by Hero/Stats/Final CTA/Footer), the real `Button` component, `container-p`.

Two deliberate simplifications from the literal reference, since there was nothing to match exactly: the reference's 4-dot-to-X GSAP morph is `Menu`/`X` (lucide-react), the same icon pair `Header.tsx`'s own drawer trigger already swaps; the reference's promotional/case-study content inside the panel is agency-specific and isn't reproduced — only the reusable structural idea (large-type link list + socials in a full-screen takeover) is built.

Mechanics closely follow `MobileNav`'s already-proven pattern rather than inventing a new one: portalled onto `document.body` (Header's `sticky z-40` is a stacking context a nested panel would be trapped under), a small hand-rolled focus trap (Escape closes, Tab cycles inside the panel, focus moves to the first link on open and back to the toggle on close), background scroll locked while open, nothing rendered while closed (no DOM, no tab stops). No enter/exit transition, matching `MobileNav`'s own precedent (its drawer also mounts/unmounts abruptly). The panel's own `top` is set inline from the real header's *measured* height (`headerRef.current.offsetHeight`, the same "measure, don't guess" approach `Header.tsx`'s hide-on-scroll threshold already uses), so it starts exactly below the top bar at any breakpoint.

Social icons reuse `LinkedinIcon`/`InstagramIcon`/`FacebookIcon`, extracted this same day from `Footer.tsx` into `components/icons/SocialIcons.tsx` once a second component needed them — a real "build once" refactor, not speculative (`Footer.tsx` now imports from there too, same SVGs, no visual change). Facebook renders unlinked (a plain `<span>`), matching Footer's own established rule: no real Facebook page exists yet, and this project never links a placeholder URL.

### Footer — Built
`components/sections/Footer.tsx` · recipe: `footer`

Site footer, homepage section 15. Figma: desktop node `461:2683`, mobile node `499:3275` (moved from the original `461:2715` at some point during the desktop content-arrangement pass below -- the old ID stopped resolving; re-confirmed 2026-08-27, same real values: 40px top / 20px side inset) -- genuinely different layouts per breakpoint (desktop: multi-row grid, nav/description then contact/address; mobile: one flat stacked column, gap-24, 3 dividers instead of desktop's 1), not one responsive reflow.

Desktop was updated 2026-08-27 (owner content-arrangement pass, desktop-only frame supplied) from the original `461:2650`: nav and description swapped sides (nav now left-flush, description now sits `gap-[78px]` to its right rather than pushed full-width right); the address block (`10 KM...`/`Caprio 2000-2026...`) changed from a vertical stack to two columns side by side, 32px gap, still bottom-aligned flush with the contact block per `desktopRow3`'s `items-end`; the contact label's colour moved from `#3c3c43` to `#17191e`; and the frame's own height grew from 720 to 750, entirely a consequence of `desktopRow3`'s own top-gap increasing from 194px to 296px (there's no explicit height anywhere in this component -- content spacing drives it, same as every other section). A same-day follow-up narrowed just the copyright line's own container (194px → 174px, `desktopAddressLineNarrow`) -- the row itself still ends flush at the container's real right edge (confirmed live: `1186 + 174 = 1360`), only that one line's own width changed.

Social icon buttons (`socialButton`, shared with `HeaderOverlayNav`) fill with the brand accent orange on hover and the icon (`currentColor`) turns white -- both existing tokens (`bg-accent`/`text-accent-ink`), replacing the earlier plain opacity fade (owner call, 2026-08-27).

Its root carries the site's "reveal" scroll transition (owner reference, 2026-08-26: `afternow.co/services/`), which needs **four** things, not one -- three rounds of the same bug class shipped before this was right. Round 1 (`sticky bottom-0` alone): `position: sticky; bottom: 0` on `footer.root` pins the box to the viewport's bottom edge for the *entire* scroll range, not just near the page's end, and being "positioned" with no z-index of its own to lose, paints *above* ordinary in-flow content by default -- so it permanently covered the page from the very first scroll pixel (owner report: "footer covers content"). Round 2 added `footer.root`'s `z-0` and `<main>`'s `relative z-10` (`app/page.tsx`) to win the stacking order -- necessary, but not sufficient: winning the stacking order only re-orders *paint layers*, it doesn't make a transparent one opaque. `<main>` and every light section (anything not `bg-ink`) had no background of their own, relying on `<body>`'s `bg-paper` instead -- which sits *under* both `<main>` (z-10) and Footer (z-0) in the paint stack, not between them, so Footer's content showed straight through every light section's transparent box the whole time it was scrolled past. Confirmed via the owner's own screen recording: ghosted footer nav/address text visible over Trust Signals and Certified & Compliant. Fixed by adding `bg-paper` directly to `<main>` -- a real, opaque white layer between Footer's `z-0` and every light section's own transparent wrapper. Dark sections were never affected (their own `bg-ink` already covered them). The same visual effect as the reference site, produced by getting the full paint stack right (opaque `<main>` above `z-0` Footer above `<body>`), not by sticky's timing alone. No JS.

**Round 4 (2026-08-27), a different class of bug**: owner report that the mobile logo's own top padding "isn't showing" during the reveal. The padding (40px, confirmed again against the current Figma node above) was never actually missing -- confirmed live via `document.elementFromPoint()` that `<main>`'s own FAQ content is what's genuinely painted at that screen position mid-scroll, not the logo. The real cause: `<main>` and Footer share the exact same `bg-paper` white, so the covering edge that sweeps up during the reveal is itself invisible -- whatever Footer content sits right at its own top (the logo, its own real gap above it) appears to pop in already clipped for the few frames while `<main>`'s edge is still crossing it, purely because there's no visible boundary to show a transition is even happening. A shadow was tried on `footer.root` itself first and reverted the same session: it doesn't work, for the identical reason the padding didn't show -- Footer is the *covered* (`z-0`) layer, so a shadow painted as part of its own box is exactly as hidden by `<main>`'s covering as the logo was, until the same instant everything else becomes visible; it would pop in exactly as abruptly as the content it was meant to soften. The real fix lives on `<main>` instead (`shadow-[0_16px_24px_-12px_rgba(14,14,18,0.18)]`, `app/page.tsx`) -- `<main>` is the layer actually doing the covering, so a shadow cast from *its* bottom edge moves with the visible seam as it scrolls, staying visible at whatever point the reveal currently sits, giving the transition a deliberate "the page above is lifting away" depth cue instead of relying on a colour boundary that was never actually visible.

Social links (LinkedIn, Instagram) read from `content/site.ts`'s `ORGANIZATION.sameAs` -- matched to an icon by URL substring, not array position. Icons are real exported Figma assets (also saved to `public/icons/linkedin.svg` / `public/icons/instagram.svg`), pulled from the shared `components/icons/SocialIcons.tsx` module (also used by `HeaderOverlayNav`, see below). Facebook's icon renders too (owner call, 2026-08-26 -- also saved to `public/icons/facebook.svg`), but as a plain `<span>`, not a real link: no Facebook page exists yet, and `content/site.ts`'s own rule is never to link a placeholder URL. Swap it for a real `<a href>` the moment `ORGANIZATION.sameAs` gains a Facebook URL.

**`content.nav`'s links corrected to `next/link` (2026-08-27)**, ahead of adding `basePath: "/capriowear"` at launch: all six (`desktopNavColumnOne`/`Two`, `mobileNavList`) were plain `<a href={link.href}>` -- fine today, but a plain anchor doesn't get basePath's automatic rewrite once it's added, so these would silently stop pointing at `/capriowear/...` the moment that config lands. Social links and the `mailto:` email stay plain `<a>`, correctly -- they're genuinely external/non-routable destinations, exactly the case basePath rewriting shouldn't touch. See the file's own header comment for the rule this follows.

A two-tone orange seam bar at the transition (owner reference, 2026-08-27: `afternow.co/services/`'s own footer transition) was attempted and abandoned the same day, after four tries across two components (a blurred glow, a static bar, then two scroll-linked animation builds, the last one in a separate `FooterSeam.tsx`) never read close enough to the reference to keep -- owner call: "leave it, let's remove it." All of it (the component, its recipe keys, its wiring into `app/page.tsx`) was deleted rather than left half-working. If this is revisited, the real lesson from the attempt is worth keeping even though the code isn't: a correct-looking DOM measurement (`getBoundingClientRect()`, `getComputedStyle()`) is not proof something is actually painted on screen -- every wrong version along the way had plausible, even "correct," computed values right up until an actual pixel-level screenshot was taken and checked. Verify visually, not just structurally, for anything involving `position: sticky`, stacking order, or scroll-driven layout.

`content/home.ts`'s `footer.nav` reuses real routes already used elsewhere (Services → `/capabilities`, Our Factory → `/our-factory`, Download Catalog → `/catalog`, Request a Sample → `/request-a-sample`); `/our-story` is a new link with no page built yet, the same "link ahead of the page" pattern already accepted for other nav links.

**Mobile logo moved onto `stacked` (owner, 2026-09-06: "Update the mobile footer logo", Figma node `590:1421`)** — `footer.mobileBrandLogo` now renders `<Logo stacked className={footer.mobileBrandLogo} />`, matching the same "CAPRIO" over "WEAR" mark already used everywhere else (Header, Footer desktop). `get_metadata` on that frame's own "Logo" node measured the real block at `190.99x50.91`, so the recipe's height moved from the old default-geometry's fitted `h-[28px]` to `h-[51px]` — at the stacked mark's ~3.7:1 aspect ratio that renders ~189px wide, comfortably inside the 320px column available at the narrowest (360px) target viewport. This was the one real-page usage still left on `Logo`'s retiring default (side-by-side) geometry — see `Logo`'s own entry above, which now only lists `IntroLoader` there. Confirmed live at 375px and via the full `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` suite (all 53 passing).

**Mobile top spacing corrected to a flat 40px (owner, 2026-09-07: "make it 40px only")** — `footer.mobileBrandGroup` dropped its own `mt-14` (56px); the logo now sits directly on `mobileOuter`'s existing `pt-10` (40px) instead of that padding plus the group's extra margin stacked on top (was 96px total). Confirmed live: `logoRect.top - footerRect.top === 40` at 375px.

**Desktop tagline relocated + logo top spacing (owner, 2026-09-06/07)**: `content.tagline` ("Capriowear, a division of Caprio Sports") moved out of row 1's `desktopBrandGroup`/mobile's brand group (both now just the logo) and now renders directly above the description paragraph instead, in a new `desktopDescriptionGroup`/`mobileDescriptionGroup` wrapper (`flex flex-col gap-3`, i.e. tagline, 12px gap, paragraph — no divider between them, tried once and explicitly reverted: "don't add separator under division line"). Space above the logo itself was confirmed live (via `get_metadata` on Figma node `587:5755`) to already be exactly `desktopInner`'s existing `pt-14` (56px, `desktopInner`'s "Content" frame sits at `y=56` inside the footer frame) — a `pt-20` (80px) trial was tried and reverted back to `pt-14` the same session.

**Desktop footer height trimmed, `desktopRow3` 296px → 170px (owner, 2026-09-07)**: the footer's own `sticky bottom-0` reveal trick (see the "reveal" note above) can only ever show as much of itself as fits inside the current viewport height. At the old ~826px total footer height, this clipped the footer's own top edge (the 56px gap + logo) on any browser window shorter than that — common on 13-14" laptops (~700-850px of actual usable viewport after browser chrome) — which read as "the logo doesn't have its 56px" even though the value itself was always correct; a large external monitor's taller viewport comfortably fit the whole 826px, so it "looked fine" there. Confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect` (footer height 826px > a tested 816px laptop-sized viewport). Fixed by trimming `desktopRow3`'s own top margin (Figma's real 296px, the single largest contributor to the footer's total height) down to `170px`, landing the footer at ~700px total — comfortably under that whole laptop range again, confirmed live post-change. A genuine, confirmed deviation from Figma's own 296px number, not a mistake -- kept as the exact value that fixed the real bug, not reverted back to the Figma-exact one.

**Nav column 1 gained "Activewear"/"Teamwear & Uniforms" ahead of "Services" (owner, 2026-09-07: "in the footer, all platforms, we are missing activewear and teamwear & uniforms, add them on top of services")** — `content/home.ts`'s `footer.nav.columnOne` gained two entries at the top, reusing the exact same label/href pairs as the header nav (`/activewear`, `/teamwear`), not new routes. Pure content-array change on both platforms at once, since both `Footer.tsx`'s desktop (`desktopNavColumnOne`) and mobile (`mobileNavList`, which flattens `columnOne`+`columnTwo` into one list) read the same `columnOne` array.

**Real wrap bug found immediately after, fixed the same day (owner: "make teamwear & uniforms in one line")**: `desktopNavColumnOne` was a fixed `w-[182px]` box (Figma's own real value, sized for the 3 original short links) with no `whitespace-nowrap` — "Teamwear & Uniforms" (~232px natural width) wrapped to 2 lines inside it, the identical defect already found and fixed once for this exact string in the header's own mega menu (see `HeaderOverlayNav`'s `megaGroup`/`megaItem` entry). Fixed the same way conceptually: `desktopNavColumnOne` dropped its fixed width for `whitespace-nowrap` (matching `desktopNavColumnTwo`'s own existing auto-width/nowrap approach), and `desktopNavGroup` gained an explicit `gap-12` (48px) between the two columns — the old fixed 182px box had supplied that visual separation for free, as unused space beside 3 links all narrower than it; an auto-width column needs a real gap now that nothing pushes column 2 over automatically. Mobile was already single-line at every real width (`mobileNavList` has no fixed-width constraint), confirmed unaffected (40px tall, one `leading-10` line).

**`desktopNavGroup`'s gap widened from `gap-12` (48px) to `gap-[78px]`, later the same day (owner: "the space between 3 columns [Activewear column, Download Catalog column, description paragraph] should be the same")** — `desktopRow2`'s own `gap-[78px]` between the nav group as a whole and the description paragraph was already 78px; the two nav columns' own internal gap was a separate, smaller 48px, so the row read as unevenly spaced. Both gaps are now 78px, so all 3 visual columns in the row are evenly spaced.

**A second wrap bug, same day, in the tagline above it (owner: "make sports in one line too and adjust the under paragraph with the same width")**: `desktopDescriptionGroup`'s `max-w-[307px]` (shared by both the tagline and the description paragraph below it) wrapped `content.tagline` ("Capriowear, a division of Caprio Sports", ~347.6px natural width at this size) to 2 lines. Widened to `max-w-[350px]` — a few px of buffer over the measured natural width. Since both the tagline and the paragraph live inside this one shared flex container, widening it gives the paragraph the exact same new width automatically — the "same width" the owner asked for wasn't a second value to set, just the one shared container growing. Confirmed live: tagline now measures a single 24.4px-tall line (was 48.9px/2 lines); both tagline and paragraph measure the same 350px width. Real mobile's tagline was already single-line at every real width (`mobileDescriptionGroup` has no max-width of its own — confirmed 335px/one line, unchanged).

## Homepage sections

Composed from the components above, in wireframe order. Built section by section as Phase 2 progresses — see [05-plan.md](05-plan.md).

Section files live in **`components/sections/`**, separate from `/components`: a section is a real layout composition (built and verified against one specific Figma design), where everything in `/components` is a reusable atom or molecule. `Header` is the one exception, kept in `/components` since it renders on every page, not just the homepage.

**Every section takes its content as props, not a direct `content/home.ts` import** (changed 2026-08-24) — each one used to import `home` and read its own key directly, which meant the layout itself could only ever render homepage copy. A section's *layout* (e.g. "certification logos: static row desktop, bordered grid mobile") is often genuinely reusable on another page even when the copy isn't — reusing it there is now just passing a different content object as a prop, not touching the section's markup or styling at all. Prop types are `typeof home.x`, so the shape stays derived from one real source rather than duplicated. This is a deliberately small, reversible step — not a push to build a fully generic, config-driven layout system before a second real page actually needs one of these patterns (see the decision log in [05-plan.md](05-plan.md), 2026-08-24).

### Hero — Built
`components/sections/Hero.tsx` · Figma: desktop node `322:1522`, mobile node `323:1648`

Three layers in one Figma frame, so one component: banner (eyebrow, H1, single CTA), video placeholder with a play control, and the offerings ticker.

**Real differences between desktop and mobile, confirmed against Figma, not guessed:**

- **Secondary "Download Catalog" CTA is desktop-only** (`xl` and up). Not on mobile at all — final as of 2026-08-23, after two corrections (present on both → removed entirely → desktop-only).
- The video placeholder is **edge-to-edge on mobile** (no side inset) and inset like every other section from `xl` up. `.container-p` can't express "no padding below xl, padding from xl" since it's a hand-written CSS class, not composable Tailwind utilities — `hero.videoWrap` re-implements its max-width and centring with responsive padding instead, confined to this one section.
- **Video box height gained a `md:` tablet tier (owner, 2026-09-03, tablet-width review: "hero banner height should be fixed next ... this looks small at the moment")** — `hero.video` was `h-[390px] xl:h-[650px]`, so the 390px mobile height (tuned for a ~375-430px phone) stayed pinned all the way through 1279px tablet width while the box's own width grew to the full container, reading thin. No tablet Figma frame exists for an exact number, so `md:h-[520px]` is a judgment call, not a confirmed value — roughly the midpoint between 390 and 650. Confirmed live: 390px at 375px width, 520px at 1024px, 650px at 1440px (unchanged at both real mobile and desktop).
- The ticker is a horizontal `Marquee` on desktop, and a **plain vertical list** on mobile — no scrolling, no icons. These are different layouts, not one component reflowing, so mobile is bare markup in `Hero.tsx` rather than a `Marquee` mode. Desktop's label ("Fully Custom Offerings," `marquee.labelBold`) is **font-normal regular**, not semibold (owner call, 2026-08-26) — the only usage of `labelVariant="bold"` sitewide, so this was a safe direct weight change. **Never pauses on hover** (`pauseOnHover={false}`, owner call, 2026-08-26) — same reasoning as Client Logos' tickers: decorative, not something to read.
- **Desktop Marquee now shows from `md:` (768px), not `xl:` (owner, 2026-09-03, first of a page-by-page tablet-width review, starting with the homepage: "fully custom offerings, that should be as shown in desktop, not mobile for 768-1279px")** — the scrolling ticker reads fine at real tablet width; the mobile stacked-list fallback (`hero.tickerMobile`) is now genuinely mobile-only. Both wrappers (the desktop `<div className="hidden md:block">` and `hero.tickerMobile`'s own `md:hidden`) moved together — confirmed live: desktop Marquee renders at 1024px, mobile list still renders unchanged at 375px, both unaffected at 1440px (`xl:` desktop already showed Marquee) and no layout overflow in the screenshot suite's own 768/810/820/834px viewports.
- The mobile eyebrow is smaller than the shared Overline token — passed via `Eyebrow`'s `size` prop, not a change to the token everyone else uses.
- The video placeholder's play control is a circle (96px desktop via `size-24`, a 4px rounding from Figma's 100px; 64px mobile, exact) with a "Play Video" label underneath, using `MediaPlaceholder`'s `showLabel={false}` so its own caption doesn't double up with the play control's label.
- The mobile ticker item colour is a flat **`#838D97`** (`hero.tickerMobileItem`, owner call, 2026-08-24), not `text-paper/70` — no existing colour token matches this hex, so it's a one-off arbitrary value scoped to this line, not a new sitewide token.
- **Hero's gap to the next section is a `margin` on `hero.section`, not `padding` inside it** — Hero is the only built section with its own full-bleed background colour, so its standard 72px mobile section gap (see [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md)) can't be `padding` on `hero.tickerMobile` the way every other section does it: padding sits inside the element carrying `bg-ink`, so it would just render as more black, not a visible gap (a real bug, found from an owner screenshot and fixed 2026-08-24). `hero.tickerMobile` keeps a fixed 48px top/bottom inset instead (content inside a coloured box always gets this, independent of the between-sections gap), and `hero.section` carries `max-xl:mb-[72px]` / `xl:mb-0` for the actual gap, which renders as the page's own background.
- **No longer widens past 1440px at 1920px+** (`.hero-wide-pilot` removed 2026-08-26, see [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md)'s Grid and container section and the decision log) — `bannerInner`/`videoWrap` are plain `container-p` now, matching every other section's container width at any viewport.
- **The H1 animates in word-by-word on page load** (`TextReveal`, owner reference `afternow.co/services/`, 2026-08-27) — same existing `hero.heading`/`text-display` classes, only the children changed from a plain text node to `TextReveal`'s word-reveal markup. See `TextReveal`'s own entry above.
- **The video block reveals via a `clip-path` unfold** (`RevealBox`, owner reference `cuberto.com`, 2026-08-27) — the existing `<MediaPlaceholder>` is wrapped in `RevealBox`, no changes to `MediaPlaceholder` itself. Trails the headline's own word-reveal slightly (`transition-delay: 0.2s`) so the two don't pop in at the same instant. See `RevealBox`'s own entry above.
- **A desktop photo-grid experiment (2026-08-27) was built, then removed the same day** — three attempts at a Hero banner photo grid (see `PhotoCollage`'s own "Removed" entry above) never matched the owner's reference closely enough. `hero.bannerInner` is back to its original single-column layout on every breakpoint; no `bannerTextCol`/`bannerPhotoCol` split remains.

**Noted, not fixed here:** the mobile Figma frame shows a nav trigger styled as a pill-outlined "Menu" button with a visible label, different from the icon-only `MobileNav` trigger already shipped and reviewed. Flagged for its own task, since changing the nav wasn't in scope here.

### ClientLogos — Built
`components/sections/ClientLogos.tsx` · Figma: desktop node `341:1732`, mobile node `343:1840`

The first light (paper-background) homepage section — every other section built so far is on ink. No label above the logos at either breakpoint in the original Figma frame, correcting an earlier wireframe-era guess ("PRODUCING FOR BRANDS WORLDWIDE") that had no real design behind it — **a title was added back on top of Figma 2026-08-26** (`brandLogos.title`, "Trusted by top brands worldwide"), a deliberate owner addition referencing tedy.app's "Trusted by 500+ businesses" treatment, not something Figma shows.

**Real differences between desktop and mobile — a genuinely different layout, not one responsive treatment:**

- **Desktop** is a horizontal `Marquee` (`separator="none"`, `gap="loose"` for the confirmed 72px spacing, `divider={false}`, `pauseOnHover={false}`, `edgeFade`), full colour, no border under the band. Each logo renders at its **exact Figma display size** (no shared height, no scaling) — `width`/`height` in `content/home.ts`'s `brandLogos.registry` are each node's own real export dimensions, not the source file's intrinsic size. The title sits **beside** the ticker, not above it (`label`/`labelVariant="title"`/`layout="inline"`) — matching tedy.app's side-by-side treatment, Heading 5, its box tightened to a chosen 200px width (2026-08-26 — see `Marquee`'s own entry above for why this needed an `inline-block`, not a naive `max-width` on the label itself). Hovering doesn't stop the scroll (`pauseOnHover={false}`) and the strip's own left/right edges fade to transparent (`edgeFade`) — both 2026-08-26, referencing tedy.app, neither in the original Figma frame.
- **Mobile is a title, then a scrolling `Marquee` of the logos** (`separator="none"`, `gap="default"`/40px, `divider={false}`, `pauseOnHover={false}`, `edgeFade`) — the live treatment as of 2026-08-26, replacing an earlier static 2-column grid (kept, styleguide-only, as `ClientLogosMobileGrid` — see below). Each logo renders at its **own exact Figma mobile size** (`mobileWidth`/`mobileHeight` in the registry — a different size than the desktop ticker uses for the same logo, not the same one reused smaller). The title sits **above** the marquee, centred — **24px/400 regular** (owner call, 2026-08-26, matching Hero's own "Fully Custom Offerings" ticker label size rather than the sitewide "mobile title is 30px" rule from 3x, a deliberate exception for this one title). Section padding is a flat **40px top and 40px bottom**, with exactly **32px** between the title and the marquee (`clientLogos.mobileWrap`, owner call, 2026-08-26) — also its own exception to the standard mobile `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]` pattern every other section uses. Getting the padding exact requires disabling `Marquee`'s own internal `pt-10`/`pb-8` on this specific instance via its `padded={false}` prop (see the note below and `Marquee`'s own entry above — not a negative margin, which was tried first and caused a real clipping bug).

  **The now-archived static grid** (`ClientLogosMobileGrid`, styleguide-only) took four attempts to get right, worth recording so the same dead ends aren't retried if it's ever revived: giving every cell a full 1px border on all sides makes every internal shared edge two lines thick, since adjacent cells' borders sit on two different pixel rows, not one. `gap: -1px` looks like the fix but isn't — CSS `gap` cannot go negative at all (the spec disallows it; the browser silently clamps to 0, so nothing visibly changes). `outline` doesn't solve it either, for a less obvious reason: outline is still owned by one element at a time, so two adjacent cells' own outlines still end up side by side, exactly like two borders would. A fourth approach — edge-ownership border-collapse, where each cell draws only its own right and bottom edge and a separate wrapper draws the outer top and left edge — solved the doubled lines, but broke a corner radius when one was later added (see below): clipping a square-cornered border with a rounded `overflow-hidden` mask doesn't round the border itself, it just cuts it off at an angle, showing as a visibly jagged, stair-stepped corner rather than a smooth curve. **What actually works, corners included**: the outer rectangle is drawn as **one border on `mobileGrid` itself** (all 4 sides, so its own `rounded-[4px]` renders as a native, smooth curve — a border and its radius only curve smoothly when they live on the same element). Cells (`mobileItem`) draw **only the internal dividers between them** (`mobileItemDividerRight` on left-column cells, `mobileItemDividerBottom` on every row but the last, computed per cell in `ClientLogos.tsx`) — never the outer edges, so there's nothing left for a radius to clip awkwardly. `mobileGrid` has to be a distinct element from its own wrapper (which carries the section's own vertical padding) — putting the border on the padded element pushes the line 40px away from the actual cells instead of hugging them. Corners were square here until 2026-08-24, when `rounded-[4px]` was added to match Certified & Compliant's grid (owner call, applying the same radius to both) — the corner-radius bug above was actually found here first (a jagged ISO 9001 corner in Certified & Compliant), then fixed on both sections at once, since they share this exact recipe pattern.
- Section padding is **120px top / 0px bottom on desktop** — given directly by the owner after the desktop Figma frame had been temporarily widened to show off-screen logos, which made its own height untrustworthy for padding even though its width (used for the logo positions and the confirmed 72px gaps) still was. **Mobile is `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]`**, the standard mobile section-to-section pattern (2026-08-24) — was 40px/40px until that standard existed.
- The desktop ticker's horizontal inset matches every other section (80px, same as the offerings ticker) — a first pass wrapped the ticker in its own `container-p` *in addition to* `Marquee`'s own internal one, silently doubling the padding to 160px. Fixed by removing the outer wrapper's `container-p`, since `Marquee`'s `innerStacked` already provides it.

**Real logo artwork, permission confirmed 2026-08-24** (open question 4 resolved). Eight files in `public/logos/`, each exported directly off its own Figma node (`341:1734`–`341:1741` desktop, `343:1861` for WOLFpak's separate mobile asset) via `download_assets` **per node**, not from the parent frame's aggregated asset list — that list mixed in an unrelated raster fill for RISE (its real mark is a vector shape in Figma) and would have risked the wrong file for at least one brand. Full colour, not the greyscale-on-hover treatment `LogoRow` uses for other logo rows, since Figma shows these in colour throughout — and `LogoRow` itself isn't used here at all (see above).

**WOLFpak has two different real assets, not one reused**: desktop's node is icon-only (no wordmark); the mobile grid's actual Figma fill for that cell is icon-plus-wordmark together. `brandLogos.registry.WOLFpak.mobileOverride` carries the second one, and `ClientLogos.tsx`'s `Logo` helper picks it when rendering mobile. (A first read of the tiny desktop crop misjudged the name as "OLFpak" — resolved once the full-size source was inspected directly; it is WOLFpak.)

**`public/logos/wolfpak-mobile.png` was the wrong asset, found and fixed 2026-08-26** (owner-flagged): the file in place was a different, more detailed wolf-icon-plus-full-"WOLFpak"-wordmark image, not actually sourced from Figma node `343:1861` despite this project's own rule that every logo is exported directly off its Figma node. Re-exported from that node and replaced (50×57, matching the registry's already-correct `mobileWidth`/`mobileHeight` — only the image file itself was wrong, not the dimensions). **Worth remembering**: `download_assets` returns two different things per node — `rawImages` is the original uploaded source *before* Figma's own crop/fill transform (can look completely different, and was the wrong one grabbed on the first attempt here); `export` is the actual rendered, cropped-to-fit PNG that matches what Figma displays, and is the one to use. Desktop's `wolfpak.png` (node `341:1740`) was checked separately and confirmed already correct — no change needed there.

**Mobile treatment swapped, 2026-08-26 — the marquee is now live, the grid is now the archived alternative.** `ClientLogos`'s own mobile block renders `MobileMarquee` directly (title, then a scrolling `Marquee` of the mobile logos, `gap="default"`/40px — `"loose"`/72px read as too much space between logos at this narrower width). The former live grid was kept, not deleted: it's exported as `ClientLogosMobileGrid`, styleguide-only, alongside `ClientLogosMobileMarquee` (also exported, rendered without `xl:hidden`, both forced to a fixed mobile width so `/styleguide` can show them for comparison regardless of the reviewer's actual browser width). `MobileMarquee` and `MobileGrid` are two internal helpers inside `ClientLogos.tsx`, each parameterized by `wrapClassName` so the live and styleguide renders share one definition and can't drift apart.

The wrapper contract is exactly **40px top / 32px title-to-marquee gap / 40px bottom**, confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()`. `Marquee`'s own `marquee.base` carries internal padding (`pt-10`/`pb-8`, 40px/32px) that would otherwise stack with the wrapper's own `pt-[40px]`/`pb-[40px]` (72px each side, not 40 or 32). **`MobileMarquee` disables it via `padded={false}`**, a real `Marquee` prop (`marquee.basePaddingNone` vs. the default `basePaddingDefault`) — not a negative margin. A negative-margin cancellation (`-mt-10`/`-mb-8`) was the first attempt and caused a real, visible bug, worth remembering: it shifted the box (and its opaque `toneLight`/`bg-paper` background) up relative to its own flex position without changing where the flex layout placed it, so the box's top edge ended up 8px above the gap, painting `bg-paper` directly over the title's own descenders (later DOM siblings paint over earlier ones at the same stacking level) — this read as "the title is being cut off," not a spacing bug. `padded={false}` removes the padding at its source instead, so the box's edges never move relative to its layout position and there's nothing left to paint over a neighbour.

**Edge fade is a fixed 20px, not a percentage**, corrected 2026-08-26: the original `mask-image` used percentage stops (`black 8%, black 92%`), which widens on every viewport bigger than the one it was tuned on, hiding more logos than intended as the track gets wider. `.marquee-edge-fade` in `app/globals.css` now uses fixed px stops (`black 20px, black calc(100% - 20px)`), so the fade stays exactly 20px regardless of the track's width and the maximum number of logos stays visible.

**Hero's own mobile section margin was doubling this gap**, also found and fixed 2026-08-26: `hero.section` carried `max-xl:mb-[72px]` (the standard mobile section-to-section gap), which stacked with Client Logos' own explicit `pt-[40px]` for 112px of total space above the title, not the 40px asked for. Since Client Logos is the only section that ever follows Hero, `hero.section`'s mobile margin was set to `mb-0` — Client Logos' own top padding is now the sole source of that gap. A future section that follows Hero on mobile and wants the *standard* 72px gap instead would supply it itself (the usual `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]` pattern), the same way every other section-to-section gap already works.

### Trust Signals (homepage section 5)

**Not `CapabilityCard ×4`** — that was a wireframe-era guess with no real design behind it, corrected 2026-08-24 after reading the real Figma frames (desktop `348:1862`, mobile `348:1880`). The real structure is a media block plus a text list, genuinely different per breakpoint, so it's its own section component (`components/sections/TrustSignals.tsx`), not a reuse of `CapabilityCard`.

- **Desktop**: media box on the left (fixed **400×296px**, not proportional to the text side), two side-by-side text sub-columns on the right — left column is Product Development then Low MOQ, right column is Private Label then Worldwide Shipping (confirmed via `get_metadata`: this is genuinely two separate stacked columns, not a single 4-item grid read row-major). 120px section top/bottom, 84px media-to-text gap (off-scale, kept exact, not rounded to a spacing token), 80px between the two sub-columns, 40px between the two stacked items in a column, 8px title-to-body. No divider between desktop items.
- **Mobile**: artwork on top (fixed **320×220px** — a different crop from the desktop box, not the same image scaled down), 24px gap, then all four items as **one divided list**, not a grid: item 1 has bottom padding + a bottom border, the two middle items have vertical padding + a bottom border, the last item has only top padding and no border — giving a uniform 48px gap between every pair with a single 1px divider at each gap's midpoint (confirmed via `get_design_context` on three separate item nodes, not guessed from a repeating pattern). Section top padding is **0px** (owner call, 2026-08-24) — the artwork sits flush against whatever precedes it, not offset by the standard section rhythm; bottom keeps the standard rhythm.
- **Media ratios**: neither box matches the app's existing `16:9` / `4:5` / `1:1` `MediaRatio` options — `4:5` would in fact be flatly wrong, since these boxes are landscape, not portrait. Two new exact ratios were added to `MediaPlaceholder`'s type and the `media.ratio` recipe map: `50:37` (400:296 reduced) and `16:11` (320:220 reduced), rather than rounding to an existing option.
