Both placeholders use `radius="none"` (owner call, 2026-08-24 — square corners, not the `lg` default).
- **No placeholder border, sitewide**: `media.placeholderLight` no longer carries a border at all (owner call, 2026-08-24) — this isn't a Trust Signals-only change, it corrects `MediaPlaceholder`'s shared recipe, so it applies to every section that uses the light-tone placeholder, not just this one.
- **Typography**: desktop title is 30px/500/normal leading, an exact match for the `H3` token. Desktop body is 20px/400 but with a **28px line-height** — not the Body Large token, which is 20px/400 with a 24px line-height — so it's its own one-off recipe value (`trustSignals.body`). Mobile title was 24px/500/normal, an exact match for `H5` — sized up to **30px** on mobile (owner call, 2026-08-26, sitewide mobile-title bump; `trustSignals.mobileTitle`, no responsive split needed since this key is only ever used in the mobile block). Mobile body is 18px/400 but with a **24px line-height** — not the Body token's 22px — another one-off (`trustSignals.mobileBody`). All four values confirmed via `get_design_context`, not estimated from box height.
- **Bold emphasis**: two of the four items (Low MOQ, Private Label) carry a real inline bold span in Figma's own copy. Since no other section needed rich body text before this, `content/home.ts`'s `trustStrip[].body` is now an array of segments (`string | { bold: string }`) instead of a plain string — a plain string becomes a one-element array, no rich-text system beyond that. `TrustSignals.tsx` renders it with a small local `Body`/`MobileBody` helper; `font-semibold` next to the body's own 400 weight is deliberate emphasis within the style, same convention as the offerings ticker label.
- **Copy**: Figma's real wording differs from the wireframe-era guess previously in `trustStrip` (e.g. "no middleman" singular, "0% EU import duty" not "0% EU duty") — updated to match exactly, per the standing rule that real design/copy wins. Figma's own stray "⚠️" authoring flags on two of the four bodies (Low MOQ, Worldwide Shipping) were treated as the designer's own "needs verification" marker, not real end-user copy, and left out of the displayed text; both items are marked `// VERIFY` in `content/home.ts` so they're easy to find again if the designer confirms different wording later.
- **Mobile artwork at tablet width, two passes (owner, 2026-09-03, tablet-width review)**: first report, "the image container looks big" — fixed like `TrustPoints`/`WhatWeCover`/`WhatWeMake`, capping width to phone size (`md:max-w-[420px] md:mx-auto`). Corrected the same day: "you make it very small, keep it as it was before maybe a little height can be shrink, not too small." Width cap reverted entirely (full tablet container width again, as before); the ratio itself widens instead, `md:aspect-[16/9]` (1.78:1) in place of the base `16:11` (1.45:1) only at `md:` — a modest ~18% height reduction at a given width, not the width cap's ~45% cut. `trustSignals.mobileMedia` is now just `"md:aspect-[16/9]"`, passed as `className` on the mobile `MediaPlaceholder`; a later `aspect-*` utility with a responsive prefix wins over the unprefixed base one at that breakpoint by generation order, same mechanism `hero.video`'s own `xl:h-[650px]` override relies on. Confirmed live: 960×540px at 1024px (was 960×660 before either fix, 420×289 mid-fix); unchanged (335×230, full `16:11`) at 375px real mobile.

### What We Make (homepage section 6)

Figma: desktop node `366:129`, mobile node `366:161` — both titled "Activewear Categories". Only that one category group's spacing is Figma-confirmed; `content/home.ts`'s second group (Teamwear & Uniforms) has no confirmed Figma frame yet, but `WhatWeMake.tsx` maps over every group in `whatWeMake.categories` generically, so it renders using the same confirmed pattern by inference — a reasonable, low-risk call for a repeating content shape, consistent with how repeated content elsewhere in this project doesn't need separate per-instance confirmation.

- **Section padding**: desktop is **120px top / 120px bottom** — the top value went through two owner corrections on 2026-08-23: the original Figma frame only captured the Activewear group in isolation (giving a wrong 60px/0px top reading), corrected first to 180px/100px, then revised down again the same day to 120px/64px. **Mobile is now `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]`**, the standard mobile section-to-section pattern (2026-08-24) — supersedes that 64px top.
- **Desktop**: `SectionHeading`, 90px gap (off-scale, kept exact) down to the category group(s). Group title 36px/500/normal + 8px gap + body 20px/400/28px-leading, constrained to **`max-w-620px`** so it wraps to Figma's real 2 lines instead of running the full column width (owner correction, 2026-08-23 — it initially rendered as 1 line, unconstrained) with one real bold span ("from sample to bulk"), then 40px gap down to a 4-column `Card` grid: 16px column gap, 48px row gap. **Between multiple category groups** (e.g. Activewear to Teamwear & Uniforms): **120px desktop / 64px mobile** (`whatWeMake.groupsGap`, owner call, 2026-08-23, same two-pass revision as the section padding above — distinct from the 90px/48px heading-to-first-group gap above). **The group title's mobile size ("Activewear," "Teamwear & Uniforms") was 24px, an exact `text-h5` match — sized up to 30px on mobile only** (owner call, 2026-08-26, sitewide mobile-title bump; `whatWeMake.groupTitle`, desktop's 36px unchanged). **Desktop product tiles' media is 0px radius** (`mediaRadius="none"`, owner call, 2026-08-26) — `Card` previously had no way to override this at all, defaulting to `lg`; mobile's own tiles already used `MediaPlaceholder`'s `radius="none"` directly and needed no change.
- **Mobile**: tiles are a **single stacked column, not a grid** — each is a landscape (16:11, the same ratio already added for Trust Signals) image, 16px gap, label **22px/500/normal, left-aligned, not centred**. Confirmed via `get_design_context`: desktop's tile label explicitly carries `text-center`, mobile's does not — different enough from `Card`'s contract (square + centred) that mobile uses its own bespoke markup instead of reusing `Card`, the same reasoning Trust Signals and Client Logos already used for their own mobile treatments. 40px gap between tiles, no divider. Media is already 0px radius here (`MediaPlaceholder`'s own `radius="none"`, set from this section's first build).
- **Tablet width, three passes (owner, 2026-09-03)**: first fix capped the tile itself (`md:max-w-[420px] md:mx-auto`, matching `TrustPoints`/`WhatWeCover`'s own artwork fix) — same defect, a single-column `w-full` tile with a fixed `16:11` image reading disproportionately tall through 1279px tablet width. Reverted the same day (owner, reviewing the Activewear/Teamwear PLP grids which reuse this same tile pattern: "images are totally broken, use 2 product tiles in each row adjust them to the page width") — a single column of narrow, centred tiles read as broken/empty, wasted width either side of a small floating tile. `whatWeMake.mobileList` gained `md:grid md:grid-cols-2 md:gap-x-6 md:gap-y-10` (was `flex flex-col gap-10` at every width below `xl:`) — a real 2-column grid fills the tablet width properly; `mobileTile`'s own width cap is gone, each tile now sizes to its grid cell and its fixed `16:11` image ratio scales correctly on its own. Third pass, same day (owner: "should not the images placeholder be tall, like desktop and mobile ... same width but a little taller") — mobile's flat `16:11` ratio read too short against the new, much wider 2-column tile; new `whatWeMake.mobileTileMedia` (`md:aspect-[4/3]`, passed as `className` on that `MediaPlaceholder`) is a real middle point between mobile's `16:11` (1.45:1) and desktop `Card`'s own square tile, taller without going all the way to square. Real mobile (`<md:`) keeps the single stacked column at the original `16:11`. Confirmed live: both category groups render 2 columns, 468×351px each, at 1024px; unchanged (single column, 335×230px) at 375px.
- **Real mobile only, 2026-09-07: each group's tile list capped to its first 4, plus a "View All" CTA to the category's landing page** (owner: "on the mobile homepage, add a cta after 4 products in what we make section 1 cta under activewear after 4 products, and cta for teamwear, it will take customers to their respective landing pages"). `category.href` (already on the content shape — `/activewear`, `/teamwear` — added earlier, unused until now) is finally wired in. Because a single CSS-reflowed list can't have a different tile *count* per breakpoint (only a different layout), the real-mobile tile list split into two real DOM instances, the same "two breakpoint-specific instances, not one repositioned via CSS" pattern already used elsewhere (e.g. `ProductGallery`): `whatWeMake.mobileListCapped` (`flex flex-col gap-10 md:hidden`, `.slice(0, 4)` in `WhatWeMake.tsx`) for real mobile, and `whatWeMake.mobileListFull` (`hidden md:grid md:grid-cols-2 md:gap-x-6 md:gap-y-10 xl:hidden`) for tablet — every tile, no CTA, pixel-identical to before this task. Activewear (7 tiles) now shows its first 4 on real mobile (Leggings, Sports Bras, Shorts, Hoodies); Teamwear & Uniforms (4 tiles) is unaffected in count, since it already had exactly 4.
  - **CTA styling, three same-day corrections**: `variant="secondary"` (`Button`, outline pill) so it doesn't compete with the page's real primary CTAs. Label is "View All {category.title}", except Teamwear & Uniforms shortens to "View All Teamwear" (owner: "for teamwear just use view all teamwear" — the group's own `<h3>` heading keeps the full title, only the CTA label shortens). Both CTAs ended up sharing an accent-coloured outline instead of `button.secondary`'s default `currentColor` one — first requested for Teamwear only ("make the outline and text orange as primary color"), then extended to Activewear too the same day ("make view all activewear also orange outline with text") — `whatWeMake.mobileGroupCta` carries `!border-accent !text-accent hover:!bg-[#FFF6F3]` (the `!` needed since `button.secondary`'s own `border-current`/`text-current` is the same specificity — the identical fix already applied once to `productCtas.secondaryDesktop`, reusing its exact hover tint rather than inventing a second one).
  - **Spacing, one real bug found and fixed live**: owner's numbers were first "space from the top should be 40px," then corrected same day to "32px." A first attempt used `mt-8` (32px) directly — live measurement showed a 56px gap, not 32, because `whatWeMake.group`'s own `gap-6` (24px) flex gap already applies between every child in that column, the CTA included; a margin on a flex child *adds* to that shared gap rather than replacing it (the same mechanism already documented on `productCtas.desktopRow`'s own `xl:mt-2`). Corrected to `mt-2` (8px), so `8 + 24 = 32px` total, confirmed live.
  - Confirmed live: real mobile (375px) shows exactly 4 tiles per group then the CTA, 32px below the last tile, both orange-outlined, linking to `/activewear`/`/teamwear`; tablet (1024px) shows every tile (7/4) in the 2-column grid with no CTA, unaffected; desktop (1440px) `Card` grid unaffected at the time (7 tiles) — see the desktop follow-up directly below, same day.
- **Same day, follow-up: desktop grid also gained a CTA, in the last cell of each group's own row set** (owner: "on the desktop, for activewear, shall we use the last 8th box space and put a cta there?", then, once asked how to keep Teamwear consistent (it only has 4 tiles/1 full row, no empty cell): "the pattern should be consistent for both... use the same cta labels as mobile"). Rule, computed generically rather than as two hardcoded numbers: `rows = Math.ceil(tiles.length / 4)`, then show `tiles.slice(0, rows * 4 - 1)` — the same number of rows the tiles already need, with the CTA reserving the last cell. Activewear (7 tiles): `rows = 2`, all 7 tiles keep showing, CTA is the 8th cell (no trim). Teamwear & Uniforms (4 tiles): `rows = 1`, trims to its first 3 (Soccer, Basketball, Football Uniforms — "Fighting Wear" no longer shows on desktop only), CTA is the 4th cell, completing that one row the same way Activewear's completes its second. Mobile/tablet read `category.tiles`/`category.tiles.slice(0, 4)` directly, both unaffected — "Fighting Wear" still shows there.

  The CTA tile itself is a plain `<Link>` (`whatWeMake.desktopGridCta`), not `Card` (expects an image+label pair) or `Button` (a pill sized to its own text, not a grid cell) — a bordered box at the exact same `aspect-[15/16]` footprint as `desktopTileMedia`, so it's the same size as every photo tile beside it, with `text-h5` (matching `card.label`'s own desktop size) centred inside, `border-accent`/`text-accent` for the orange outline+text, and a lighter `hover:bg-accent/5` fill (accent orange reads much stronger than a neutral tint at the same opacity, so this is scaled down from `button.secondary`'s own `hover:bg-current/10`). Labels reuse the exact same `ctaLabel()` helper the mobile CTA already uses (factored out of `WhatWeMake.tsx`'s mobile block into a shared function, rather than duplicating the "Teamwear & Uniforms" → "Teamwear" ternary a second time) — "View All Activewear" / "View All Teamwear", same copy as mobile, per the owner's own instruction.

  Confirmed live: 1440px — Activewear's grid renders 7 `Card`s + 1 CTA tile (2 full rows), Teamwear & Uniforms renders 3 `Card`s + 1 CTA tile (1 full row), both CTA tiles measuring the same `308×328.5px` as their neighbouring photo tiles, both linking correctly (`/activewear`, `/teamwear`); 1024px tablet and 375px mobile both pixel-identical to before this follow-up (Teamwear still shows all 4 tiles including Fighting Wear at both). Full 30-viewport screenshot suite passing.

  **Trailing chevron, same day, both CTAs (desktop grid tile and mobile pill)**: owner, "put a chvron next to the cta label," then "on the hover chvron should have the same animation we applied on pdp, related styles," then a size/spacing correction, "chvron icon should be a little big, text to icon gap should be 8px." Reuses `productRelatedStyles.chip`/`chipIcon`'s own established `group` + trailing-icon + `group-hover:translate-x-0.5` hover-nudge shape (lucide's `ChevronRight`, `aria-hidden`) rather than inventing a second hover pattern — but with this CTA's own tuned size/gap, not that chip's values copied verbatim: new shared `whatWeMake.ctaIcon` token (initially `size-4`, 16×16px — one step up from the chip's own 14px `size-3.5`, per "a little big"), and both `desktopGridCta`/`mobileGroupCta` gained `group gap-2` (8px, up from an initial `gap-1`/4px). Confirmed live: both icons measured 16×16px with an 8px gap to their label text.

  **Two further corrections, same day**: owner: "chevron size make 7by16 px. text to ion gap 8px. make the view all activewear and teamwear 20px." `ctaIcon`'s square `size-4` became `w-[7px] h-4` — `ChevronRight` is a square glyph by default, so this deliberately renders it taller and narrower, not a token this project has elsewhere. Gap was already 8px, unchanged. Label size: `desktopGridCta` dropped `text-h5` (24px, `card.label`'s own size) for a literal `text-[1.25rem] leading-[1.2] font-medium` (20px, same weight/leading `text-h5` had, only the size number changed); `mobileGroupCta` gained `!text-[1.25rem]` overriding `Button`'s own `base` (`text-button`, 18px) — `!` needed since both are same-specificity utilities on the same property, the same fix already applied once to `productCtas.mobileButton`'s own `!text-[1rem]`. Confirmed live at both breakpoints: 20px label, 8px gap, 7×16px chevron.

  **Desktop icon swapped from `ChevronRight` to `NextArrowIcon`, same day (owner: "desktop chevron is very small make it same size as servies product range 'explore activewear' cta")**: matching the numeric size alone (`w-[7px] h-4` was already ~7×16px) wasn't the real fix — lucide's `ChevronRight` is thin and stroke-based on a 24×24 native viewBox, reading much lighter than `NextArrowIcon` (`components/icons/NextArrowIcon.tsx`, a small SOLID chevron with no stroke at all, purpose-built at exactly `7×16.6667`, first built for `ProductRange`'s own "Explore X" links — see that component's own header comment: "a lucide substitute would read as visibly different weight/style"). New `whatWeMake.desktopCtaIcon` copies `productRange.exploreIcon`'s size verbatim, so this genuinely is the same icon at the same size, not a lookalike. Mobile keeps `ctaIcon`/`ChevronRight`, unchanged — the owner's report was specifically "desktop." Confirmed live: both `WhatWeMake`'s desktop CTA icon and `/services`' own "Explore Activewear" icon measure identical `7×16.66px`.

  **Chevron vertical centring, same day (owner: "the chvron is not 100% center aligned to the text cta labe, make it fully centrealigned")**: not a flexbox bug — `desktopGridCta`'s own `items-center` was already correct. The real cause: `NextArrowIcon`'s drawn path isn't centred within its own `0 0 7 16.6667` viewBox — the path's own y-range is `4`–`15.6667` (visual centre ≈`9.83`), not the viewBox's true centre (`8.33`), a built-in ~1.5px offset toward the bottom baked into the asset itself. Flex-centering the icon's *box* therefore still left the visible glyph reading low. First fixed here with a compensating `desktopCtaIcon` `-translate-y-[1.5px]` nudge — **superseded the same day** once the identical report came in against `ProductRange`'s own "Explore Activewear" link (this icon's first, shared home): the root cause was fixed at the source instead (`NextArrowIcon.tsx`'s viewBox cropped to the glyph's real bounds, see that component's own header comment and `ProductRange`'s own entry below), so the manual nudge here was removed and `desktopCtaIcon`'s height corrected to `h-[11.667px]` to match — stacking the old translate on top of the now-fixed icon would have pushed it off-centre the other way.

  **Tablet now follows the same capped+CTA behaviour as real mobile, same day (owner: "tablet will also follow mobile cta behavior")** — the earlier "5eo" split (real mobile capped to 4 tiles + CTA; tablet showing every tile with no CTA, a deliberate scope line at the time) is reversed: `mobileListCapped`/`mobileListFull` merged back into one `whatWeMake.mobileList` token (`flex flex-col gap-10 md:grid md:grid-cols-2 md:gap-x-6 md:gap-y-10 xl:hidden` — same responsive layout as before, just visible/capped through the whole sub-`xl` range instead of splitting at `md:`), and `mobileGroupCta`'s own `md:hidden` moved to `xl:hidden` to match. `WhatWeMake.tsx` now renders one list (`.slice(0, MOBILE_TILE_LIMIT)`) + one CTA for both real mobile and tablet, not two separate DOM instances. Desktop's own `xl:`-only "CTA fills the last grid cell" mechanic is untouched. Confirmed live at 1024px: both groups now show exactly 4 tiles (in the unchanged 2-column grid) then the CTA; 375px real mobile pixel-identical to before.

  **Tablet-only extra top gap above the CTA, same day (owner: "on the tablet, add extra 16px space from top of the ctas")** — `mobileGroupCta` gained `md:mt-6` alongside its existing `mt-2`. `group`'s own `gap-6` (24px, unprefixed, applies to both real mobile and tablet) already supplies part of the gap above this button; `mt-2` (8px) brings real mobile to 32px total, unchanged, while `md:mt-6` (24px) brings tablet to 48px — 16px more than mobile, not a second unrelated value. Confirmed live: 48px at 1024px, unchanged 32px at 375px.

  **CTA label typography corrected to match the site's real primary-CTA text, same day (owner: "the cta label should be upper case as we use in our primary cta, 18px and 24 line height")** — the site's actual primary-CTA typography is `button.base`'s own `text-button uppercase` (`app/globals.css`: 18px/700 weight/1.3333 line-height = 24px). Desktop's `desktopGridCta` dropped its custom `text-[1.25rem] leading-[1.2] font-medium` (20px/1.2/500, no uppercase, added the same day for an earlier "make it 20px" request that's now superseded) for `text-button uppercase` directly. Mobile's `mobileGroupCta` had a `!text-[1.25rem]` override fighting `Button`'s own correct default down to 20px — removed entirely rather than replaced, since `Button`'s untouched `base` already supplies the exact spec asked for. Confirmed live at all three breakpoints: 18px, uppercase, 24px line-height.
- **A real cascade bug, caught and fixed here and retroactively in Hero**: writing a heading's mobile values as unprefixed utilities plus an `xl:`-prefixed override for the desktop/token value (e.g. `text-[1.875rem] font-[460] leading-[34px] xl:text-h1`) is not safe — the unprefixed classes can keep winning the cascade at desktop widths too, since Tailwind doesn't guarantee a bare utility loses to a same-specificity `xl:` one just because it comes later in a class string. Confirmed live: `sectionHeading.heading`'s mobile weight/line-height were still rendering at 1440px until fixed. The fix is `max-xl:`/`xl:` as mutually exclusive media conditions instead (`max-xl:text-[1.875rem] max-xl:font-[460] max-xl:leading-[34px] xl:text-h1`) — applied to every recipe with this shape, including `hero.eyebrowSize`, which had carried the identical pattern since Hero shipped and only "worked" by coincidence (see `SectionHeading`'s entry above for the second bug this same code had — a dropped font-weight).
- **`SectionHeading`, `Card`, `MediaPlaceholder`'s placeholder border**: all corrected here on first real use — see their own entries above for details.

### Certified & Compliant (homepage section 7)

Figma: desktop node `369:266`, mobile node `375:435`. `content/home.ts`'s existing `certified` entry (eyebrow, h2, 6 certs) already matched exactly, confirming this was the right section; its `badges` string array was replaced with a `logos` registry (same shape as `brandLogos`: `src`/`width`/`height`/`mobileWidth`/`mobileHeight` per cert) once real assets existed, and its h2 copy got a punctuation correction to match Figma exactly ("...environment, and ethics").

- **Section padding**: provisional, **120px top/bottom desktop** — the same caveat as What We Make: neither Figma frame's own top/bottom edges reliably reflect the section's real page padding, so this reuses the latest confirmed sitewide rhythm rather than trusting this frame's raw edges. **Mobile is `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]`**, the standard mobile section-to-section pattern (2026-08-24) — supersedes an earlier same-day 80px top.
- **Heading is `SectionHeading`'s new centred variant** (`align="center"`) — confirmed genuinely centred here (both eyebrow and H2), unlike Trust Signals/What We Make's left alignment. See `SectionHeading`'s own entry above.
- **Desktop**: a **single static row, not a ticker** (owner instruction) — no border, no background, bare logo marks, each at its own real size (five at 135×135, BSCI at 156×134, not normalised to a shared box). **72px gap from the heading** (`certified.root`, off-scale, kept exact); **69px gap between logos**, confirmed consistently across all 5 gaps via `get_metadata` coordinates.
- **Mobile heading-to-logo gap is a separate, smaller value: 32px** (`certified.rootMobile`, owner call, 2026-08-24) — not the desktop's 72px reused, since the two are genuinely different and mobile has its own recipe key rather than a responsive override on one shared class.
- **Mobile**: the same bordered grid `ClientLogos` already established (`clientLogos.mobileGrid`/`mobileItem`, reused here as `certified.mobileGrid`/`mobileItem`), now with **rounded 4px corners on both** (owner instruction) — Figma confirms this per-cell (each true-corner cell carries a single-corner 4px radius, the middle-row cells carry none). The first implementation replicated that literally as an edge-ownership split (wrapper owns top+left, each cell owns right+bottom) clipped by a rounded `overflow-hidden` wrapper — this produced a visibly jagged, stair-stepped corner, not a smooth curve, since clipping a square-cornered border with a curved mask just cuts it off at an angle rather than rounding it (caught from an owner screenshot of ISO 9001's corner). **Fixed 2026-08-24** by moving the entire outer border onto `mobileGrid` itself as one border (native smooth radius, since the border and its radius live on the same element) and having cells draw only the internal dividers between them (`mobileItemDividerRight`/`mobileItemDividerBottom`, computed per cell in `CertifiedCompliant.tsx`) — see `ClientLogos`' own entry above for the full technique, since both sections now share it identically.
- **Real logos exported per-node from Figma** (each of the 6 "Logo" frames under node `369:266`), matching the same per-node-export convention `brandLogos` established — the composited export already includes each mark's own padding, so it's used at its own exact size, not re-cropped.
- **Tablet width, a real third breakpoint tier (owner, 2026-09-03, two-part correction)** — first pass moved `desktopSection`/`mobileSection`'s split from `xl:` to `md:` ("certified, use desktop style"), same as Hero's own ticker: the mobile bordered grid became real-mobile-only, the bare single-row desktop treatment took over at 768px+. Corrected the same day once the row's own `flex-wrap` genuinely wrapped to 2+ lines at tablet width (6 logos + 5×69px gaps need more room than 768–1279px has): "certificates, make them in one line and add marquie just for these viewports." `desktopSection` reverted to `xl:` only (the true, unwrapped static row); a new third block, `tabletSection` (`hidden md:block xl:hidden`), renders a scrolling `Marquee` of the same logos instead — reusing Client Logos' own desktop technique (`separator="none"`, images as items, `edgeFade`, `pauseOnHover={false}`) rather than inventing a new mechanism. Confirmed live: only the tablet `Marquee` block renders at 1024px (both other blocks hidden); the static row is back at 1440px; the bordered grid is unchanged at 375px real mobile. **Top/bottom padding trimmed the same day** (owner: "reduce the space from top and bottom of the section, extra space atm") — `tabletSection`'s `pt`/`pb` cut from the static row's own 120px to 80px, a judgment call (no tablet Figma frame exists), roughly the midpoint between that 120px and mobile's 72px; confirmed 80px at 1024px, 120px unchanged at 1440px. **Two further trims, 2026-09-04** (owner, reviewing this tablet block again: "certified section title and logo space reduce space to 24px. and reduce 24px from the bottom if the logos") — `certified.root`'s heading-to-logo-row gap, `gap-[72px]` (the confirmed real desktop value, shared by both the `xl:`-only static row and this `md:`-only tablet block, since the two are mutually-exclusive-by-visibility DOM instances), is now `md:gap-6 xl:gap-[72px]` — 24px at tablet, 72px unchanged at desktop. `tabletSection`'s own `pb` cut a further 24px, 80px → 56px (`pt` untouched, not flagged). Confirmed live at 1024px: 24px heading-to-logo gap, 80px top / 56px bottom section padding; unchanged (72px gap, 120px/120px padding) at 1440px `xl:` desktop; real mobile (375px) unaffected.
- **A real, reproducible lazy-load bug, found and fixed here**: this section sits far enough down the page that `next/image`'s default `loading="lazy"` did not reliably fire even after the section was scrolled fully into view (confirmed: `naturalWidth` stayed `0`) — unlike every other real image on the page so far, none of which sit this far down, and unlike the same section's own text content, which rendered fine. Fixed with `priority` on all 6 logos at both breakpoints, trading a small amount of eager-load cost for deterministic rendering in the Playwright screenshot QA gate, which is the actual thing that broke first (a `fullPage` screenshot silently omitted the logos with no console error).
- **A second real bug, on mobile only**: Tailwind's own preflight sets `img { height: auto }`, which silently overrode `next/image`'s intended height for any mobile logo whose crop has a different aspect ratio than its source file (confirmed live: BSCI's mobile tile rendered 119×102 — scaled by the source file's own 156:134 ratio — instead of its real 119×43). Desktop never hit this, since every desktop size matches its source file's own intrinsic ratio exactly by construction. Fixed with an explicit inline `style` width/height on the mobile `<Image>`, which always wins over any stylesheet rule regardless of specificity — the reliable fix, not a class-ordering guess.
- **A third bug, one level deeper (2026-08-24)**: even with the box's own dimensions fixed, BSCI and IMAC still looked visibly stretched — both were displaying the **desktop-cropped file** at the **mobile box's different aspect ratio** (BSCI's real crop is 156:134 on desktop but 119:43 on mobile). `certified.logos` gained a `mobileSrc` per entry — a genuinely separate export, downloaded per-node from Figma's mobile frame (node `375:435`'s own per-cell image nodes), not the desktop file reused smaller. Every logo got its own dedicated mobile export for consistency, not just the two that visibly showed the distortion.
- **A third bug**: `certified.mobileGridWrap` initially had no `w-full`, so it shrank to its own content width under `root`'s `items-center` (a flex-column child doesn't stretch to fill by default) — the grid rendered centred with a 43px inset from the section edges instead of the intended 20px `container-p` inset every other section uses. Fixed by adding `w-full`.
- **Desktop heading wraps to Figma's real 2 lines**: the real heading column is 812px wide (node `369:267`); unconstrained, the H2 ran the full container width and wrapped to only 1 line. `SectionHeading` gained a `headingClassName` prop (passed `certified.headingNarrow`, `max-w-[812px]`) for this — desktop instance only, since mobile's own narrower column already wraps correctly without it.

### Stats (homepage section 8)

Figma: desktop node `387:498`, mobile node `387:511`. **Not `StatBlock`** (3 stats side by side, already built) — the real layout is media plus a stacked text list, structurally like Trust Signals, and this section's own typography doesn't match `StatBlock`'s Stat Number/Stat Label tokens either, so it's its own bespoke section. The first dark section since Hero (`#131314`, matching `ink-2`).

- **Section padding**: **120px top/bottom desktop**, confirmed from the frame's own geometry (the image height plus symmetric 120px margins accounts for the whole frame height exactly — unlike the unreliable frame-crop reading that bit What We Make and Certified & Compliant). **Mobile follows the standing dark-section rule** (established on Hero, 2026-08-24, applies to every current and future full-bleed coloured section): a fixed **48px** inset from the box's own top/bottom edges, and the standard **72px** section-to-section gap as a `margin` on the section itself, not padding inside it.
- **Desktop**: media block left (660×660-ish, its own confirmed `33:31` ratio — close to square but not quite, kept exact rather than rounded to `1:1`), 3 stats stacked right in a **fixed 300px column** (`stats.desktopList`, not `flex-1` — corrected 2026-08-24, it had been stretching to fill whatever space was left after the media block, running captions far wider than Figma's real column), **130px gap** between media and text (corrected the same day from an incorrect 84px, mistakenly copied from Trust Signals' own unrelated gap value), 48px gap between stats, no divider. The whole 1090px media+text group is **centred within the standard 1280px content area** (`mx-auto w-fit` on `desktopInner`), not flush against the left edge — confirmed via `get_metadata` (a symmetric 95px margin on both sides beyond `container-p`'s own 80px padding). Value **54px/400 (regular)/64px leading** — the size matches `text-h1` exactly but the weight doesn't (H1 is 500), so it's its own one-off. Caption **20px/400/28px leading, colour `#838d97`** — the same muted-on-dark hex already confirmed independently for Hero's mobile ticker items, now a second real confirmation, not a coincidence.
- **The dark background is on a separate, unconstrained outer wrapper** (`stats.desktopOuter`), not on the same element as `container-p` — corrected 2026-08-24: `container-p` caps at `max-width: 1440px`, so putting `bg-ink` on that same element meant the black fill itself stopped at 1440px on any wider viewport, showing the page's default background outside it instead of true edge-to-edge black. `container-p` now lives on an inner content wrapper inside the unconstrained outer one — the same split Hero already used (background on the outer `<section>`, `container-p` on an inner wrapper), which Stats just hadn't followed at first.
- **Mobile**: artwork on top (16:11, the same ratio already established for Trust Signals/What We Make), 24px gap, then a **divided list** (border between each pair, none after the last — the same pattern already used in Trust Signals and What We Make), 24px vertical padding per item, divider colour `#2a2e33` (close to but not identical to `border-line-dark`, kept as its own confirmed value). Value is **30px/500 (medium)/normal leading** (`stats.mobileValue`) — Figma's own frame reads 24px, an exact `text-h5` match, but the owner asked to size it up to 30px (2026-08-24); weight and leading kept from the Figma match, size only changed, so it's now its own one-off rather than the token. Caption **18px/400/24px leading, `#838d97`** — same colour as desktop, confirmed separately on the mobile frame.
- **Copy**: Figma's own two frames disagreed on the second stat — desktop says "75,000 sq ft", mobile says "50,000 sq ft" (already flagged unresolved in `content/home.ts` before this section was built). Owner resolved 2026-08-24: follow desktop on both breakpoints. Captions also updated from generic wireframe-era placeholders to Figma's real, specific copy.

### Inside the Factory (homepage section 9)

Figma: desktop node `402:904`, mobile node `402:914`. The third dark section (Hero, Stats, now this) — same background-bleed split as those two (`bg-ink` on an unconstrained outer wrapper, `container-p` only on an inner content wrapper). `content/home.ts`'s `insideFactory.body` was dropped: the real Figma frame is a centred heading plus an image gallery only, no body copy — the same wireframe-era-guess story that hit Trust Signals' first pass. A CTA was later added on top of the Figma frame (owner request, 2026-08-26, not a Figma-sourced element): `insideFactory.cta` (`{ label: "Explore Our Factory", href: "/our-factory" }`), a plain `Button` (`variant="primary"`, the default) centred under the gallery at both breakpoints, 48px below it (`desktopCtaWrap`/`mobileCtaWrap`, `pt-12`/`mt-12`) — the same gap value already used above the gallery (`desktopGalleryWrap`'s 72px top and `mobileGalleryGap`'s 48px), kept inside `container-p` on mobile since the full-bleed gallery itself is not.

- **Heading is `SectionHeading`** (`align="center"`, `eyebrowTone="dark"`) at both breakpoints — corrected 2026-08-24: it was bespoke markup at first, to avoid a colour-class conflict with a section-specific eyebrow hex, but that reason disappeared once the sitewide eyebrow-colour rule existed (`dark` tone already equals the exact colour this section needs, so no override, no conflict). Desktop heading wraps to Figma's real **2 lines**: the real column is 812px wide (node `402:906`, the same value already confirmed for Certified & Compliant), passed via `headingClassName`; unconstrained it ran to 1 line.
- **Desktop: a chevron-driven, snap-centred carousel through all 5 shots** (owner request, 2026-08-26 — replaces the earlier static 3-tile row, which only ever showed the first 3 of the 5 real factory shots, the rest reachable only on mobile). Uses the shared `useDesktopChevronScroller` hook (see `DesktopChevronScroller` above for the mechanism) — `insideFactory.desktopScrollerWrap` supplies this section's own layout, the chevron circle itself is the shared `chevronScroller` recipe. Two real differences from How It Works: cards use `snap-center` (not `snap-start` — the point here is the active card centres itself, not aligns to an edge), and the track's own horizontal padding is calculated as `calc(50%-475px)` (475 = half the 950px card width) rather than a fixed inset, so the first and last card can still scroll to true centre at any viewport width, with matching `scroll-pl`/`scroll-pr` so scroll-snap's own snap-point maths treats that padding as safe space (the same class of bug already fixed once on How It Works' fixed 80px padding). Click pages exactly one card (`950 + 48`, this section's own card pitch).
- **Cards are now uniform (950×550, `19:11`), not narrow/wide** — dropped the old two-size narrow-wide-narrow split (195px sides / 950px centre, two different `MediaPlaceholder` ratios). A chevron-paged, snap-centred carousel needs one consistent card pitch to page by; How It Works' cards are all one size for the same reason. This is a deliberate trade of the old static row's narrow-wide-narrow identity for "one full image centred, neighbours peeking" — what the interaction is meant to read as.
- The gallery is still a **full-bleed sibling of the heading, not nested inside its `container-p`-padded wrapper** — confirmed via `get_metadata`: the real gallery spans the frame's full width (x=0 to 1440), unlike the heading above it, which does use the standard 80px inset. Section padding: provisional 120px top (this frame's own top edge reads 60px, the same unreliable frame-crop pattern that bit What We Make/Certified & Compliant/Stats' initial read).
- **Mobile**: a **finger-swipeable carousel via native CSS scroll-snap**, not custom drag-tracking JS or an auto-rotating timer — confirmed from the owner's own animation reference (a Shopify marketing page, saved to `reference/`): they were swiping it by hand, the recording just didn't show the finger. `overflow-x-auto` + `snap-x snap-mandatory` on the track, `snap-center` on each 300px card, `shrink-0`. Cards touch with **zero gap** — confirmed via `get_metadata`: a peek sliver's edge exactly equals the adjacent card's edge. Eyebrow is **centred** (owner correction, 2026-08-24; `SectionHeading`'s `align="center"` already handles this). Gallery is **full-bleed edge to edge** on mobile too, same as desktop, unlike every other section's mobile content. A `.no-scrollbar` utility (new, `app/globals.css`) hides the native scrollbar while leaving touch/drag scrolling fully working — there's no Tailwind utility for this across all three browser engines. Standing dark-section mobile rule applies (48px internal inset, 72px external margin) — this frame's own raw edges happen to already read 48px/48px, matching the rule for once.
- **The active (centred) card renders taller than its neighbours — a real overlap effect, not a flat filmstrip** (owner correction, 2026-08-24, after checking the real Figma geometry more closely: `get_metadata` shows the active card at 300×340 and the flanking cards at a shorter 300×248, both vertically centred). Since every card becomes "active" as the user swipes through in turn, this can't be pre-baked into static CSS: `InsideFactory.tsx` is the project's **first client component** (`"use client"`). Height is **continuous per-frame interpolation**, not a two-state swap: each card's height is `lerp(340, 248, distanceFromCentre)`, computed every `scroll` event (throttled with `requestAnimationFrame`) and written straight to each card's DOM `style.height` via refs, not React state, so a 60fps drag never waits on a re-render — a two-state `Math.round(scrollLeft / cardWidth)` swap was tried first but popped visibly at the rounding threshold. `MediaPlaceholder` gained an optional `style` prop for this. The track itself (`mobileTrack`) is pinned to a fixed `h-[340px]` — without it, the track's own intrinsic height reflows every frame as children resize, which reads as the whole section's background moving during the swipe (a real regression, found and fixed 2026-08-24).
- **Side padding is capped at 40px, not a pure `(100% - 300px) / 2` percentage formula** (corrected 2026-08-25, owner report: layout broken on a large mobile viewport, e.g. Samsung Fold-class devices, not Fold-specific — reproduced at any "mobile" width around 700-1024px, since this carousel is `xl:hidden` and so also renders at tablet-range widths). The pure formula produces a huge inset at those widths (e.g. 250px at 800px wide) — wide enough that a second full card becomes visible alongside the first, breaking the intended one-active-card-with-peeking-neighbours read into what looks like a broken 2-up grid. `px-[min(40px,calc((100%-300px)/2))]` caps the inset at the design's own confirmed ~40px peek at any width, while still producing true centring below ~380px wide (where the formula's own value is already smaller than 40px).
- **Desktop gallery now shows from `md:` (768px), a real tablet-only layout, not the desktop card reused verbatim (owner, 2026-09-03, three-part correction)**: first pass moved `desktopOuter`/`mobileSection` from `xl:` to `md:` (owner: "Inside the factory should also use desktop version"), same split already applied elsewhere on the homepage. This alone was broken: the desktop card's own centering padding, `calc(50% - 475px)`, goes negative below a 950px viewport and is silently clamped to 0px (padding can't be negative), so the first card rendered flush against the left edge at 768px, no centering/peek at all — confirmed live. A second attempt kept the centered-snap technique but shrank the card (600px, half 300) so the calc stayed positive; corrected the same day (owner: "image placeholder should have same gap from the left as other sections have as default state, image size should be reasonable, currently too wide, in the default state, 2nd image peek should be visible so customer knows its scrollable") — centering itself was wrong here, not just the width: it puts equal padding on both sides (matching nothing else on the page) and doesn't reliably show a second-card peek at rest. Final version: a genuinely different tablet layout, not a scaled desktop one — flush-left, starting at `container-p`'s own standard `md:` inset (32px, `desktopRow`'s `px-8`, matching every other section's left edge) with `snap-start` (aligns to each card's own left edge, not centre) and a smaller `TABLET_CARD_WIDTH` (initially 520×301, `19:11`, InsideFactory.tsx). `xl:` (1280px+) keeps the original centered-snap `950×550` card and `calc(50%-475px)` padding, fully unchanged. The chevron's own click-to-scroll pitch is now responsive too (`cardPitch` state via the same `matchMedia("(min-width: 1280px)")` pattern `ProductGrid.tsx` already uses for its page size), so a chevron click scrolls the correct distance for whichever card size is actually rendered. **Card size corrected twice more, same day**: first "make the image size a little big not too bog" (`TABLET_CARD_WIDTH` 520 → 560×324, a middle point between that and the earlier rejected 600); then, once Exhibitions got this same tablet treatment (see below), "i like the exhibition image size and it has gap from the left, use the same for factory" — `TABLET_CARD_WIDTH` settled at Exhibitions' own confirmed `469px`, matching across both sections rather than each carrying its own separate number.
- **One more gap, same day: width matched but the image still read a different SIZE** — owner: "Inside the facoty image container size and behavior still look different than the exhibition contianer, make the foctory the same as exhibition." Width was equal (469px) but height wasn't: this section's own real `19:11` ratio gives 271px tall, Exhibitions' `469:320` gives 320px. New `insideFactory.desktopCardMedia` (`md:aspect-[469/320] xl:aspect-[19/11]`, passed as `className` on the tablet-tier `MediaPlaceholder`) overrides the ratio at `md:` to match Exhibitions exactly, while explicitly restoring this section's own real ratio at `xl:`. **A real bug caught before shipping**: a first version omitted the `xl:` half of that pair, on the wrong assumption that a `md:` utility only applies within the 768-1279px range — `md:` is a min-width breakpoint, so it stays active at every wider screen too (including `xl:`) unless overridden there; the real desktop card briefly rendered 950×648 instead of 950×550 until `xl:aspect-[19/11]` was added back. Confirmed live at rest (`scrollLeft: 0`): both sections now measure pixel-identical 469×320px at 768px and 1024px (card 2 at x=525 for both); `xl:` desktop (1440px) confirmed back to 950×550 (ratio 1.727, `19:11`); real mobile (<768px) still gets its own separate swipeable carousel, unaffected.
- **Same structure applied to Exhibitions (owner, 2026-09-03: "apply the same structure to exhibition")** — see that section's own entry below for the details; `Exhibitions.tsx` already used a flush-left `snap-start` scroller (copied from How It Works, not Inside the Factory's centered technique), so only its breakpoint (`xl:` → `md:`) and left padding (a fixed `px-[80px]`, correct only at `xl:`, needed a `md:px-8` tier matching `container-p`'s own tablet inset) needed to change — no card-size or chevron-pitch changes needed there, since its card width doesn't differ by breakpoint.

### Exhibitions (new homepage section, directly after Inside the Factory)

Figma: desktop node `455:2375`, mobile node `455:2387`. `components/sections/Exhibitions.tsx`. Not in the original wireframe numbering — added on top of the built homepage, placed right after Inside the Factory (owner call, 2026-08-26, corrected from an initial placement after How It Works). Owner instruction: desktop behaves exactly like How It Works' chevron scroller, mobile exactly like Inside the Factory's swipeable carousel — confirmed as a real structural match, not just a vibe: the mobile card is `300×340/248`, identical to Inside the Factory's own constants, and the desktop row's Figma-confirmed `469px` cards overflow the 1440px frame (the third of the three real "Step" frames lands past the frame's own right edge) exactly the way How It Works' 5-card row does, so both are genuinely scrollable rows dressed as a static Figma layout, not fully-visible static grids. Reuses those two proven mechanisms directly (own `DesktopScroller`/`MobileCarousel`, mirroring the two source files) rather than inventing a third variant.

- **Desktop**: `bg-ink` full-bleed outer (`desktopOuter`), `container-p` heading wrapper (`pt-[60px]`, this section's own confirmed top value, genuinely different from the usual 120px), then a full-bleed gallery wrap (`pt-[72px] pb-[120px]`) — same split as Inside the Factory. Row/card layout is How It Works' own shape verbatim (`snap-start`, `px-[80px]`/`scroll-pl-[80px]`/`scroll-pr-[80px]`) — **not** Inside the Factory's own now-center-snap variant, since the owner asked specifically for How It Works' behaviour — and the chevron itself is the shared `useDesktopChevronScroller` hook/`chevronScroller` recipe (see `DesktopChevronScroller` above). Cards are `469×320` (`MediaPlaceholder` ratio `469:320`, a new one-off added to `media.ratio`/`MediaRatio`, the same "exact fraction, not rounded" pattern as Trust Signals' `50:37`/`16:11`), `gap-6` (24px, which happens to equal How It Works' own gap).
- **Mobile**: Inside the Factory's carousel numbers reused verbatim — `300px` cards, `340px` active / `248px` inactive height (the same `lerp`-on-scroll, direct-DOM-write technique, no React re-render per frame), `min(40px, calc((100%-300px)/2))` side padding, `snap-center`. `mt-8` (32px) gap from heading to gallery, matching this section's own confirmed value.
- **Desktop scroller now shows from `md:` (768px), not `xl:` (owner, 2026-09-03: "apply the same structure to exhibition" — Inside the Factory's own tablet-width fix, see that section's own entry above)** — this section's own `snap-start`/`px-[80px]` scroller is already the same flush-left shape Inside the Factory's tablet tier needed to build from scratch, so no card-size or chevron-pitch change was needed here, only the breakpoint and the padding: `desktopOuter`/`mobileSection` moved from `xl:` to `md:`; `desktopRow`'s fixed `px-[80px]`/`scroll-pl-[80px]`/`scroll-pr-[80px]` (correct only at `xl:`, where `container-p`'s own inset is 80px) gained an unprefixed `px-8`/`scroll-pl-8`/`scroll-pr-8` base (32px, matching `container-p`'s `md:` inset) with the original `80px` values now `xl:`-scoped. Confirmed live: 32px left inset with a real second-card peek at both 768px (card 2 at x=525) and 1024px; 80px inset unchanged at 1440px `xl:` desktop; real mobile (<768px) unaffected.
- **Heading**: `SectionHeading`, `align="center"`, `eyebrowTone="dark"`. Desktop eyebrow needs no size override — Figma shows 20px/600 (semibold), which is Overline's own default, unlike Inside the Factory's confirmed 20px/500 override. Mobile eyebrow is 16px/600 (`mobileEyebrowSize`), same shape as Inside the Factory's own override. Heading forced to `max-w-[812px]` — the same value now confirmed a fourth time (Certified & Compliant, Inside the Factory, How It Works, this).
- **No CTA** in either Figma frame, unlike Inside the Factory — the section ends with the gallery.
- **5 media placeholders, not the Figma-confirmed 3** (owner call, 2026-08-26) — `content/home.ts`'s `exhibitions.media` carries 5 entries so the carousel has enough real content to page through before final exhibition photography is confirmed. The section itself doesn't assume a fixed count either way; the array can be trimmed back to 3 (or replaced) with no code change.

### Final CTA + compliance ticker (homepage section 10)

Figma: desktop node `409:5309` ("Desktop CTA"), mobile node `409:5342` ("Mobile CTA"). `components/sections/FinalCta.tsx`, a server component (its mobile scroll interaction is delegated to the shared `ScrollSpotlightList`, see below, so this file itself needs no `"use client"`). `content/home.ts`'s `finalCta` (heading/subline/CTA) and `complianceTicker` (title + items) were already written, waiting for this section; `complianceTicker.items` was trimmed from 9 to the **8 items actually confirmed** in both Figma frames (a "Safe, audited workplace" 9th item had no design behind it, same story as `insideFactory.body`/`.cta`). **Rendered twice on the homepage** — once here (its original Figma placement, after Inside the Factory/Exhibitions), and again after FAQ as the real page-closing prompt (`home.closingCta`, 2026-08-27). `ticker` is optional: the second usage omits it entirely rather than repeating the same compliance ticker twice on one page, which read as an unwanted extra section (especially on mobile, where it renders as its own large labelled block above the CTA, not a subtle strip below it like desktop's marquee).

Omitting `ticker` surfaced two real bugs the first (ticker-present) usage never exposed, both found by direct measurement, not guessed:
- **Desktop's `<Button>` had no width class**, so it sizes to its own label's text — "Request a Sample" (17 characters) measured 236.9px, "Let's Talk" (the second usage's shorter label) only 158.3px, visibly inconsistent between the two CTA bands on the same page. Fixed with a shared `finalCta.desktopButton` (`min-w-[240px] justify-center`) on both usages. Mobile was already consistent (`mobileButton`'s `w-[320px]`, shared by both), no change needed there.
- **`desktopSection` has no bottom padding of its own** — the ticker's own `Marquee` (`pb-8`) was always this section's sole source of bottom breathing room (see the comment on `desktopSection` for that original reuse decision). Without a ticker, the gap between the CTA button and the section's own bottom edge measured exactly `0`. Fixed with `finalCta.desktopSectionNoTicker` (`pb-[60px]`, mirroring the section's own `pt-[60px]`), applied only when `ticker` is absent. Mobile's `mobileCtaBlock` already carries its own unconditional `pb-[60px]` regardless of a preceding ticker, confirmed already correct.

- **Desktop and mobile are different layouts, not one responsive shape**, same pattern as `Hero`/`InsideFactory`: desktop is the CTA block (heading, subline, `Button`) *then* the ticker, as one continuous `bg-ink` band with no visible seam; mobile is the ticker *first* (its own label + vertical list), then the CTA block below it.
- **Desktop reuses `Marquee` untouched apart from one addition**: the compliance ticker's item text is 20px/`#F2F2F7`, not the offerings ticker's 18px — added as `Marquee`'s new `itemSize="lg"` prop (default `"body"` preserves Hero's ticker exactly) rather than forking the component. Separator is `separator="sparkle"` — the real exported vector (`get_screenshot` on node `409:5322`) is pixel-identical to lucide's `Sparkle`, already `Marquee`'s existing sparkle option, so no new icon asset was needed. `Marquee` supplies its own `container-p` and its own `pt-10 pb-8` vertical padding internally, so it's rendered as a **sibling** of the CTA block's own `container-p` wrapper, never nested inside it (the exact double-padding bug already found once on the offerings ticker) — a `mt-[110px]` on the `Marquee` instance plus its own 40px top padding makes up Figma's confirmed 150px block gap.
- **Mobile's compliance list gets a scroll-linked colour highlight**, per the owner's request: the item nearest the viewport's vertical centre brightens to white, one line in focus at a time, fading back as it scrolls past. This is `ScrollSpotlightList` (see its own entry below) — also reused on Hero's mobile ticker, not a one-off built for this section alone.
- **Reused on the Activewear PLP with a third, context-specific prop, `compactMobileTop`** (2026-08-30, owner: "'Standard on every order' should have a 72px gap from the section above it") — on mobile the ticker block (`mobileTickerBlock`, holding this "STANDARD ON EVERY ORDER" title) is the first thing in the section; its own `pt-[72px]` is load-bearing for the homepage's usage above (which follows `Exhibitions`, whose own `mobileSection` bottom padding is only 48px), but on the PLP it follows `Faq`, whose own `mobileSection` already supplies the standard `pb-[72px]` — stacking both doubled the real gap to 144px. `compactMobileTop` (passed only from `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`) swaps in `finalCta.mobileTickerBlockTight` (`!pt-0`), the same "one side owns the gap" pattern as `mobileCtaBlockNoTicker` above — the homepage's own usage is unaffected.
- **Desktop block now shows from `md:` (768px), not `xl:` (owner, 2026-09-04, homepage tablet-width review: "Use the same cta as desktop")** — same split already applied to Hero's ticker, Certified & Compliant, and Inside the Factory/Exhibitions: `desktopOuter`/`mobileOuter` moved from `xl:block`/`xl:hidden` to `md:block`/`md:hidden`. This section's own desktop layout has no fixed-width elements that risk overflow at tablet widths (centred flex column, no hardcoded pixel widths on the heading/subline), so no further tablet-specific tuning was needed, unlike Inside the Factory's gallery. Applies identically to both of this component's homepage usages (the original placement and the post-FAQ closing CTA), since both read the same shared recipe. Confirmed live: the desktop CTA block (heading, subline, `Button`) plus its `Marquee` ticker render correctly at 1024px, mobile block hidden; real mobile (375px) still gets the ticker-list-first layout unchanged; `xl:` desktop (1440px) unaffected.
- **Compliance ticker's own top/bottom padding trimmed at tablet width, bottom then corrected (owner, same day)**: first, "reduce the space from top and bottom of compliance section. reduce 32px from both sides" — `Marquee`'s shared `basePaddingDefault` (`pt-10 pb-8`, every Marquee instance sitewide) is untouched; a new `finalCta.desktopTickerTablet` token, passed as an extra `className` on this one `<Marquee>` instance only (`FinalCta.tsx`, alongside its existing `mt-[110px]`), cut 32px off each side at `md:`. Corrected the same day (owner: "cta bottom AQL inspection ticker does not have right space from the bottom. use same as desktop") — the bottom cut was wrong; reverted entirely, so bottom padding stays the shared `pb-8` (32px) at every width. The top cut stayed (not flagged). `desktopTickerTablet` is now `"md:pt-2 xl:pt-10"` — top only, `xl:pt-10` restoring the original desktop value explicitly (`md:` is a min-width breakpoint and stays active at `xl:` too unless overridden there, the same bug class already caught once on Inside the Factory's own tablet image ratio).
- **Section's own top padding (above the heading) also trimmed 32px at tablet width, same day (owner: "reduce 32px space from the top of the title let's build")** — `desktopSection`'s confirmed `60px` desktop value (see this entry's own opening comment) is now `md:pt-[28px] xl:pt-[60px]`, applying to both of this component's homepage usages since the token is shared. Confirmed live: `desktopSection`'s own top padding measures 28px and the ticker's own top padding measures 8px (bottom 32px, matching desktop) at both 768px and 1024px; all three values (60px/40px/32px) unchanged at 1440px `xl:` desktop; real mobile (375px, a fully separate DOM block) unaffected.
- **Third real usage: `/services` page, directly under `HowItWorks` (owner, 2026-09-07: "Add this cta setion, already built, use as is, put it under how it works section" — Figma node `770:895`)** — `<FinalCta content={home.finalCta} ticker={home.complianceTicker} />`, the exact same content pairing as the homepage's own first usage, no new content or props. Figma's own subline on this node ("Share your tech pack, sketch or a reference legging...") is `pdpShared.ts`'s leggings-PDP-specific `buildCtaSubline` wording, clearly a copy-paste leftover on a generic Services frame — `home.finalCta`'s own real subline is used instead (see `app/services/page.tsx`'s own comment for the full reasoning). Confirmed live: renders directly after `HowItWorks`, heading/button/ticker copy correct, button computed style `uppercase`/`18px`/`700`/`~24px` line-height (owner follow-up, same day: "How it works CTA should be uppercase 18 by 24px line height" — already the shared `Button`/`button.base` default, no change needed here).
- **Fourth real usage: `/services` page's own real closing section, after Faq (owner, 2026-09-07: "reuse the homepage final CTA + compliance bar component" — its own copy, not `home.finalCta`/`home.complianceTicker`)** — `<FinalCta content={services.finalCta} ticker={services.complianceBar} secondaryCta={services.finalCta.secondaryCta} />`. Genuinely different content from the third usage above despite the near-identical heading: subline "Share your tech pack, sketch or a reference. We'll come back within 24 hours with next steps." (owner-supplied verbatim this time, not a leggings-PDP leftover), and a shorter, owner-specified 5-item compliance bar (`services.complianceBar`, the first 5 of `home.complianceTicker`'s 8, reusing its "STANDARD ON EVERY ORDER" title since none was given for the shorter set) instead of the full 8. Introduces the component's new `secondaryCta?: { label: string; href: string }` prop (a second, outline `Button` rendered alongside the primary one, both breakpoints) — optional, so every other usage of this component (a single button) is unaffected. `FinalCtaProps`'s `content`/`ticker` types were widened from `typeof home.finalCta`/`typeof home.complianceTicker` to plain, `readonly`-compatible shapes for this usage — `content/services.ts` is an `as const` file (unlike `content/home.ts`), so its own content is a readonly tuple; the same widening `Marquee.items`/`ScrollSpotlightList.items`/`TrustPoints.points` needed for the same reason once this content started flowing through them.

### Our Services (homepage section 11)

Figma: desktop node `415:5449` ("Desktop Our Services"), mobile node `415:5484` ("Mobile Our Services"). `components/sections/OurServices.tsx`. `content/home.ts`'s `services` entry (eyebrow, h2, 5×`{title, body}`) and `CapabilityCard` were both already built in anticipation of this section, but this is the first time either is checked against the real design — see `CapabilityCard`'s corrections above.

- **Desktop: a sticky sidebar, not a scroll listener.** The eyebrow+heading column gets `position: sticky; top: 56px` (`ourServices.desktopSticky`) inside the two-column flex row; the right column's own stacked height (5 cards) is what makes the page taller than the viewport, so the left column naturally stays pinned at 56px until the right column's bottom edge passes it, then scrolls away with the page — exactly the "fixed while the right side scrolls" behaviour asked for, with zero JS.
- **A real overflow bug, same shape as Inside the Factory's earlier one**: the sticky column initially had `flex-shrink-0`, so at 1280/1366px viewports `579px (heading cap) + 221px (gap) + 480px (card)` didn't fit and the row overflowed. Fixed by removing `flex-shrink-0` (`min-w-0` instead) — the heading's own min-content is far smaller than 579px, so it just wraps to more lines at narrower widths instead of forcing an overflow.
- **Mobile: a swipeable card slider with real dot pagination**, not the static 4-dot graphic Figma exports for this frame — that asset is a generic placeholder mismatched against the section's actual 5 items. This is `CardCarousel` (see its own entry below) — originally built inline here, extracted into a shared component once How It Works needed the exact same pattern. Cards use `snap-start` with a real 16px gap between them, not Inside the Factory's zero-gap/centred-peek variant — Figma shows cards starting flush at the standard side inset here, not centred with peeks both sides.
- **Copy now matches Figma's own text exactly** (owner correction, 2026-08-25, overriding the initial "copy file wins" assumption below) — `content/home.ts`'s `services.items` bodies were rewritten to Figma's exact wording, including the British "Fulfilment" spelling. Note: Figma's own "Quality & Compliance" and "Logistics & Fulfilment" bodies read as if swapped (Quality's body describes logistics/shipping, Logistics' body describes inspections/audits) — reproduced exactly as designed anyway, per explicit instruction, rather than silently "fixing" what might be a genuine Figma-side mismatch.
- **The sticky heading's `max-width` doesn't actually control its rendered width — confirmed by measurement, not assumed** (2026-08-26): the column has no `shrink-0`, so its real width comes from flex-shrink math (`container-p`'s content width minus the row's `221px` gap minus the card column's fixed `480px`, landing on exactly `579px` regardless of viewport or the heading's own `max-width`). That's why the heading wrapped to 3 lines at a real 1920px viewport instead of 2 — `text-h1` grows to 65px there, and no amount of raising the heading's `max-width` gives the column more room, since the column was never sized by that property to begin with. The fix is `min-\[1920px\]:!text-[3.375rem]` on `desktopHeadingWidth`, freezing this one heading's size at its 1440 value (54px) instead of trying to widen a column that can't widen — `!important` is required, since `xl:text-h1` (from the shared `SectionHeading` recipe) otherwise wins the cascade at 1920px despite being applied first in the class string (see the decision log).
- **Mobile block's top padding gained a tablet-only override (owner, 2026-09-04, tablet-width review: "Our services eyebrow section should have 40px more space from the top")** — this section's desktop sticky-sidebar layout is still `xl:`-only (not yet extended to `md:`, unlike Hero/Certified/Inside the Factory/Exhibitions/Final CTA), so the mobile `CardCarousel` block (`ourServices.mobileSection`, `xl:hidden`) is what actually renders at tablet width today. `pt-12` (48px) gained `md:pt-[88px]` (+40px) — no `xl:` reset needed here, unlike those other fixes, since this block never renders at `xl:` regardless of the override. Confirmed live: 88px at 1024px, unchanged 48px at real mobile (375px), block correctly hidden (desktop sticky sidebar showing instead) at 1440px.
- **Mobile heading gained the sitewide 2-line wrap width, same day (owner: "our services should also be in title in 2 lines as others")** — unlike Certified & Compliant/Inside the Factory/Exhibitions/How It Works, whose centred headings all force their real Figma 2-line wrap via `max-w-[812px]`, this section's mobile-block heading had no width constraint at all, so it just ran the full container width and wrapped wherever that happened to land instead of a reliable 2 lines. New `ourServices.mobileHeadingWidth` (`max-w-[812px]`, the same sitewide value), passed as `headingClassName` on the mobile block's `SectionHeading` only — the desktop sticky-sidebar heading already has its own, unrelated width constraint (`desktopHeadingWidth`, 579px, driven by flex-shrink math, not this fix). Confirmed live: 2 lines at 768px, 1024px, and 375px (real mobile — the constraint has no effect there, container is already narrower than 812px, wrap was already 2 lines); desktop's own 579px-constrained heading at 1440px unaffected (the mobile block is hidden there).

### How It Works (homepage section 12)

Figma: desktop node `430:1929` ("Desktop How It Works"), mobile node `430:1961` ("Mobile How it Works"). `components/sections/HowItWorks.tsx`. Mobile is deliberately treated the same as Our Services (owner call, 2026-08-25) — the shared `CardCarousel`, not a bespoke layout. Neither Figma frame shows a step number anywhere, despite `content/home.ts`'s `howItWorks.steps` originally carrying an `n` field for one and `FeatureNumbered` existing to render it — both are now gone; this section uses plain `CapabilityCard`s, same component as Our Services (see its corrections above, and its new size-override props).

- **Desktop: a centred heading forced to 2 lines**, via the same `max-w-[812px]` wrapper already confirmed for Certified & Compliant and Inside the Factory's centred headings — not a new one-off value.
- **Desktop cards are genuinely fixed-width and horizontally scrollable, not shrunk to fit** — first built as `flex-1 min-w-0` (the fix already applied twice elsewhere for a *different* overflow bug, Inside the Factory and Our Services), but corrected once `get_metadata` on the real frame showed the 5th card sitting at x=1516 in a 1440-wide frame, entirely off-canvas (even the 4th hangs slightly over) — the design itself only shows 4 cards at a time, with a 5th reachable by scrolling, not 5 cards proportionally squeezed to fit. Card width was Figma's own confirmed 335px until 2026-08-27, when it was widened to 469px to match Exhibitions' own desktop card exactly (owner call: both sections' media containers, and the gap between them, should read as the same size) — a deliberate departure from the literal Figma number, not a correction to it; title/body text switched from a How-It-Works-specific smaller override to `CapabilityCard`'s plain defaults (Our Services' own sizes) at the same time, since the card is now close enough to Our Services' 480px width that the smaller text no longer read proportionally.
- **A single floating chevron, in a white circle, tracks the cursor and pages the row** (owner request, 2026-08-25, iterated three times before landing here — first two small fixed corner buttons, then static left/right click-halves of the row, finally this cursor-tracking version): its position and left/right icon follow the mouse within the row, flipping direction at the row's horizontal midpoint; clicking scrolls exactly one card (469px + 24px gap) in that direction with a smooth animation. Position and visibility are written directly to the icon's DOM `style` from mousemove/enter/leave handlers (same direct-DOM-write pattern as `InsideFactory`'s carousel and `ScrollSpotlightList`), not driven by CSS `:hover`, since the icon's transform has to track the cursor continuously. The native cursor is hidden over the row (`cursor-none`) — otherwise the OS pointer/hand icon shows redundantly alongside the custom chevron.
- **Tracks the cursor's Y position too, not just X** (corrected 2026-08-26) — it originally only moved horizontally, pinned to a fixed vertical position centred on the 220px media block, so hovering the card's title/body text below the image never brought the chevron anywhere near the cursor, reading as "only works over the media." **The jerky appearance was a stale-position bug, not an animation problem**: `mouseenter` only set opacity before, so the chevron faded in wherever it was last left (or its 0,0 default) and then visibly snapped to the real cursor position on the next `mousemove`. Fixed by seeding the transform from the entry event's own coordinates inside the `mouseenter` handler itself, before opacity turns on — no snap, since it already starts exactly where the cursor is.
- **Position is clamped, not the raw cursor coordinate** (corrected 2026-08-26, same session as the Y-tracking fix above) — `desktopScrollerWrap` clips overflow, so once Y could reach the full card height, entering or leaving near the wrap's top/bottom/left/right edge hard-clipped the 80px circle the instant its centre came within 40px of that edge, reading as "coming from the top" / "hiding in the container" rather than a clean fade. Both `x` and `y` are clamped to keep the whole circle inside the box at every cursor position (`Math.min(Math.max(coord, CHEVRON_HALF), dimension - CHEVRON_HALF)`), so `opacity` is the only thing that ever visibly changes — no clipping pop dressed up as a show/hide transition.
- **`transition-all`, not `transition-opacity`** (corrected 2026-08-26 — still reported jerky after clamping) — `transform` had no transition at all, so every single `mousemove` sample teleported the circle instantly to that exact pixel with zero easing between samples. For a cursor-following element that's inherently jerky/discontinuous motion on its own, most noticeable exactly at the first frame (entry) and the last one (exit), independent of the opacity fade. A single shared `transition-all duration-150 ease-out` covers both properties, turning per-pixel teleporting into a smooth trailing glide — and doesn't affect click accuracy, since the click handler reads `directionRef` from the real cursor position directly, never this element's (deliberately lagging) visual position.
- **Superseded the same day**: a CSS transition still has a ceiling on smoothness, since it re-eases from scratch every time `mousemove` retargets it and `mousemove` itself fires unevenly — a further owner report ("still not 100%... should be really smooth") led to extracting the whole mechanism into `useDesktopChevronScroller` (see `DesktopChevronScroller` above), which replaces the CSS transition with a continuous `requestAnimationFrame` easing loop. This section's own `desktopChevronCircle`/`desktopChevronIcon` recipe keys are gone — the chevron is now the shared `chevronScroller` recipe.
- **`scroll-pl-[80px]`/`scroll-pr-[80px]` on the track, matching its own `px-[80px]`** — without them, CSS scroll-snap's snap-point math doesn't know that padding is safe space, and silently auto-corrects the rest scroll position to consume it, collapsing the intended 80px gap before the first card to 0 (a real bug, found and fixed 2026-08-25).
- **`CapabilityCard`'s own size differs from Our Services'** — 28px desktop title (24px mobile originally, now 30px — see 2026-08-26 note below) vs Our Services' flat 30px, 32px/16px media-to-text and title-to-body gaps (desktop/mobile) vs Our Services' 24px/16px. Passed via `CapabilityCard`'s new `rootClassName`/`bodyClassName`/`titleClassName` overrides (`capabilityCard.rootHowItWorks`/`.bodyHowItWorks`/`.titleHowItWorks`) rather than changing the shared defaults, which stay Our Services' own confirmed values.
- **Copy is shared across both breakpoints** (owner call, 2026-08-25) — Figma's own two frames disagreed slightly (desktop's step 1 said "24 hours", mobile said "24 to 48"; mobile's step 2 body was a fuller sentence). `content/home.ts` adopts mobile's fuller wording for every step except step 1, which was corrected back to desktop's "24 hours" (a second owner call), and drops the now-meaningless `n` field entirely.
- **Mobile heading is forced to Figma's own real 3-line break** ("From tech pack to" / "shipped order in five" / "easy steps", confirmed via `get_screenshot` on node `430:1961`) — our natural wrap at this viewport also landed on 3 lines, but at different word breaks than Figma's. `HowItWorks.tsx`'s `renderMobileHeading` splits `content.h2` at Figma's exact break points rather than hardcoding a second copy of the string, so `content/home.ts` stays the single source for the copy.
- **Desktop scroller now shows from `md:` (768px), not `xl:` (owner, 2026-09-04, homepage tablet-width review: "from tech pack to title should also be treated like other titles")** — same split already applied to Hero's ticker, Certified & Compliant, Inside the Factory/Exhibitions, and Final CTA: `desktopOuter`/`mobileSection` moved from `xl:`/`xl:hidden` to `md:`/`md:hidden`. This section's card size/ratio/gap were already widened to match Exhibitions exactly (2026-08-27, see the note above), so extending it needed the same two changes Exhibitions' own tablet fix needed: `desktopRow`'s fixed `px-[80px]` (correct only at `xl:`) gained an unprefixed `px-8`/`scroll-pl-8`/`scroll-pr-8` base (32px, matching `container-p`'s `md:` inset) with the original 80px values now `xl:`-scoped; `cardMediaRatio`'s `xl:aspect-[469/320]` became `md:aspect-[469/320]` (no separate `xl:` value needed, since it's the identical ratio at both tiers). The chevron's click-to-scroll pitch (`CARD_WIDTH + CARD_GAP`, fixed constants in `HowItWorks.tsx`) needed no change, since the card size doesn't differ by breakpoint here. Confirmed live: 32px left inset with a real second-card peek at 1024px (card 2 at x=525, media box measuring 469×320px); 80px inset/469×320px unchanged at 1440px `xl:` desktop; real mobile (<768px) still gets its own separate `CardCarousel`, unaffected.

**Dark-mode `tone` variant added 2026-09-07, reused on `/services` under Product Range (Figma node `767:868`).** Owner: "this is the same setion we have on homepage, I just changed the background to black and subline text color to 838D97... put this section under product range section", then explicitly: "build this as a new component in design system with dark mode, same section just dark mode variant" — a real, reusable `tone?: "light" | "dark"` prop (default `"light"`, so every existing caller is byte-for-byte unaffected), not a forked file:
  - `HowItWorks.tsx`: `desktopOuter`/`mobileSection` gain `howItWorks.darkSurface` (`"bg-ink text-paper"`) via `cx()` when `tone === "dark"`; both `SectionHeading` calls get `eyebrowTone={tone}` instead of the hardcoded `"light"`. Reusing `SectionHeading`/`Eyebrow`'s own existing `"light"`/`"dark"` vocabulary directly (rather than inventing a new one) means the eyebrow automatically picks up the *standing* dark-eyebrow colour (`eyebrow.dark`, `#ABB5C0` — "no exceptions" per that token's own comment, deliberately **not** Figma's literal `#838d97` on that one line, since CLAUDE.md rule 4 says colour isn't copied exactly from Figma sitewide and the owner's explicit ask was scoped to "subline text," read here as the card body copy) and the heading falls through to inherit the new ambient `text-paper` (white) instead of `light` tone's own `headingLight`/`text-subline` override — no separate heading-colour code needed.
  - `desktopOuter`'s own `pt-*` was pulled out into two tone-specific tokens, `desktopOuterLight` (`pt-[60px]`, the homepage's original value) and a desktop-only `desktopOuterDark`, rather than living on the shared base — appending a second `pt-*` alongside a base one would be the exact "two same-specificity utilities racing" bug class this project already avoids elsewhere. `mobileSection` keeps its own unchanged `pt-12` at every tone — 200px+ is a desktop-scale number the owner never asked for on mobile.
  - `Card.tsx`: `CardMediaProps`/`CapabilityCardProps` both gain the same `tone` prop. `CardMedia` selects `cardMedia.placeholder` vs. a new `cardMedia.placeholderDark` (`"bg-ink-2"`, reusing the exact token `media.placeholderDark` already uses for this identical "no photo yet, on dark" case elsewhere — not a new dark-surface colour). `CapabilityCard` forwards `tone` to its own `CardMedia` and selects `capabilityCard.text` vs. a new `capabilityCard.textDark` (identical size/leading, `text-subline` swapped for the owner's explicit `#838D97` — already this project's extremely well-established muted-on-dark colour, used 15+ times elsewhere) for the body paragraph. The title needs no switch — `capabilityCard.title` carries no colour class at all, so it already inherits whichever ambient colour the section sets.
  - `CardCarousel.tsx`: `CardCarouselProps` gains `tone`, forwarded straight through to each `CapabilityCard`. Its own dot pagination (`bg-accent` active / `bg-[#D1D1D6]` inactive) needed no dark counterpart — both read fine on either background.
  - **/services-specific top gap (owner follow-up, same day: "how it works should have 200px gap from the top section", then refined: "remove any space from the bottom of product range section or add 80px under product rage and 120px top how it works. total space should be 200px")** — `ProductRange.inner` already carries its own `xl:pb-20` (80px, scoped to that one section/page only), so `desktopOuterDark` is `pt-[120px]`, not a literal 200px, landing on the requested 200px total (80 + 120) without zeroing out Product Range's own existing bottom gap — kept as the less structurally invasive of the two options the owner offered. Confirmed live: `ProductRange.inner`'s `paddingBottom` (80px) + this section's `paddingTop` (120px) = 200px exactly at 1440px.
  - Confirmed live on `/`: `HowItWorks` pixel-identical to before (`pt-[60px]`, no `bg-ink`) — the new prop's default is fully non-breaking. Confirmed live on `/services`: `bg-ink`/`text-paper` on both root divs, `#ABB5C0` eyebrow, `#838D97` card body text (`rgb(131, 141, 151)`), `bg-ink-2` placeholder boxes (`rgb(23, 23, 29)`), white (`rgb(255,255,255)`) card titles, 200px total gap down from Product Range.

### FAQ (homepage section 13)

Figma: desktop node `438:2150`, mobile node `438:2192`, copy sourced from dedicated copy node `438:2262`. `components/sections/Faq.tsx`. No eyebrow — the real design is one large heading, not a `SectionHeading` composition (the earlier eyebrow, "TOP QUESTIONS FROM B2B BUYERS", was a wireframe-era guess with no eyebrow behind it in the real frame).

- **Desktop**: `bg-ink` lives on its own unconstrained `desktopOuter`, `container-p` only on an inner `desktopInner` flex row — the same full-bleed split every other dark section uses (Hero, Stats, Inside the Factory). Shipped once with `container-p` and `bg-ink` on the same element, which caps the fill at `container-p`'s own 1440px max-width instead of true edge-to-edge at 1920px+ — fixed the same day.
