Two-column flex inside: a `shrink-0` (genuinely sized by its own `max-width`, unlike Our Services' sticky heading below) `text-h1` heading on the left, `Accordion` filling the rest (`flex-1`, not a hardcoded pixel width, so it scales correctly at any width) on the right. **Heading width is `max-w-[8.85em]`, one em-based value covering every width from `md:` (768px) to 1920px+ (owner, 2026-09-06, after two rounds of "still in 3 lines")** — three separate fixed-px breakpoint caps (320px/360px at `md:`, 474px at `xl:`, 500px at 1920+) were each tried and each broke somewhere else, because `text-h1`'s font-size is a *continuous* fluid clamp, not stepped: at 1279px (the top of `md:`, just before `xl:`) the font is already ~50px and needs ~440px to hold 2 lines, far more than what worked lower in the `md:` band. Live-measured, the minimum width needed for "Top questions from" (the forced first line, see `Faq.tsx`'s `renderHeadingWithB2BBreak`) to hold one line is a near-constant ~8.73–8.75× the heading's own font-size at every width checked (342px/39.07px at 768, 440px/50.42px at 1279, 472px/54px at 1440, 564px/64.67px at 1920) — expected, since it's the same string and rendered width scales with font-size. `em` units are relative to the element's own font-size, so a single `8.85em` (a small buffer above the measured ratio) tracks the clamp automatically at any width — no breakpoint chasing needed.
- **Mobile**: left-aligned heading above a full-width `Accordion`, `pt-[72px]`/`pb-[72px]` — this frame's own top padding really is 72px, unlike the general `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]` pattern used elsewhere on the page (decision 2z). Heading was centred (`items-center`/`text-center`) until 2026-09-04 — see the dated note below; every other mobile section heading on the site is left-aligned by default, and this was the one outlier.
- **`defaultOpen={1}`** on both breakpoints — Figma shows the MOQ question (index 1) open by default.
- **First real use of `Accordion` against a real design** — see the corrected values under Accordion above.
- **First real use of `faqSchema()`** (SEO/AEO rule 1) — `app/page.tsx` renders `<JsonLd data={faqSchema(home.faq.items)} />` fed by the exact same `home.faq.items` array the visible `Accordion` reads, so the schema can never drift from what's on the page.
- **Reused verbatim by the Services page (2026-09-07)** — `app/services/page.tsx`'s own FAQ section, the page's last section (after Certified & Compliant), is `<Faq content={services.faq} />`, same component/styling, no fork (owner: "same pattern as the homepage ... do NOT fork or restyle it"). `FaqProps` was widened from `{ content: typeof home.faq }` to a plain `{ h2: string; items: readonly { q: string; a: string }[] }` shape so it accepts either `home.faq` (a mutable object) or `services.faq` (from an `as const` content file, so its `items` is a readonly tuple) — `lib/schema.ts`'s `faqSchema()` took the same `readonly FaqItem[]` widening for the same reason. `services.faq` is its own 14-question set (`content/services.ts`), owner-supplied verbatim, not paraphrased — house rules for that copy: American spelling, no en/em dashes, "spandex" (never elastane/Lycra), no prices. Rendered via `<Faq content={services.faq} />` plus `<JsonLd data={faqSchema(services.faq.items)} />`, the same "one content object feeds both" pattern below.
- **A second `<FinalCta>` renders directly after this section, with no ticker** (owner request, 2026-08-27: "the same CTA section as below Exhibitions") — `home.closingCta`, the page's real closing prompt before the Footer, button label "Let's Talk" (still linking to `/request-a-sample`, same form as every other CTA). See Final CTA's own entry below for the full detail (the `ticker` prop being optional, and two real bugs the ticker-less usage surfaced — a button that hugs its label's own width, and a section with genuinely zero bottom padding without the ticker's own `Marquee` supplying it).

New `Category` field `faqHeading` feeds `content.h2`. `leggings.ts`'s `faqs` carries all 11 real Q&A pairs from the Figma reference (no `PLACEHOLDER:` rows left) — one genuine gap in that reference (the closing "How do I get started?" has no answer in the Figma text layer) filled by reusing the homepage's own answer to the identical question verbatim, confirmed matching word-for-word, rather than inventing new copy. `faqSchema(data.faqs)` runs the same way as the homepage's own FAQ.

Falls through to the plain string unchanged if a future heading doesn't contain "B2B" at all. Confirmed live: renders as `Top questions from<br>B2B buyers` at every checked width (375/1024/1440px) on both the homepage and a PLP (`/activewear/t-shirts`).

**Mobile heading corrected to left-aligned, matching Figma (owner, 2026-09-04, spotted against a design screenshot: "why the FAQ title is not same as the design")** — `mobileSection` had its own one-off `items-center` and `mobileHeading` its own `text-center`, both removed (`items-start`, no `text-center`). This was the single outlier on the site: every other mobile section heading (`SectionHeading`'s own `max-md:` styles) is already left-aligned. `mobileHeading` gained `px-5` directly (20px) so it still lines up with the accordion questions below it — `mobileSection` intentionally has no `container-p`/horizontal padding of its own (`mobileAccordion` spans it edge to edge; each `accordion.item` supplies its own `max-xl:px-5` inset), so adding `container-p` to the section would have doubled that inset to 40px. Applies everywhere `Faq` renders (homepage, both PLPs, both PDPs), since it's the one shared component. Confirmed live at 375px on the homepage and `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression`: heading flush with the accordion questions' own left edge, matching the design screenshot; desktop (`md:`+) was already left-aligned and is unchanged.

**Desktop block now shows from `md:` (768px), not `xl:` (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — `faq.desktopOuter`/`mobileSection` moved `xl:block`/`xl:hidden` → `md:block`/`md:hidden`. This section is shared by the homepage, both PLPs, and both PDPs, and hadn't been touched by the earlier homepage-only pass, so this one fix covers all of them at once. **Tablet-specific tightening was needed**: the desktop row's fixed `474px` heading + `124px` gap left the accordion column only ~100px wide at 768px — not an overflow, but unusably narrow. `desktopInner`'s gap is now `gap-10` (40px) at `md:`, `xl:gap-[124px]` restoring the original. (`desktopHeading`'s own width cap was later replaced entirely by the single `8.85em` value described above, 2026-09-06 — see that entry for why the original `md:`/`xl:`/1920px breakpoint split couldn't stay 2 lines everywhere.) One dot per real item, not necessarily whatever count a Figma dot-graphic export shows — those are flat images unaware of the section's real item count (Our Services' own export was a mismatched 4 dots for 5 real cards). First built inline in `OurServices.tsx`, extracted here once How It Works needed the identical pattern.

### DesktopChevronScroller — Built
`components/DesktopChevronScroller.tsx` · recipe: `chevronScroller`

The shared floating cursor-tracking chevron for a horizontally scrollable desktop card row: How It Works, Inside the Factory and Exhibitions each need one full image/card centred-or-aligned at a time, paged by clicking a chevron that follows the cursor and flips direction at the row's midpoint. Originally built once (How It Works, 2026-08-25) then copy-pasted twice more verbatim as the other two sections needed it — extracted here 2026-08-26 once a third owner report of residual jerkiness made clear the remaining smoothness ceiling needed fixing once, not three times independently.

**The fix, and why the old approach couldn't get smoother**: the original version wrote the cursor's raw position straight to the chevron's `transform` on every `mousemove`, with a CSS `transition-all` easing between samples. That's a hard ceiling: `mousemove` fires at an uneven, browser-decided rate (bursty under fast movement, sparse under slow), and a CSS transition re-eases from scratch every time its target changes mid-flight — which reads as a subtle stutter or rubber-band on quick or reversing motion, not one continuous glide, no matter how the duration/easing curve is tuned. The fix replaces the CSS transition with a `requestAnimationFrame` loop that runs continuously while hovered: every frame, the chevron's rendered position eases a fixed *fraction* of the remaining distance toward wherever the cursor last was (exponential smoothing), decoupling the visual motion entirely from `mousemove`'s own event rate. The smoothing factor is scaled by the frame's real elapsed time (`1 - (1 - SMOOTHING) ** (dt / (1000/60))`), so it converges at the same real-world speed on a 144Hz display as a 60Hz one, rather than trailing faster the higher the refresh rate. The chevron's own CSS now only transitions `opacity` (200ms) — a transition on `transform` would just fight the per-frame JS writes, never settling.

`useDesktopChevronScroller(cardPitch: number)` returns `wrapRef`/`trackRef`/`chevronRef` (attach to the hover wrap, the scrollable track, and the floating circle respectively), `direction` (`1 | -1`, for which icon to render), and the four event handlers (`handleMouseMove`/`handleMouseEnter`/`handleMouseLeave`/`handleClick`) to spread onto the wrap. `cardPitch` is the exact scroll distance for one click-to-page (card width + gap) — each section passes its own. `DesktopChevron` renders the circle itself (`ChevronLeft`/`ChevronRight`, swapped by `direction`) using the shared `chevronScroller` recipe, so no section needs its own `desktopChevronCircle`/`desktopChevronIcon` keys any more.

**Used by**: `HowItWorks.tsx` (`snap-start`, edge-aligned), `InsideFactory.tsx` (`snap-center`, active-card-centred), `Exhibitions.tsx` (`snap-start`, same as How It Works) — three different scroll-snap behaviours around the one shared chevron mechanism.

**Used by:** Our Services, How It Works.

### JsonLd — Built
`components/JsonLd.tsx`

Renders one schema.org JSON-LD `<script type="application/ld+json">` tag. Takes a single `data` prop and serializes it — it has no knowledge of schema shape itself; every caller passes the output of a `lib/schema.ts` builder function (`organizationSchema()`, `websiteSchema()`, `breadcrumbSchema(items)`, `faqSchema(items)`, `navigationSchema(links)`, `megaMenuSchema(name, href, groups)`). See [06-seo.md](06-seo.md) for the full SEO/AEO rule set this supports.

`app/page.tsx` renders `navigationSchema(home.nav.links)` once for the top-level nav, plus `megaMenuSchema(...)` for each nav link that carries a real `megaMenu` (Activewear, Teamwear & Uniforms today) — fed by the exact same data `Header.tsx`/`MobileNav.tsx` already render, so the schema can never drift from the real nav/mega-menu content.

**Used by:** `app/layout.tsx` (Organization + WebSite, site-wide), `app/page.tsx` (BreadcrumbList).

### ScrollSpotlightList — Built
`components/ScrollSpotlightList.tsx`

A vertical `<ul>`/`<li>` list where the item nearest the viewport's vertical centre brightens to a focus colour (default white), fading back to a rest colour as it scrolls past — one line spotlighted at a time, not a group dim. The project's client-component pattern for a scroll-position-driven visual state with no static-CSS equivalent: colour is written straight to each item's DOM `style.color` via refs, bypassing React state so a fast scroll never waits on a re-render (same approach as `InsideFactory`'s carousel, but simpler and with no reflow risk, since this only ever touches `color`, never a layout-affecting property like `height`). Falloff is normalized against the list's own live row spacing (the measured gap between two consecutive items' centres), not the viewport height — normalizing by the viewport faded 3-4 lines at once instead of spotlighting one (corrected 2026-08-25, reference: studio-size.com's services list).

**Recalculated via a persistent `requestAnimationFrame` loop, not a `scroll` event listener** (corrected 2026-08-25, owner report: no animation at all on a real Samsung device/large mobile viewport, not reproducible in this project's own Chromium-based browser testing). Some mobile browsers batch or throttle `scroll` events far more coarsely during momentum/fling scrolling than desktop Chrome; a self-scheduling rAF loop recalculates every animation frame regardless of whether or how often `scroll` fires, removing that whole class of cross-browser inconsistency at the cost of a few always-on `getBoundingClientRect` reads per frame while the list is mounted — negligible for a handful of list items.

| Prop | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `items` | The list, plain strings |
| `listClassName` / `itemClassName` | Passed straight to the `<ul>`/`<li>` — this component owns no appearance of its own |
| `restColor` / `focusColor` | Default `#838D97` / `#FFFFFF` |

**Used by:** Final CTA's mobile compliance list, Hero's mobile "Fully Custom Offerings" list (added 2026-08-25 — same interaction, reused rather than re-implemented; Hero's list previously had no scroll interaction, just a static colour).
- **Dark-to-dark flush spacing**: Inside the Factory (dark) is immediately followed by this section (also dark). Per the standing rule fixed 2026-08-24 (see Inside the Factory above), neither section's mobile wrapper carries a `mb-[72px]`/margin between them — both sit flush, no page-background seam.

| # | Section | Built from |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Header / Nav | Header, MobileNav |
| 2 | Hero | ✅ **Built** — `components/sections/Hero.tsx`. MediaPlaceholder, Eyebrow, Button |
| 3 | Fully Custom Offerings ticker | ✅ **Built** — same file as Hero. Figma groups banner, video and ticker into one "Hero" frame, so they're one component, not two. Marquee (desktop, `xl`+) / `ScrollSpotlightList` (mobile, below `xl`) |
| 4 | Brand logos row | ✅ **Built** — `components/sections/ClientLogos.tsx`. Marquee (desktop) / 2-column grid (mobile) |

### Hero — Built
`components/sections/ServicesHero.tsx` · Figma: desktop node `729:139` (mobile not yet designed — owner handling separately)

A simpler variant of the homepage `Hero` (no eyebrow, no video placeholder block), not a copy of it — the layout genuinely differs enough to warrant its own component, but every reusable piece is the real shared one: `Button`, `TextReveal`, and `Marquee` itself (identical props to homepage `Hero`'s own usage — `label="Fully Custom Offerings"`, `labelVariant="bold"`, `separator="sparkle"`, `tone="dark"`, `pauseOnHover={false}`, same `home.customOfferings` content object, not a duplicated copy of the 9 items). New `servicesHero` recipe in `components/ui/styles.ts`, separate from `hero`, since forcing this simpler shape onto `hero`'s own props (eyebrow, video) would mean passing unused props through for no reason.

**Every spacing value confirmed via `get_metadata` against the real Figma node**, not eyeballed off the screenshot: banner content starts 140px from the top (same real Figma constant `hero.bannerInner` already uses — reused directly, not re-derived), the H1's own 832px wrap width is exactly `max-w-[52rem]` (identical to `hero.heading`, reused directly), the gap between H1 and buttons is 48px (matches `hero`'s own `xl:gap-12`).

**Two real bugs found live and fixed the same day (owner report: ticker content reading as cut off above the fold, plus a stray line under the ticker text):**

- **A real, rendered `border-bottom` line under the ticker** — `Marquee`'s default `divider: true` (correct for homepage Hero's own ticker, which Figma shows one for) was left on here too, but this Figma frame (`729:139`) has no divider at all. Confirmed live before the fix (`getComputedStyle` on `.marquee`: `border-bottom-width: 1px`), fixed with `divider={false}`.
- **Total section height (87px sticky header + hero) exceeded a real ~800-820px laptop browser viewport** — this component's Figma-literal bottom banner padding (126px, `578px total Banner height − 140px top − 312px of real content`) and its custom ticker wrapper (a `padded={false}` `Marquee` inside a bespoke `pt-[72px]/pb-8` wrap) together pushed the ticker below the fold on shorter real viewports, even though the numbers matched Figma exactly (same root cause as the Footer height fix earlier the same day — see that entry's own comment and the decision log). Fixed by dropping both Figma one-offs in favor of this codebase's own already-established "standard" values instead: the banner's bottom padding is now `xl:pb-20` (80px, `hero.bannerInner`'s own existing bottom inset, reused directly), and the ticker is now a plain, unwrapped `Marquee` with its own default padding (`padded` unset) — identical usage to homepage Hero's own ticker, no bespoke wrapper at all. Total header + hero height dropped from ~849px to a confirmed live **771px**, comfortably fitting inside a real 816px-tall laptop viewport (45px to spare, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect`, no scroll needed).

**Desktop-only per the owner's own brief** ("desktop design is ready, I'm handling mobile separately, build desktop to the design and keep it responsive-safe") — mobile/tablet values (`container-p`, stacked full-width buttons, `flex-col gap-4 xl:flex-row`) are a reasonable, non-breaking fallback matching the site's existing responsive conventions, not read from a real mobile Figma frame. Confirmed live at 375px: no overflow, both buttons stack full-width, ticker scrolls correctly. Unlike homepage `Hero`, the secondary "Download Catalog" button is NOT hidden on mobile here — no mobile Figma exists yet to confirm hiding it the way homepage's does, so it stays visible at every width until a real mobile design says otherwise.

**Copy**: the H1 ("Custom activewear and teamwear, from fabric to packaging") and both button labels are read directly off the Figma text layers — the design's own real copy, not placeholder text. `metaTitle`/`metaDescription` in `content/services.ts` ARE placeholders, flagged with a `TODO(owner copy)` comment — no real SEO copy has been given for this page yet.

`npm run build`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` all clean; full `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` suite (30 tests, existing pages only — `/services` not yet added to that suite's target list) still green, no regressions.

### How we work with you — Built
`components/sections/ServicesHowWeWork.tsx` · recipe: `servicesHowWeWork` · Figma: desktop node `750:770` (mobile not yet designed)

A centred intro (heading + subheading), 3 "path" cards (OEM Production / ODM Development / Private Label), and a closing note with an accent-orange asterisk icon. `get_metadata`-confirmed exact: section padding `pt-[160px] pb-[80px]` (`px-[80px]` is `container-p`'s own desktop inset, reused not redeclared), `gap-[72px]` between the intro block and the cards row, `gap-[140px]` between the cards row and the closing note, each card's own image-to-text `gap-[32px]`.

**Real Figma page order matters here**: this section's own y-position (`1949`) sits directly between `ServicesIntro`'s (`833`) and the homepage-reused `OurServices`' (`3279`) — confirmed via `get_metadata` on all three nodes. `OurServices` had already been wired in as this page's "section 3" (right after Intro) before this section was built; both `app/services/page.tsx`'s own header comment and its JSX order were corrected to insert this section between the two, matching the real Figma order, rather than appending it after `OurServices` just because that's where the page happened to already end.

- **New, bespoke component, not a reuse** — `CapabilityCard` (Our Services/How It Works) only carries a single title + body per card, not this card's real shape (title + subtitle header, then two separately-labeled "What it means"/"Best for" blocks); forcing this content into it would mean bolting on unused props rather than a clean fit. What IS reused: `MediaPlaceholder` for each card's plain image box, a new `AsteriskIcon` component (`components/icons/AsteriskIcon.tsx`, currentColor, same pattern as every other icon in that folder — not an inlined one-off `<svg>`), and `NoteSegment` (from `content/activewear/types.ts`) for the closing note's bold-phrase paragraph, the same segmented-note pattern `ServicesIntro`'s own paragraph already uses.
- **New `MediaPlaceholder` ratio, `"397:234"`** — each card's image box is a real, exact Figma pixel size (397×234, node `750:777`) that doesn't match any existing ratio token; 397 is prime, so this fraction is already in lowest terms, added following this table's own established "keep exact Figma ratios, don't round" precedent (`50:37`, `469:320`, `79:100`, etc.).
- **Cards use a real CSS grid** (`grid-cols-1 xl:grid-cols-3`), not Figma's fixed 397px card widths — the row reflows to a single stacked column below `xl` instead of overflowing at narrower widths, the same "responsive-safe, not a confirmed mobile design" caveat as every other section on this page.
- **Typography**: card titles reuse `text-h3`, which hits exactly 30px/500 at this project's own 1440px reference width (matching Figma's flat 30px/500 title) — the same "reuse a fluid token at its matching breakpoint" approach `ServicesIntro` takes with `text-h1`. The "What it means"/"Best for" labels (22px/500, 1px letter-spacing) are a real one-off (`text-[1.375rem] font-medium tracking-[1px]`), not `text-h3` — that token's own 22px value only occurs at a much narrower viewport than this desktop-only component ever renders at, so reusing it here would be a coincidence, not a real match. Body/subtitle colour (`#17191E`) matches the same literal Figma value already used elsewhere on this page (`ServicesIntro`'s own body colour, Footer's contact label) — a real, confirmed Figma colour with no matching token, kept exact per this project's own established precedent for that specific case, rather than swapped for the nearest token.

Copy (heading, subheading, all 3 cards' title/subtitle/"What it means"/"Best for" text, and the closing note) is the design's own real text layer, read directly — not placeholder copy.

**Two real bugs found live and fixed the same day (owner report):**

- **The 140px gap above the closing note was rendering as 72px** — `inner` originally put a single uniform flex `gap` (72px) between every child, but Figma's real gap is 72px above the cards row and a *different* 140px above the closing note. A uniform `gap` can't express two different values, so both ended up at 72px. Fixed by removing `inner`'s own `gap` entirely and giving `pathsGrid`/`noteWrap` each their own top margin instead (`xl:mt-[72px]`/`xl:mt-[140px]`) — confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect`, the gap now measures exactly 140px.
- **The closing note had no reveal animation** (owner: "it should apply the text animation... apply the same text animation that we applied on text on homepage and other places"). `TextReveal` gained a new `segments` mode (`TextRevealSegment[]`, mutually exclusive with its existing `text` prop) so a `NoteSegment[]` paragraph can animate word-by-word while still preserving which words are bold — every other `TextReveal` caller (Hero's H1, every section eyebrow/heading) is untouched, still passing plain `text`. A second issue surfaced immediately after wiring it in: this is the first `TextReveal` usage on a long, ~32-word paragraph rather than a short heading, and `app/globals.css`'s per-word stagger (45ms/word, uncapped) was only ever tuned against headings a handful of words long — the same mechanism took ~2.2s to finish here versus ~1s elsewhere, which read as a genuinely different, sluggish effect even though it's mechanically identical CSS. Fixed by capping `segments` mode's own `--reveal-index` at 10 (`MAX_STAGGER_INDEX`), so trailing words in a long paragraph share the last few staggered slots instead of marching on indefinitely — confirmed live: total reveal duration dropped from ~2195ms to a confirmed 1250ms, in the same ballpark as every short-heading usage elsewhere. `text` mode is completely unaffected (no existing heading/eyebrow comes anywhere near 10 words).
- **The `<h2>` had no reveal animation either** (owner, same day: "should have the same animation as we applied on some titles on homepage"). Wrapped in `TextReveal` on plain `text` mode, the exact same shape every homepage section heading already uses (`WhatWeMake`, `CertifiedCompliant`, `HowItWorks`, etc: `<TextReveal as="h2"/"span" text={...} />`) — no new prop or variant needed, just applying the existing, already-correct pattern that had been missed when this section was first built. Confirmed live: renders 5 `.reveal-word` spans, `aria-label="How we work with you"`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` all clean. Live on `/services` at 1440px: cards grid measures 3× ~397px columns totaling 1280px (`container-p`'s own inner width); all 3 card titles and their "What it means"/"Best for" copy render exactly; closing note renders both `<strong>` segments matching Figma's own bold phrases; no horizontal overflow at 375px.

- **"Explore" link hover fixed from dark to light orange** (owner: "on hover on the explore cta make it light orange not dark") — `hover:opacity-80` (the sitewide default hover treatment) dims a colour toward the page background, which lightens it on every other hover link (all on light/paper backgrounds) but DARKENS it here, since this link sits on `bg-ink`. Fixed with `hover:brightness-125`, which lightens the actual orange instead of blending it toward black — no new colour token needed.
- **Chevron nudges right on hover** (owner: "on the hover chevron should have the same animation we applied on pdp, related styles") — reuses PDP Related Styles' own established chip-chevron treatment verbatim (`productRelatedStyles.chipIcon`'s `group-hover:translate-x-0.5`), not a new one-off: `exploreLink` gained `group`, `exploreIcon` gained `transition-transform group-hover:translate-x-0.5`.

- **`NextArrowIcon`'s viewBox cropped to the glyph's real bounds, fixing an off-centre chevron, 2026-09-07 (owner: "the chveron is not 100% center aligned to the text cta label, make it fully centre aligned")** — not a flexbox bug (`exploreLink`'s `items-center` was already correctly centring the icon's own bounding *box* against the text); the glyph's path only spans y 4–15.6667 within Figma's exported `0 0 7 16.6667` viewBox (confirmed by parsing every coordinate in `d`, not eyeballed) — an asymmetric ~4px top / ~1px bottom margin baked into the export frame, so the *box* was centred but the *ink* inside it visibly sat low. Fixed at the source: `NextArrowIcon.tsx`'s viewBox is now `0 4 7 11.6667`, cropped exactly to the path's own bounds, with no change to the path data itself (same shape, same rendered ink size — every consuming `h-*` class shrinks by the identical proportion the viewBox does, `16.667px` → `11.667px`, so the glyph's actual on-screen pixel size is unchanged, only the invisible padding around it is gone). `exploreIcon` updated to `h-[11.667px]` to match. `WhatWeMake`'s own `desktopGridCtaIcon` (same icon, reused there for its desktop "View All" CTA — see that entry) had already worked around this identical report with a manual `-translate-y-[1.5px]` compensating nudge; that's now removed and its height corrected the same way, since stacking a manual nudge on top of the now-fixed icon would just introduce a new offset in the other direction.

Copy (eyebrow, heading, both card descriptions and "Explore" labels) is the design's own real text layer, read directly — not placeholder copy.

Re-checked `729:363` directly: content starts 160px from the frame's own top (correct, unchanged) and `2660 − 160 − 2360 = 140px` remains below the tallest card column. Confirmed live post-fix: Services measures `160px/140px`, homepage's own default `pageVariant="home"` still measures unchanged `120px/120px`.

`npm run build`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` all clean.

### Certified & Compliant — Built

Owner, 2026-09-07: "Add this section under how it works, same as we use on homepage" (Figma node `729:276`, "Certifications"). The exact same section as homepage's `CertifiedCompliant` (same eyebrow "CERTIFIED & COMPLIANT," same heading "Audited for quality, safety, environment, and ethics," all 6 real cert logos) — not a new component, not a copy of one. Desktop needed no override: this Figma frame's own 120px top / 120px bottom already match `certified.desktopSection`'s existing values exactly. `app/services/page.tsx` renders `<CertifiedCompliant content={home.certified} pageVariant="services" />`, the same `home.certified` content object the homepage itself uses.

Read "under how it works" as "further down the page from How It Works" (which it is — both `FinalCta` and this section sit below it), the same "trust `get_metadata`'s real y-order over a literal reading of the request" call made throughout this page's build.

On `/services`, this section instead follows the mid-page `FinalCta` usage, whose own ticker'd mobile block owns only 60px of bottom padding (`finalCta.mobileCtaBlock`'s `pb-[60px]`, correct for that component's own context, not a shared section-boundary value) — landing a real 60px mobile gap here instead of the standard 72px. `pageVariant="services"` swaps in `certified.mobileSectionServices` (`pt-[72px] pb-[72px]`), a real top value of its own rather than touching `finalCta`'s unrelated 60px. Default `"default"` (homepage) is byte-for-byte unchanged — confirmed live: 0px unaffected on `/`, 72px on `/services`, both at 375px.

Confirmed via SSR output: eyebrow/heading render correctly, all 6 logos (`ISO 9001`, `ISO 45001`, `ISO 14001`, `BSCI`, `IMAC`, `WFSGI`) present, section sits after `FinalCta` in DOM order.

## Activewear category PLP (product listing page)

The reusable, data-driven template for every Activewear category page. Route: `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`. Content shape: `content/activewear/types.ts`'s `Category` type; one file per category (e.g. `content/activewear/leggings.ts`) registered in `content/activewear/categories.ts`. The page itself has no category copy typed into it — everything renders from the matching `Category` object, including metadata (title, description, canonical, OpenGraph). Reuses the homepage's exact `Header`/nav, no PLP-specific nav variant.

Being built section by section against Figma node `406:3075` (owner note 2026-08-28: "still half of it been designed"), banner first. Style card content is placeholder pending real product names and photography.

**Gotcha for every future category page or section:** this route is a nested child segment of the root layout, so the layout's `title.template` ("%s | Capriowear") applies automatically — `Category.metaTitle` must NOT carry its own "| Capriowear" suffix (doing so doubles it in the rendered `<title>`; found and fixed 2026-08-28). `openGraph.title` is not templated, so `generateMetadata` builds it explicitly with the suffix. See the doc-comment on `Category.metaTitle`.

### CategoryBanner — Built
`components/sections/CategoryBanner.tsx` · recipe: `categoryBanner`

Breadcrumb, H1, and a row of muted trust bullets (small sparkle glyph) on an ink band. Figma: desktop node `502:3310` (1440×400) — **revised twice on 2026-08-28**, this is the current, third real version of this section (originally a centred ink band with a single "quick answer" subline; briefly a white/black-text/orange-checkmark version; now back to ink/white-text with muted sparkle bullets).

Props: `breadcrumbItems`, `h1`, `trustBullets` (the earlier `quickAnswer` field is gone entirely — nothing in any current design renders it). Renders the shared `Breadcrumb` component (5j) as its own first child, `tone="dark"` — reused, not a second copy of breadcrumb markup — so the page no longer renders a separate `<Breadcrumb>` beneath this section. `h1` renders via `TextReveal` (5b, owner request 2026-08-29) — the same word-by-word entrance already on Hero's H1, not a new animation; always above the fold, so it plays on mount.

**`Breadcrumb`'s own `tone` prop** (`"light" | "dark"`, default `"light"`): `tone="light"`'s `current` (`text-ink`) and `link`'s `hover:text-ink` are both near-black, invisible against this section's ink background. `tone="dark"` swaps in `currentDark` (`#abb5c0`, Figma's own real value for the current-page item here) and `linkDark` (`hover:text-paper`).

**Layout matched Figma's original two-absolute-layers construction exactly**: `breadcrumbWrap` is `absolute inset-x-0 top-0` (the breadcrumb overlays the top, out of normal flow); `contentWrap` centred the H1+bullets group on the banner's *full* height. A first attempt put the content in normal flow after the breadcrumb with `flex-1 items-center`, which centres in the *leftover* space below the breadcrumb instead — `get_metadata` shows the content block's own vertical midpoint sits exactly at half the banner's total height, not half of what's left after the breadcrumb; live-measured, that first attempt gave an `109px` breadcrumb-to-H1 gap against Figma's real `80px` (owner caught this directly: "what's the gap from breadcrumbs to the title?"). Corrected, then measured exactly `80px`.

**Resized 2026-08-29, owner's own Figma edit**: `section` is now `h-[340px]` (was `400px`). The content block is no longer pure-centred either — the owner's revised frame positions it at `top: calc(50% + 68px)` with `-translate-y-1/2`, not `items-center` on the full height; `contentWrap` reproduces that literal offset now. This measures out to a real 40px gap between the last trust bullet and the banner's bottom edge, matching the owner's own description exactly ("banner size, content placement on the banner from bottom 40px gap").

The 54px/400-weight/64px-line-height H1 is a fixed px value, not the sitewide fluid `text-h1` clamp token — confirmed via Figma that this design's H1 is a genuinely different weight than the sitewide H1 scale (same size/line-height, coincidentally, but Regular not Medium), not a rounding of it. `whitespace-nowrap` stays gated to `xl:` (owner request, "the title should be in 1 line") — applied unconditionally it caused real mobile overflow in an earlier pass (`578px` scrollWidth against a `390px` viewport) by giving the H1 an unshrinkable min-content width; gating to `xl` keeps the guaranteed single line on every real desktop width while mobile falls back to safe wrapping. No mobile Figma frame exists yet, so this and the trust-bullet row's own `flex-wrap` remain safe structural fallbacks, not confirmed mobile designs.

**Real mobile overflow bug fixed (owner report, 2026-08-30, "fix the nav")**: `section` was an unconditional fixed `h-[340px]` with `contentWrap` unconditionally `absolute`-positioned. Absolute positioning removes an element from the flow a fixed-height parent would otherwise need to grow for — on mobile widths, the wrapped 4-line trust-bullet row plus H1 simply overflowed past the box's own bottom edge, visually bleeding into the white `ProductGrid` section below. Fixed structurally, not by shrinking any type size (this is still desktop-only typography, unchanged): below `xl`, `section` is `h-auto` with real `pt`/`pb` padding and both `breadcrumbWrap`/`contentWrap` are `static` (normal flow, box grows to fit); at `xl` the original fixed-height/absolute-centred desktop layout is untouched byte-for-byte. Confirmed live at 375×812 — clean break to the section below, no overlap — and confirmed the `xl` box still measures exactly `340px`, unchanged.

**Breadcrumb hidden below `md`, CSS-only, not unmounted** (owner request, 2026-08-30: "hide the breadcrumb visually on mobile only... shows from tablet up... Do NOT remove the breadcrumb from the HTML"): `breadcrumbWrap` gained `hidden md:block` ahead of its existing `max-xl:static xl:absolute xl:inset-x-0 xl:top-0` — the component itself renders the exact same `<Breadcrumb items={breadcrumbItems} tone="dark" />` at every viewport; only `display` changes below 768px. Deliberately `md`, not this page's usual `xl` mobile/desktop split. Confirmed independently that the page's `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD (`app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`) is unaffected — it's a sibling `<JsonLd>` built straight from `data.menuLabel`/`data.slug`, not from this visible markup — and that the PLP still has an unrelated, always-crawlable `/activewear` link via `Header`'s own nav (`home.nav.links` → real `next/link`), so hiding this strip doesn't remove the page's only upward internal link.

Trust-bullet glyph is lucide's `Sparkle` (`fill="currentColor"`) — the same 4-point shape this project already uses for `Marquee`'s separator and `Header`'s mega-menu promo bullets, reused rather than downloading a third copy of the same asset as a new icon file. The previous revision's `CircularTickIcon` (orange checkmark) had exactly one call site, which no longer wants it — deleted, not left orphaned. **Text size reduced to 20px** (2026-08-29, owner note: "reduced the font size to 20 for moq 50 all texts") — was `1.375rem` (22px), now `1.25rem`, one shared style across all four bullets. **Colour corrected to `#abb5c0`** (2026-08-30, owner: "make the banner text under the title and the star icon... this color") — was `#838d97`, applies equally to the icon and all four bullet strings.

**H1 threshold moved xl:/1280px → md:/768px, including `xl:whitespace-nowrap` (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — fixed 30px/38px mobile → fixed 54px/64px desktop, both literal (not the sitewide fluid `text-h1` — see this heading's own note above for why). The single-line `whitespace-nowrap` moved with it, which is the real risk here (a real mobile overflow bug already happened once when this was applied unconditionally, see above) — confirmed live, no overflow at 768px or 1024px on a real category page (`/activewear/t-shirts`, "T-Shirts" heading). `xl:` desktop (1440px) and real mobile (375px) both unaffected.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`, seeded with `content/activewear/leggings.ts` (`/activewear/leggings`).

**Not yet built:** style card grid pagination beyond page 1's own content, Overview, customization strip, fabric table, trust block, spec quick-facts table, FAQ, related links, final CTA. JSON-LD (ItemList, FAQPage) and the `app/sitemap.ts` entry are deferred until the sections that need them (style cards, FAQ) are built; `breadcrumbSchema()` is separately ready whenever this page gets its own JSON-LD pass (the pattern is already established on the homepage).

### CategoryFilters — Built
`components/sections/CategoryFilters.tsx` · recipe: `categoryFilters` · icons: `components/icons/FilterChevronIcon.tsx`, lucide-react's `SlidersHorizontal`/`X`

The PLP's left-panel category accordion (Figma node `406:3085`, "Filters", 2026-08-28; restyled 2026-08-29, node `573:5457`) — a 252px list of the same 5 category groups already in the mega menu, the current page's item in accent orange.

**Restyled 2026-08-29** (owner: "Changed the filter style a bit"): heading relabelled "Categories" → "Filters", enlarged to `1.375rem` (22px, was 18px) and left-aligned (was centred); the panel lost its outer bounding box (`list` no longer carries `border-x border-t` — only the row-to-row dividers remain); row padding increased `20px` → `24px` throughout (`groupHeaderCollapsed`, `groupHeaderExpanded`, `itemList`).

**Sticky mechanic confirmed against a live reference** (owner: "follow the website reference for filters fixed behavior. Follow exactly the same," `gymshark.com/collections/leggings/womens`). Inspected their desktop filter column directly (scripted Playwright check): plain `position: sticky` with a fixed `top: 90px`, locking with zero drift once stuck — exactly this component's own mechanic already. One deliberate, called-out deviation: their panel clips overflow (`overflow-y: hidden`) rather than scrolling it, relying on most of their filter groups being collapsed by default so it rarely matters — this project's own groups all start open (see below), so clipping would hide real categories outright; `overflow-y-auto` stays.

**Lock offset dropped to `top-[24px]` (2026-08-29 trial)** — was `top-[111px]` (87px header + 24px gap). Following the reference check above, the owner asked to trial Gymshark's own look one time ("let's try one time gymshark approach and see how it looks"): their filter panel locks flush at the bare viewport top only because their site header is non-sticky (scrolls away entirely). `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx` now renders `Header` with `sticky={false}` for this page only (see Header's own entry above) — with no persistent header left to clear, `list`'s offset became a plain `24px` breathing gap (`max-h` adjusted to match, `calc(100vh-48px)`), matching the reference's own flush lock.

Props: `activeSlug` (e.g. `"leggings"`) plus, since Teamwear (owner spec, 2026-09-05), three optional overrides — `menuGroups?` (defaults to `activewearMegaMenu`), `basePath?` (defaults to `"/activewear"`), `ariaLabel?` (defaults to `"Activewear categories"`) — so `app/teamwear/[sport]/page.tsx`/`[style]/page.tsx` pass `teamwearMegaMenu`/`"/teamwear"`/`"Teamwear categories"` and get the exact same accordion rendering Teamwear's own category list, while every existing Activewear call site (which passes none of the three) renders byte-identical to before. The trailing "Teamwear & Uniforms →" cross-link (and its old `teamwearHref` prop) was removed 2026-08-28 (owner call) — this panel now genuinely supports either group's own data via the props above, not by re-adding a cross-link. `activewearMegaMenu`'s/`teamwearMegaMenu`'s labels are both stored upper-case for the mega menu's own small-caps display; this component's own `toTitleCase()` helper re-cases them for this design's plain title-case labels, rather than storing a second, differently-cased copy in the shared data.

**Client component, a real accordion, every group open by default** (owner call, 2026-08-28: "by default open all the sub-categories") — corrects the first build's single-open-at-a-time behaviour (`useState<string | null>`, closing whichever group was open when a new one expanded). State is now a `Set<string>` of open labels, seeded from every group's own label (`activewearMegaMenu.map(g => g.label)`) so a future 6th group opens by default too, with no second place to remember to update; toggling one group no longer affects any other. Both chevron states are the same Figma glyph (confirmed by downloading and diffing the two exported assets — identical path, only fill colour differs) — one icon component, colour and 180° rotation both driven by the caller.

**Outer border colour corrected to `#e8ecf1`** (owner call, 2026-08-28, "use the new color") — every other border in this list (the "Categories" header, each group row, the expanded-group wrap) already used this exact hex; only the outer box had drifted to an unconfirmed `#f2f4f8`.

Every row owns only its own bottom border, not a shared one (edge-ownership technique, same as the adjacent grid-cell borders elsewhere on the site — `05-plan.md`'s 2026-08-24 decision log entry) — confirmed live, 0px gaps between every row, no doubling. **An open group's header carries no border of its own** (corrected 2026-08-28, owner report) — the header and its item list share one outer border as a single box (`groupWrapExpanded`, matching Figma node `510:3326`'s own "Container"), not two separately bordered pieces; a border on the header alone read as a stray underline right beneath the open category's label.

**Sticky panel** (owner request, 2026-08-28): `categoryFilters.list` is `sticky top-[111px] self-start` — 111px is the header's real height (87px) plus a 24px gap. `self-start` matters once this sits beside the future product grid in a flex row: without it, a flex child stretches to match its tallest sibling's height, which breaks sticky (nothing left to stick against).

**Internal scroll added so the panel reads as truly fixed, not moving** (owner report, 2026-08-29: "the left panel should be fixed, not move while scrolling the products"). Now that the page has real height (9 style cards, 3 pages), the earlier "nowhere to demonstrate sticky yet" note no longer applies — but every category group starts expanded (see above), which makes the panel's own content taller than most viewports. A sticky element taller than the viewport still lets its own overflow scroll past once pinned, which reads as "the panel is moving" even though the CSS is correct. Fixed by adding `max-h-[calc(100vh-135px)] overflow-y-auto` to `categoryFilters.list` — the panel itself stays pinned below the header; if its content is taller than the visible space, it scrolls internally instead of drifting with the page.

**"Categories" header row** (Figma node `406:3087`, added 2026-08-28 — missing from every build until now): centred, 18px/medium/26px line-height, its own bottom border (`categoryFilters.header`) — the sidebar's real first row.

**Top-aligned with the product cards, not the heading above them** (owner request, 2026-08-28, with a Figma screenshot): `categoryFilters.list` gained `xl:mt-20` (80px) — correct at the time, since `CategoryMetaStrip`'s title+chip row still lived inside `ProductGrid`'s own column then, sitting above the grid within the same flex item.

**That offset removed again on 2026-08-29** once 5v moved `CategoryMetaStrip` out to a full-width sibling above both the sidebar and the grid (see its own entry below): with the title row no longer inside the grid's column, the grid's own top starts at the same y as the sidebar's with no offset needed — the leftover `mt-20` then pushed the sidebar `80px` below the grid (live-measured: sidebar top `685px` vs. grid top `605px`, owner report: "the left panel should be top aligned with the product grid"). `categoryFilters.list` carries no top-offset class today; live-measured, sidebar top and grid top compute to the exact same value.

**Smooth expand/collapse** (owner report, 2026-08-28, "its jerky"): the item list no longer conditionally mounts/unmounts. `itemListGrid`/`itemListGridOpen` toggle a `grid-template-rows` `0fr`↔`1fr` track (300ms transition) — the standard zero-JS-measurement accordion technique, same "CSS-only motion" precedent as `Marquee`/`TextReveal`. `chevron` also gained `transition-transform` so its rotation eases with the panel. **Real bug found building this**: `overflow-hidden` directly on the padded `<ul>` never actually reached 0px — it floored at exactly the `<ul>`'s own padding (36px), since padding isn't shrinkable content. Fixed with an extra unpadded `itemListClip` wrapper (`overflow-hidden`) between the grid track and the padded `<ul>`, so the padding sits inside the element being clipped, not on it.

**Sub-category hover** (owner request, 2026-08-28): `item`'s hover is `hover:text-accent` (was `hover:text-ink`) — the primary orange, not a dark-text hover.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`, directly beneath `CategoryBanner`, inside a `container-p` wrapper owned by the page (not the component), now sitting beside `ProductGrid` in that same row. Also `app/teamwear/[sport]/page.tsx`/`[style]/page.tsx` (owner spec, 2026-09-05, Teamwear launch), passing `menuGroups={teamwearMegaMenu}` `basePath="/teamwear"` `ariaLabel="Teamwear categories"`.

**Teamwear group (owner spec, 2026-09-05)** — `app/teamwear/[sport]/page.tsx` and `app/teamwear/[sport]/[style]/page.tsx` are new PLP/PDP route files, structured identically to their Activewear counterparts and importing every shared component/schema builder/content helper (`FabricOptions`, `categoryEntityFaq`, `buildCtaSubline`, `pdpFaqOperational`, `pdpCustomizationSteps`, `pdpSpecHighlights`, `breadcrumbSchema`/`collectionPageSchema`/`productSchema`/`faqSchema`) from the exact same places the Activewear routes already import them from — nothing in either file is a second copy of that logic, only the group label ("Teamwear"), base path (`/teamwear`) and content registry (`content/teamwear/sports.ts`, not `content/activewear/categories.ts`) differ. `Category`/`StyleCard` (`content/activewear/types.ts`) needed no shape change — the existing `group: string` field (present since the type's first build, unused by any page render until now) is what marks a category as belonging to Teamwear rather than Activewear. Breadcrumb trail: Home → Teamwear → [Sport] → [Style]. `app/sitemap.ts` gained a second enumeration block reading `content/teamwear/sports.ts`, the same "read straight from the registry, never a second hand-typed list" pattern the Activewear block already uses. Cricket (`content/teamwear/cricket.ts`) is the first sport; adding a second sport is adding its own content file and registering it in `content/teamwear/sports.ts`, touching neither route file, same "content file plus one registry line" pattern Activewear categories already follow.

**Mobile: FAB + bottom sheet, not the in-flow panel (2026-08-30, owner: reference screenshots of a Babyshop-style filter FAB and bottom sheet)** — below `xl` the sticky sidebar above is `hidden` outright (`categoryFilters.list` is now `hidden xl:sticky xl:block ...`, no more `max-xl:*` gating); a fixed "Filter" pill (`categoryFilters.fab`, `SlidersHorizontal` icon) is the only mobile-visible trace of this component. Tapping it opens the exact same category groups — still plain nav `Link`s, no checkboxes, since there's no togglable filter *state* here, only navigation to a different category page — in a bottom sheet (`categoryFilters.drawerPanel`: `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0`, `max-h-[85vh]`, square corners — no `rounded-*` at all, per owner correction 2026-08-30) over a dimmed backdrop (`drawerBackdrop`, `bg-ink/40`, tap to dismiss), with a decorative top drag-handle bar. Picking a sub-category navigates straight to that page, which unmounts the drawer along with it — no separate "Apply"/"Clear all" affordance, since nothing here is a selectable filter.

The accordion body itself (`openLabels` state + the `activewearMegaMenu.map(...)` render) is factored into a shared `FilterGroupList` local component so the desktop sidebar and the mobile sheet render identical open/close behaviour from one place — each instance keeps its own independent state, since the two are never visible at the same time.

The sheet reuses `MobileNav.tsx`'s own proven overlay mechanic rather than a new one: portal to `document.body` (escapes `Header`'s `sticky z-40` stacking context), a hand-rolled focus trap (focus in on open, Tab/Shift+Tab cycled inside the panel, Escape closes, focus returns to the FAB on close), background scroll lock while open, and a `rendered`/`revealed` two-state dance (`CLOSE_TRANSITION_MS = 300`, matched to `drawerPanel`'s own `duration-300`) so the closing slide-down transition finishes before the portal unmounts. It's a new component (`FilterFabAndDrawer`, colocated in `CategoryFilters.tsx`) rather than a variant folded into `MobileNav`, per this project's component rule: different trigger (a bottom FAB vs. the header's "Menu" pill), different content (this category list vs. the nav's links/mega-menu/contact/social), different shape (a light `bg-paper` bottom sheet with a visible backdrop vs. the nav's dark, backdrop-less full-screen takeover).

**FAB sits `bottom-[72px]`, not `bottom-6` (owner, 2026-09-02: "filters should be above the cta, have a gap of 12px")** — the FAB is `fixed`, always anchored to the viewport bottom, while `ProductCtasMobileBar`'s own `productCtas.mobileBar` (rendered as the mobile sticky "Request a Sample" bar at the end of `<main>`) is a 60px-tall `sticky bottom-0` bar that's part of the same page's flow; on a short page the two can be on screen together. 72px = the bar's own 60px height + 12px, giving the FAB a clean 12px gap above the bar instead of sitting inside it (was `bottom-6`/24px, which put the FAB's own box inside the bar's 60px height). Verified live: 12px gap, both PLP viewports.

`SlidersHorizontal`/`X` are imported directly from `lucide-react` rather than added to `components/icons/` — that directory reproduces glyphs exactly from a Figma export (see `MenuIcon.tsx`'s own comment on why it deviates from lucide's Menu icon for that reason), and there's no Figma node for this new mobile-only pattern to match; this project already imports lucide icons directly elsewhere (`X` in `MobileNav.tsx` itself, `Header.tsx`, `Accordion.tsx`).

**Deliberately left `xl:`-gated, not extended to `md:` (owner, 2026-09-04, PLP/PDP tablet-width pass)** — this sidebar is a fixed `252px` column beside `ProductGrid`'s own 3-column grid; the FAB+bottom-sheet mobile pattern above (no in-flow sidebar at all — no Figma tablet frame exists for one either) is what actually shows below `xl` today, including at 768–1279px tablet width. Forcing the fixed sidebar into a 768px container alongside a 3-column grid would overflow (the grid's own card width can't support that combined layout at that width); fitting both needs a dedicated tablet-specific sidebar/grid redesign, not a same-day judgment call. `ProductGrid`'s own typography (`productCard.title`/`subline`) was already moved to `md:` in this same pass — only the column-count/sidebar structure stays deferred.
