Re-checked against the file as it stands now (`get_metadata`/`get_screenshot` on node `406:3075`): the text starts at the exact same x as the product grid (`x=380` = 252px sidebar + 48px gap past the container's left edge), with nothing above the "Categories" panel at all. `categoryMetaStrip.text` now carries `xl:ml-[300px]` (252 + 48) to match; `chipRow` was left unchanged.

**Top gap corrected to match Figma** (owner report, 2026-08-29: "follow the same gap from the top of the legging heading as in design"). Measured directly from Figma (`get_metadata` on node `406:3075` vs. the isolated banner node `502:3310`): the banner's own bottom edge sits at `470px`, this row's own top at `526px` — a real `56px` gap, confirmed by sampling pixel rows on a rendered screenshot of the combined frame. Replaces the previous `pt-5` (20px), which was never checked against Figma. `root` is now `pt-14` (56px). Re-verified against the owner's later banner resize (400px → 340px, same day): the recomputed gap from the revised frame's own coordinates is still exactly `56px`, so `pt-14` needed no further change.

**Bottom gap corrected to 32px, not 40px** — `pb-10` (40px) was the owner's own estimate ("the gap from chips men, women and the product tiles is 40px"); once the owner revised the Figma frame itself, re-measuring against the file (`get_design_context` on node `406:3075`) showed the title+chip row and the filters+grid row are two children of one `flex-col gap-[32px]` container, a real 32px. Corrected to `pb-8` now that the file is the source of truth rather than the earlier eyeballed number.

**Bottom gap corrected to 40px** (owner report, same day: "the gap from chips men, women and the product tiles is 40px"). `root`'s bottom padding was `pb-6` (24px) — 16px short. Now `pb-10` (40px); since the filters+grid row below carries no extra top margin of its own, this padding IS the full gap between the chip row and the sidebar/grid top. Live-measured: this row's own bottom edge, the sidebar's top, and the grid's top all compute to the exact same value.

**`title`/`subline` font-size threshold moved max-xl:/xl: → max-md:/md: (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — fixed values, desktop always larger. `text`'s own `xl:ml-[300px]`/`xl:flex-row` layout switch is unchanged, still coupled to `CategoryFilters`' 252px sidebar, itself deliberately left `xl:`-gated (see `docs/05-plan.md`'s PLP/PDP tablet pass entry) — so at tablet width this row stays stacked (`max-xl:flex-col`), just with the bigger desktop text sizes inside it.

Title/subline alignment and subline colour carry over unchanged from the pre-split `ProductGrid` build (see the plan log, 5-series entries, 2026-08-28) — see the recipe's own comments in `components/ui/styles.ts` for the exact reasoning.

**Title is now `<h2>`, not `<p>`** (SEO/AEO/GEO finalization pass, 2026-08-30): this is the product grid section's own real title, and every other section on the page (`WhatWeCover`, `TrustPoints`, `FabricOptions`, `Faq`) already has its own `<h2>` — this text staying a plain paragraph was a real gap in the page's heading outline, not a deliberate styling choice. `className` unchanged, so nothing visual moved.

**Real mobile layout, from node `590:1173`, 2026-08-30** (owner: "the men women filters should be above the title as in the design"): this row's plain DOM order (text, then `chipRow`) had never matched Figma's mobile frame, where the chip row is its own full-width block ABOVE the title/subline block — once `flex-wrap` stacked the two below `xl`, the title always rendered first. Fixed with `order` on both children, gated to `max-xl:` only: `chipRow` gets `max-xl:order-1`, `text` gets `max-xl:order-2` — at `xl` neither element is stacked (they sit side by side via `justify-between`), so the order classes do nothing there and the confirmed desktop layout is untouched. `text` itself also gained a real mobile stacked-column layout (`max-xl:flex-col max-xl:items-start max-xl:gap-2`) — node 590:1173's own "Title/Pills" frame has the subline directly below the title, not sharing a baseline the way desktop's confirmed row layout does (`xl:flex-row xl:items-baseline xl:gap-3` stays exactly as before). `title`/`subline` also gained their own real mobile type sizes from the same node (24px/28px-line-height and 18px/24px-line-height respectively) — were the desktop 30px/20px sizes unconditionally; `xl:` keeps those confirmed desktop values.

**Chip row gained real mobile spacing and a full-bleed separator** (owner, same day: "the space from top of the chips should be 24px, under the chips there is a separator as shown in design, it should be 24px gap too from the chips"). `root`'s mobile `pt`/`gap`/`pb` are now all `24px` (`max-xl:pt-6 max-xl:gap-6 max-xl:pb-6`, was the desktop `pt-14`/`gap-4`/`pb-8` unconditionally) — the one shared `gap` value does double duty, both the chips-to-separator gap and the separator-to-title gap below it (confirmed live: no other padding/margin anywhere in this row doubles either one). `chipRow` gained `max-xl:border-b` (node 590:1173's own "Chip" frame border) plus `max-xl:pb-6` for the 24px space above that line. **The separator is full-bleed, not inset** (owner correction, same day: "the chips separator should be 360px" — the full mobile frame width, not `container-p`'s own 20px side inset this row sits inside): `chipRow` breaks out with `max-xl:-mx-5 max-xl:w-[calc(100%+40px)] max-xl:px-5` — the negative margin/width pairing reaches the true viewport edges for the border, while the matching `px-5` keeps the chip pills themselves exactly where they'd sit without the breakout.

**Reversed the same day: chips moved back under the title, separator removed** (owner: "let's put it under the title not on the top without the separator"). Deleted the `order-1`/`order-2` flip on `text`/`chipRow` entirely — plain DOM order (title/subline first, chips second, matching `CategoryMetaStrip.tsx`'s own JSX order) already puts chips under the title once `flex-wrap` stacks below `xl`, so no override is needed. Deleted `chipRow`'s `border-b`/`-mx-5` breakout/`pb-6` too — `root`'s existing `max-xl:gap-6` is now the single gap between title and chips.

**New per-category `showGenderFilter` flag** (owner, same message: "this chips may come on some of the categories but not applicable for all. Keep it in your system with a flag use or not to use when asked"). `Category.showGenderFilter?: boolean` (`content/activewear/types.ts`) threads through a matching `CategoryMetaStrip` prop of the same name (default `true`) and `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`; the chip row now renders conditionally (`{showGenderFilter && (...)}`). `leggings.ts` sets it explicitly `true`. A future category with no gender split sets `showGenderFilter: false` in its own content file — no component change needed.

**`subline` now uses the `text-subline` token (`#17191E`)**, not `text-ink` (2026-09-01, see `docs/02-design-system.md`'s Colour section and the decision log) — the same sitewide title+subline colour rule applied to `TrustPoints`' own subline the same day.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`, directly above the `CategoryFilters`/`ProductGrid` row.

### RelatedCategories — Built, not currently used on the PLP
`components/sections/RelatedCategories.tsx` · recipe: `relatedCategories`

Internal-linking row (SEO/AEO rule 6, `docs/06-seo.md`: link related pages from real body copy, not just the nav) — built 2026-08-30 as part of the Leggings PLP's SEO/AEO/GEO finalization pass. No Figma frame exists for this row, so it's deliberately plain: an `<h2>` ("Explore more Activewear categories") plus a `<ul>` of `Button` (secondary variant) links, built entirely from existing tokens/components rather than an invented visual design.

**Removed from `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx` 2026-08-30** (owner: "remove explore more activewear section from the PLP") — the component, its recipe, and `Category.relatedLinks` all stay, since the styleguide (`app/styleguide/page.tsx`) still renders it in isolation for QA and nothing else depended on the page-level removal. Re-adding it later is a one-line import + JSX call, same as before. This does leave SEO/AEO rule 6 (internal linking in real body copy) unfulfilled on the PLP again until another mechanism covers it — worth a look next time that page's SEO is revisited.

Props: `links: RelatedLink[]` (`{label, href}`, `content/activewear/types.ts`). `content/activewear/leggings.ts`'s own `relatedLinks` holds the real 4 sibling categories (Sports Bras, Shorts, Joggers & Track Pants, Compression & Base Layers), matching `content/home.ts`'s own `activewearMegaMenu` labels/hrefs exactly.

**Used by:** `app/styleguide/page.tsx` only, for now.

### ProductGrid / ProductCard / Pagination — Built
`components/sections/ProductGrid.tsx` · `components/ProductCard.tsx` · `components/Pagination.tsx` · recipes: `productGrid`, `productCard`, `pagination`

**Desktop hover image swap (owner spec, 2026-09-02), scoped for performance** — `ProductCard`'s image box is `components/ProductCardMedia.tsx`, a new client component, not `MediaPlaceholder` directly. It reads **only** `images[0]` (default) and `images[1]` (hover) off a `StyleCard` — never the rest of that style's own gallery array, which stays the PDP's own `ProductGallery` (a fully separate render); falls back to the older flat `image`/`imageAlt` fields as `images[0]`'s equivalent for a card with no `images` array yet. On desktop hover it cross-fades to `images[1]` (`transition-opacity duration-[175ms]`, inside the requested 150–200ms range) via plain CSS (`group-hover:opacity-0`/`opacity-100`) — no JS-driven hover state, so fade-out on mouse-leave comes for free from the same rule. Gated two ways, both required before the second image is even mounted in the DOM: (1) `window.matchMedia("(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)")`, checked once via a `useState` lazy initializer (not `useEffect` + `setState`, which is a real lint error — `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`, a synchronous `setState` inside an effect body) — a touch device that happens to be wide (most tablets) never mounts `images[1]`, confirmed live: 0 hover-capable devices load a second `<img>` at all; (2) an `IntersectionObserver` (`rootMargin: "200px"`) per card, so a card still below the fold never requests `images[1]` either — only once a card has scrolled near the viewport AND hover is possible does the second `<Image>` mount, pre-loaded and ready so the very first hover swaps instantly rather than fetching on demand. `images[0]` itself still uses `next/image` with a `sizes` hint matched to the grid's own real column counts (`"(min-width: 1280px) 33vw, 50vw"`, 3 desktop / 2 mobile) and default lazy loading, so a card tile never fetches a full-viewport-resolution image. `next.config.ts` gained `images.formats: ["image/avif", "image/webp"]` (was Next's own default of `["image/webp"]` only) so every real `next/image` sitewide, not just this one, gets AVIF ahead of WebP where the browser supports it. No visual change today — no `StyleCard` has a real `src` yet on either `images[0]` or `images[1]` (site-wide, no real photography exists), so every card still renders the same empty-state placeholder; verified the actual swap mechanism live with two temporary local test images (`/logos/gymreapers.png`/`/logos/gymbeam.png`), confirmed the cross-fade fired on a real mouse hover and reverted back to `alt`-only afterward, then confirmed a mobile/touch viewport mounts exactly one `<img>` (`images[0]` only).

The PLP's product tile grid (Figma node `406:3137`, "Product Container", 2026-08-28) — the grid and pagination. The meta strip (category title + subline + gender filter chips) this used to render inline moved out to `CategoryMetaStrip` (2026-08-29, see above).

**Row-to-row gap corrected to 40px** (owner, 2026-08-29: "spacing between the product card from one row to another should be 40px gap"). `grid` had one uniform `gap-4` (16px) both ways; Figma only ever specifies 40px between rows (each subsequent "Products List" row carries its own `pt-[40px]`), with 16px staying the gap between cards within the same row. Split into `gap-x-4 gap-y-10` to match both values exactly.

Figma shows 9 duplicate placeholder tiles (3 rows × 3, plus a 4th empty/unused layer per row — a stray artifact, not a real 4th tile), all identical copy with no real per-tile data. `ProductGrid` renders one real `ProductCard` per however many entries `content/activewear/leggings.ts`'s own `styleCards` actually has — 9 today (owner request, 2026-08-28, to fill the grid's real 3×3 size), never invented duplicates beyond what's typed in content — matching this project's "no category copy typed a second time" rule. New `Category` field `gridSubline` (real Figma copy, "Every style, made to your brand spec.") and a new `cardSubline` field on `StyleCard` (the per-tile "Available in custom fabrics & colors" line). Neither `cardTitle` nor `cardSubline` carries the literal `"PLACEHOLDER:"` prefix (owner request, 2026-08-28) — they're the only two `Category` fields actually rendered on the page today; every other placeholder field (`overview`, `fabricOptions`, `specQuickFacts`, `faqs`) keeps its prefix, since nothing renders them yet.

**Published vs draft cards (owner spec, 2026-09-02, Leggings PLP pilot)** — `StyleCard` gained a required `status: "published" | "draft"` field (`content/activewear/types.ts`), no default, same "state it explicitly" pattern `Eyebrow`'s own `tone` prop already uses. `ProductCard` reads it directly: a `"published"` card renders exactly as before (a real `next/link` `Link` to its PDP). A `"draft"` card — a style with a real name and one-line spec but no PDP content yet — renders the identical visual tile as a plain, non-interactive `<div>` instead, `aria-disabled="true"`, its `MediaPlaceholder` at `opacity-60` (`productCard.draftImage`).

**Card format rewrite, same day, owner spec**: a card shows only the image placeholder, an `h3` title in the form "Custom [Style] Leggings" (`cardTitle`, e.g. "Custom High-Waisted Compression Leggings"), and one distinguishing spec line beneath it (`cardSubline`, desktop only) — no MOQ line and no "View style" affordance/chevron any more (both removed, along with their now-unused `productCard.meta`/`affordance`/`affordanceIcon` recipe tokens). A published card's own `<Link>` wrapper (normal pointer/hover) is enough to signal it's clickable on its own; a draft card keeps its muted "Coming soon" label (`productCard.comingSoon`), the one thing left distinguishing it from a real, clickable style.

Draft status cascades everywhere a style's real existence matters, not just the card's own click behavior: `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`'s own `generateStaticParams` filters to `status === "published"` only, and the same file sets `export const dynamicParams = false` — so a direct hit on a draft style's URL 404s immediately rather than falling through to an on-demand dynamic render of a thin, half-empty page (confirmed live: `getData()` would happily find a draft style by slug, since it's still a real `styleCards` entry — the `dynamicParams` gate is what actually prevents that page from ever rendering; the page component's and `generateMetadata`'s own `data.product.status !== "published"` checks are kept too, as defense in depth). `app/sitemap.ts` and the PLP's own `collectionPageSchema()` `ItemList` call both filter to published styles the same way — a draft style shows on the grid (that's the point of "Coming soon"), but is never claimed as a real, indexable page in either place. Only "High-Waisted Compression" is published today; the other 7 pilot cards (Flare / Wide-Leg, Scrunch / Ruched, V-Back / Crossover, Capri / Cropped, Pocket, Biker / Short, Fleece-Lined) are draft, replacing the previous 8 fully-generic placeholder cards (pocket-leggings, compression-leggings, etc.) that had never been real PDP destinations either. Flip a card from draft to published only once its real PDP content (description, FAQs, specifications, gallery) actually exists — see the "Parked, not fixed" decision log entry, 2026-09-02, in `docs/05-plan.md`, and treat a draft card's own `slug` as provisional until then (its permanent URL is only locked in once it's published).

**Verified (card format rewrite)**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean (only `high-waisted-compression` generates a PDP route). Live on `/activewear/leggings`: all 8 cards read title + one spec line, no MOQ, no "View style"; card 1 confirmed a real `<a href>` with `cursor: pointer`; cards 2 to 8 confirmed plain `<div aria-disabled="true">` with `cursor: default` and a "Coming soon" label. `/sitemap.xml` lists only the PLP and the one published PDP. The `CollectionPage`/`ItemList` JSON-LD lists exactly one `Product` (High-Waisted Compression).

`ProductCard` reuses `MediaPlaceholder` for the tile image (not a new placeholder box) — new `79:100` ratio added to `MediaPlaceholder`'s own ratio table for the 316×400 tile, kept exact rather than rounded to the existing `4:5`, same precedent every other one-off ratio there already sets. **`showLabel={false}`** (owner report, 2026-08-30, comparing against node 590:1173: "follow ... all the design elements") — neither the desktop nor mobile Figma frame ever shows a text label inside this box, real or placeholder; the tile's own title already renders in the `<h3>` right below it, so the overlay label (`MediaPlaceholder`'s general-purpose default) was a redundant, un-designed artifact here, not a real design element to match. `label` still supplies the alt text once a real image lands.

**Title wraps normally on mobile, never truncated** — was `max-xl:truncate` (1 line, ellipsis), correct back when card titles were short style names alone. The 2026-09-02 card format rewrite lengthened every title to "Custom [Style] Leggings," which made most of them clip mid-word on mobile; owner correction: "current titles are truncated, make them in 2 lines if they go beyond 1 line do not truncate them." `max-xl:truncate` dropped entirely (no `line-clamp-2` either — that still truncates with an ellipsis past 2 lines, which the owner's own wording rules out) — mobile now reflows to however many lines a given title genuinely needs (2 for every current title, confirmed live). Desktop keeps `truncate` — not part of this request. Font size/weight (14px/medium) unchanged throughout.

**`title`/`subline` font-size threshold moved max-xl:/xl: → max-md:/md:, `xl:truncate` left alone (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — 14px→18px / 12px→16px, fixed values, desktop always larger. `xl:truncate` deliberately stays `xl:`-gated, not moved to `md:` — `productGrid.grid`'s own 2-column→3-column switch is still `xl:`-only too (deferred alongside `CategoryFilters`' sidebar, see below), so the grid stays 2 columns through tablet width; truncating a still-narrow tablet card's title the same way a wide desktop column does would read wrong. Confirmed live: card title measures 18px at 1024px (was 14px), unchanged at 375px (14px) and 1440px (18px, `xl:truncate` active).

**Subline history — hidden on mobile, then restored (2026-09-02)**: originally removed entirely on mobile (owner, first: "Available in custom fabrics & colors, should be In custom fabrics & colors on mobile" — a shorter derived string, shipped as two `<p>`s split by breakpoint; then, same day: "Remove ' In custom fabrics & colors' from the product tile only in mobile"), with the "in custom fabrics & colors" detail moved up to `CategoryMetaStrip`'s own mobile subline instead. That reasoning stopped applying once the card format rewrite (5cr, same day) replaced `cardSubline` with each style's own one-line distinguishing spec ("Squat-proof compression, 4-way stretch," etc.) — no longer a duplicate-copy concern. Owner correction: "mobile does not have subline that shows on the desktop" — the `max-xl:hidden` class is gone; `<p className={productCard.subline}>` now renders at every breakpoint, confirmed live (`sublineVisible: true` on all 8 cards, both viewports).

**"Coming soon" label removed from the rendered card, front end only (owner, same day)**: "Remove coming soon on both platforms, only from the front end and keep it in the backend. Anything which is in draft just don't make it clickable." The `<span>Coming soon</span>` and its own `productCard.comingSoon` recipe token are both gone — nothing about `status`, the non-clickable `<div>`/`aria-disabled="true"` treatment, or any route/sitemap/schema filter reading `status` changed; only the visible label disappeared. A draft card is now visually identical to a published one except for not being a link (no `href`, `cursor: default` instead of `pointer`) — confirmed live at both breakpoints, that alone is the only remaining signal it isn't clickable.

`ProductGrid` is a **client component**, paginating internally at a page size that now differs by breakpoint: `DESKTOP_PAGE_SIZE = 9` (owner correction, 2026-08-29: "by default our landing PLP would have 9 items, if 9 or less then 9 we will not show the pagination" — an earlier pass had this at `PAGE_SIZE = 3`, three real pages for the same 9 cards, which read as "only 3 products exist" until a page number was clicked) and `MOBILE_PAGE_SIZE = 8` (owner, 2026-08-30, alongside the real mobile grid frame node `590:1173`: "same rule apply to pagination if more 8 or less than 8 items ... don't show the pagination, if more than 8 then show pagination" — 8 is that frame's own real 2×4 tile count). `pageSize` tracks the same `xl` (1280px) cutoff every other responsive split on this page uses, via a `matchMedia("(min-width: 1280px)")` listener (mirrors `Header.tsx`'s own window-listener pattern for scroll state) rather than a second, width-duplicated grid. `page` clamps back into `totalPages` during render (React's own pattern for state that must track a prop the instant it changes, same as `MobileNav`'s own `rendered`/`revealed` state) if a breakpoint crossing shrinks the page count below the current page. `Pagination` only renders when `totalPages > 1` — this one check is *all* the auto show/hide logic needs at either page size, no extra branching. `Pagination` itself (`currentPage`/`totalPages`/`onPageChange` props) is real and clickable, not Figma's own placeholder "1 2 3 … 39" — clicking a page number swaps the rendered card slice.

**Pagination cell size differs by breakpoint, radius does not** (node `590:1173`, 2026-08-30): mobile cells are `40px`, matching that frame — desktop stays `48px` (its own already-confirmed value, untouched). Radius briefly went `max-xl:rounded-[12px]` matching that same frame's own screenshot, then reverted the same day (owner: "the pagination should not have radius, 0px only") — `rounded-none` now applies at every breakpoint, only the cell size still differs. Button copy corrected to `"‹ Previous"`/`"Next ›"` (was `"‹ Previous Page"`/`"Next Page ›"`, an earlier unconfirmed guess) — matches the mobile frame's real copy, applied at both breakpoints since the shorter text fits both and nothing ever confirmed the longer wording.

**Previous button icon is a real element, not a text glyph** (owner: "Pagination previous button icon should be left to the previous text") — the "‹" character baked into the same text node as "Previous" is replaced with `ChevronArrowIcon` (`components/icons/ChevronArrowIcon.tsx`, MobileNav's own reusable left-pointing chevron, unrotated) rendered as its own element before the text, inside `edgeCell`'s existing `flex items-center gap-1.5` row — a discrete element in flex order guarantees the left position regardless of font rendering, unlike a character glyph. `Next`'s own "›" stays a text glyph (not flagged, out of scope for this fix).

**Real bug: `edgeCell`'s fixed mobile width let it overflow into the next cell** (owner report, 2026-08-30, with a screenshot: "previous box is touching the other box, no spacing in between"). `max-xl:w-[80px]` was a hard constraint, but flex items default to `min-width: auto` — when the icon+gap+"Previous" content actually needed more room than 80px minus its own `px-5` padding left available (confirmed live: real content measured `~107px`, not `80px`), the button rendered *wider than its own declared width* to fit it, eating straight into the row's `gap-2` meant for the next cell. Not reproducible in every render (a first pass of testing at 360px/375px showed a correct gap — the exact overflow point depends on the browser/device's own font metrics), which is why it needed the owner's own screenshot to pin down. Fixed by dropping the fixed width entirely: `max-xl:w-fit` (plus `whitespace-nowrap`, so the icon and word never wrap awkwardly at any width) sizes the box to its real content instead, so it can never overflow itself regardless of font rendering. Desktop was never affected (no width class of its own at `xl`).

**Mobile top/bottom gaps corrected, no more doubling** (owner: "gap of pagination from the top of the product should be 40px" / "gap from pagination and the below separator should be 24px") — `row`'s own `py-12` (48px) had been the only source of both gaps at every breakpoint. Mobile now gets `max-xl:pt-10 max-xl:pb-0` — the page wrapper below `ProductGrid` already supplies its own confirmed `24px` bottom gap down to the section divider (`app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`'s own `max-xl:pb-6`), so `row` having a *second* bottom padding of its own would have doubled it. Desktop keeps `py-12` unchanged.

**Real mobile grid, not a 1-column fallback** (node `590:1173`, 2026-08-30, replacing the earlier `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2` safe fallback below): 2 columns at every width under `xl`, with that frame's own real gaps (8px between columns, 24px between rows, vs. desktop's 16px/40px) — a different value, not the desktop gap shrunk down. `ProductCard`'s own mobile sizing also comes from this frame: `max-xl:h-[220px]` on its `MediaPlaceholder` (an explicit height wins over the existing `79:100` desktop aspect-ratio class wherever both are set, since aspect-ratio only fills in a dimension left otherwise undefined — so this overrides cleanly below `xl` and leaves the desktop ratio alone above it), `max-xl:gap-3` between image and text (was a shared `gap-4` at every width), and `max-xl:text-[0.875rem]`/`max-xl:text-[0.75rem]` title/subline sizes (were a shared `1.125rem`/`1rem`) — desktop keeps its own pre-existing, already-confirmed values throughout.

**Pagination hover, scroll target, and a tablet-width image fix (owner, 2026-09-03)**:
- `pagination.cell`/`edgeCell` gained `hover:bg-[#FFF6F3]` (`edgeCell` as `enabled:hover:` so the disabled edge button never shows a hover it can't act on) — matches the "Download Catalog" secondary CTA's own confirmed hover color (`productOptions.secondaryDesktop`), owner: "when you hover on the next page 02 2 ... it should make the background the same hover color that we used Download catalog cta."
- Clicking a page number now scrolls to `CategoryMetaStrip`'s own title block (`id="plp-title"`, set on that component's root) via `document.getElementById(...).scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth", block: "start" })`, called inside a **double** `requestAnimationFrame` from `ProductGrid`'s own `handlePageChange`. Two wrong targets were tried and confirmed live to fail first: scrolling the grid's own container (its height/position shifts with page/card count, and even after fixing the *timing* — synchronous, one rAF, `behavior: "instant"` — it still wasn't the element the owner meant) before the owner's own words ("land me ... on top of the title where it says sports bras") named the real target, a sibling component this one has no ref to, reached by id instead. `behavior: "smooth"` is safe now that the target (`#plp-title`) never moves regardless of page/card count — the double rAF still matters independent of that, to measure `#plp-title` only after the browser has actually painted the new page.
- `productCard.image`'s fixed `220px` height — correct for real mobile, where it was tuned against node `590:1173`'s own 156×220 tiles — was `max-xl:`, so the same fixed height also applied through 768–1279px tablet width, where the grid column is much wider but the image stayed pinned to 220px tall, reading squashed/stretched instead of proportional (owner: PLP images "should not be too rectangular ... match desktop container ratio"). Scoped to `max-md:` instead — from `md:` (768px) up through `xl:`, the box falls through to its own `aspect-[79/100]` ratio, proportional at every tablet width, same ratio the desktop card already uses. Real mobile and desktop are both unaffected (confirmed live: 375px unchanged at 220px fixed, 1440px unchanged at the `79:100` ratio).

**`root` is a plain block, not sticky** — a same-day pass briefly made it sticky too (matching `categoryFilters.list`) to stop it drifting away from the sidebar once the sidebar locked, but that pinned the whole filters+grid row (`CategoryMetaStrip`'s title/chip row included, sitting directly above it) in place. Reverted the same day (owner correction: "you made the whole legging title area fixed too. Dont do that, only make the left panel fixed") — only `CategoryFilters` is sticky; this grid, its own heading row, and everything else on the page scroll normally, with no special treatment.

Gender filter chips ("All"/"Women"/"Men") are real, clickable `<button>`s with a `hover:border-accent hover:text-accent` state and real client `activeChip` state (owner request, 2026-08-28) — but still not wired to real filtering logic, since there's no real product data with gender metadata to filter by yet.

**Meta strip alignment**: originally two inline `<span>`s inside one `<p>`, aligned to the shared text baseline — a 2026-08-28 pass switched this to two separate `<p>`s in a `flex items-center gap-3` row, centred to each other's own box, against a design revision current at the time. **Reverted to baseline 2026-08-29** (owner report: "leggings heading should meet the design in figma") — re-checked against the current file (`get_design_context`/`get_screenshot` on node `406:3075`/`571:5329`), "Leggings" and "Every style, made to your brand spec." really are one inline text run sharing a single baseline in Figma, not two boxes centred to each other; `items-center` sat the smaller subline noticeably higher than that. Now `flex items-baseline gap-3` — reproduces the shared-baseline look while keeping title/subline as two separate elements (not reverting all the way to inline `<span>`s in one `<p>`), each still carrying its own colour/weight.

**Subline colour corrected to `text-ink`** (owner call, 2026-08-28, "layout changes to the heading" — a later Figma revision, `557:3424`): was the muted `#3c3c43` grey; the current design renders it the same near-black as the title.

**Two real overflow regressions, both found by the scoped mobile check, both fixed the same way**: (1) `CategoryFilters` (252px fixed) + `ProductGrid` in one `flex` row has no way to shrink gracefully the way `CategoryBanner`'s wrapping text does — fixed with `flex-col xl:flex-row` on the page wrapper (the grid's own column count is now a real, confirmed mobile value — see above — not a safe fallback). (2) The meta strip's title/subline pair and its chip row, both non-wrapping flex children of a non-wrapping row, forced `422px` into a `375px` viewport — fixed with `flex-wrap` on both `metaStrip` and `metaText`. No mobile Figma frame exists for `CategoryMetaStrip`'s own wrap behaviour, so that part stays a safe structural fallback — every real value there (30px/20px meta type) stays exactly as Figma specifies.

**Known pre-existing gap, found live 2026-08-30 while verifying this task, not fixed here (out of scope, flagged separately)**: `categoryFilters.list` is unconditionally `sticky`, with no `xl:` gate — since the page wrapper stacks Filters above this grid in `flex-col` below `xl`, the sticky positioning still applies on mobile and the panel visually floats over the grid and pagination as the page scrolls. Confirmed via a fresh Playwright screenshot at 375×812. A fix (gate to `xl:sticky`) has been queued as a follow-up task rather than folded into this one.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`, beside `CategoryFilters`, seeded with `content/activewear/leggings.ts`. `Breadcrumb` (built 5j) now renders above this row too — first real page usage, was styleguide-only before this.

**Card title is now `<h3>`, not `<p>`** (SEO/AEO/GEO finalization pass, 2026-08-30): a product tile title is a sub-item under the grid's own `<h2>` (`CategoryMetaStrip`'s title, see below), the same "`h2` section → `h3` sub-item" pattern already used for `WhatWeMake`'s category tiles and Trust Signals' entries elsewhere on the site — this page's own heading outline was missing that level entirely before. `className` unchanged, so nothing visual moved.

New `imageAlt` field on `StyleCard` (`content/activewear/types.ts`) — keyword-aware descriptive alt text per card (e.g. "Custom high-waisted compression leggings"), separate from `cardTitle` (the short on-card label) since an alt needs to stand alone with no card context around it. Wired into `MediaPlaceholder`'s `image.alt`; has no visible effect yet since every card's `image` is still an empty string (no `<img>` renders at all until real photography lands), but the data is ready the moment it does.

### FabricOptions — Built (responsive: desktop table, mobile accordion)
`components/sections/FabricOptions.tsx` · recipe: `fabricOptions`

The PLP's fabric comparison table (Figma node `579:5632`, 2026-08-30): eyebrow + H2, a 3-column table (Fabric / Best For / Performance), and a closing note with two bold inline phrases.

Props: `eyebrow`, `heading`, `options: FabricOption[]`, `weightTiers?: WeightTier[]`, `note: NoteSegment[]`. `FabricOption` is 3 fields (`fabric`, `bestFor`, `performance`) — corrected from an earlier 4-field shape (`fabric`, `composition`, `feel`, `bestFor`) that didn't match what this design actually renders: every row is 3 plain text cells, fabric name+composition combined into one descriptive string (e.g. "Nylon or polyamide + elastane (70 to 85% / 15 to 30%)"), not a separate composition sub-field. `NoteSegment` (`{text, bold?}[]`) is a small typed run array for the note's two bold phrases — avoids embedding markup in a plain content string.

**`weightTiers`, optional (owner spec, 2026-09-02, T-Shirts category)** — a second, smaller real `<table>` (`WeightTier`: `{tier, gsm, bestFor}`), rendered between the main table/accordion and `note` only when the category actually provides it. Backward-compatible by construction: a category with no field renders nothing extra (Leggings/Sports Bras/Shorts confirmed live, exactly 1 `<table>` each, unchanged). No mobile accordion of its own — deliberately lighter than the main table's own heavier per-row expand/collapse mechanism, since this is a secondary, 3-short-column element: wrapped in its own `overflow-x-auto` instead, so it scrolls horizontally rather than forcing page-level overflow at narrow widths (confirmed live, no overflow at 360px). First pass folded this same data into `note` as prose instead (the brief that added T-Shirts said "no new page code"); this replaced that once the owner explicitly authorized the small, optional addition — see the decision log entry, 2026-09-02, "Fourth real Activewear category added."

**`weightTiersHeaders`, optional (owner spec, 2026-09-02, Compression & Base Layers category)** — overrides the 3 `<th>` labels on `weightTiers`' own table (`WeightTiersHeaders`: `{tier, value, bestFor}`, default `{tier: "Tier", value: "GSM", bestFor: "Best for"}`). The underlying `WeightTier` data shape is unchanged and reused as-is — Compression & Base Layers sets this to `{tier: "Level", value: "mmHg", bestFor: "Used for"}` and stores "Light"/"Medium"/"Firm" in each row's own `tier` field, the mmHg range in `gsm` — same 3 generic columns relabeled for a genuinely different kind of tiered data, not a new table type. Confirmed live: every prior category using `weightTiers` (Hoodies, Sweatshirts, Long-Sleeve Tops, Joggers & Track Pants) omits this prop and still renders "Tier"/"GSM"/"Best for" unchanged.

**`structuredBlock`, optional (owner spec, 2026-09-05, Teamwear/Cricket)** — the reusable block under the main table, generalized to a discriminated union (`StructuredBlock`, `content/activewear/types.ts`): `{type: "weightTiers", tiers, headers?}`, `{type: "decoration", eyebrow, heading, rows: DecorationRow[], note?}`, or `{type: "none"}`. Only the "decoration" variant is actually wired into `FabricOptions.tsx` today — a category's own eyebrow + H3 (`fabricOptions.decorationWrap`/`decorationEyebrow`/`decorationHeading`) above a 3-column Method/Best for/Notes table, reusing the exact `weightTiers*` table recipe classes (same `overflow-x-auto` wrapper, same header/row/cell classes) rather than a second, near-identical table recipe; the block's own optional closing note reuses `fabricOptions.note` directly. Purely additive: `weightTiers`/`weightTiersHeaders` stay the direct prop path for every existing Activewear category (unchanged, still passed straight through to `FabricOptions`), all of which omit `structuredBlock` and render unchanged — confirmed live, `/activewear/jackets` (a `weightTiers` category) unaffected. Cricket (`content/teamwear/cricket.ts`) is the first and so far only category to set `structuredBlock`, since its own reusable block is a decoration/print-method breakdown, not a GSM or mmHg weight tier.

`options[0]` renders directly beneath the column header labels (Fabric/Best For/Performance) — matches the real Figma structure exactly: header labels are grouped with row 1 (one 48px internal gap per column, `get_metadata` confirms they share row 1's own layout group), not repeated per row. **One** divider follows that combined header+row1 block; `options.slice(1)` (rows 2 onward) render as plain data rows, each with its own top padding and its own trailing divider — including the last row, matching the literal Figma layer structure rather than a guessed "no divider after the last row" convention.

**Real text-alignment bug fixed (owner report, 2026-08-30, "fix fabric options text alignment")**: row 1's three cells are real `<th scope="col">` elements (SEO/AEO finalization pass), which the browser's own UA stylesheet centre-aligns by default; every row below uses plain `<td>`s, left-aligned by default. With no explicit alignment anywhere in this recipe, row 1 alone rendered centred while every row below it rendered left — a real visible mismatch, not a design choice. Fixed with one `text-left` on `fabricOptions.headerRow` (covers all three header cells via inheritance, since they share that one row class) — the data rows needed no change, already correct by default.

Own bespoke 1164px centred column (`x=138` on a 1440 frame, `get_metadata`), not `container-p` — same "section gets its own custom width" precedent as `CategoryBanner`'s 685px column. `#e8ecf1` divider colour is the same real value already used for `CategoryFilters`/`ProductGrid`'s own borders elsewhere on this page.

**Heading forces an explicit two-line break** (owner report, 2026-08-30: the first line should read "The fabrics behind the", not "The fabrics behind"). Natural text-wrap can't produce that split at this heading's own 54px size — measured directly: the full sentence fits unbroken from ~812px wide, and any narrower width wraps it as "The fabrics behind" / "the big brands" instead, never landing on "...the" / "big brands". `fabricHeading` carries a literal `"\n"` in the content itself (`"The fabrics behind the\nbig brands"`), rendered via `whitespace-pre-line` on `fabricOptions.heading`.

**Mobile accordion, replacing the earlier stacked-table fallback** (Figma node `590:1488`, "Content", 2026-08-30 — a real mobile frame now exists): below `xl` the `<table>` is `hidden` entirely; a separate `xl:hidden` accordion (`fabricOptions.accordionStack` and friends) renders the same `options` data instead. First row opens by default, one row open at a time (`openIndex` state in the component, seeded to `0`) — clicking the open row's own header collapses it (`setOpenIndex(-1)`), clicking any collapsed row opens it and closes whichever was open. The expanded row shows its Best For/Performance detail (`#f2f2f7` box, `18px` title, `14px` uppercase `#727272` labels); collapsed rows are a bordered (`#e8ecf1`) single ellipsis-truncated line with a chevron. Reuses `CategoryFilters`' own zero-JS `grid-template-rows` expand technique (`accordionDetailGrid`/`accordionDetailGridOpen`/`accordionDetailClip`, `components/ui/styles.ts`) and `FilterChevronIcon` (`components/icons/FilterChevronIcon.tsx`) rather than a new icon export — Figma's own two icon states for this accordion are the same chevron glyph, just recoloured/rotated, which that icon already reproduces. Component is now `"use client"` (was a plain server component) for this state.

**Every row mounts the same structure always (real bug fixed, owner report, 2026-08-30: "the transition... should be very smooth... when I expand the content, the title moves a little")**: the first build branched to two different markup shapes per row (open: detail block mounted straight at `grid-rows-[1fr]`; closed: no detail block in the DOM at all) — a CSS transition can't animate a value that's freshly mounted or about to unmount, so toggling just snapped instead of animating. Fixed to match `CategoryFilters`' own proven pattern exactly: every row's header + detail block stay mounted always, only the grid track class toggles (`accordionDetailGrid` 0fr ↔ `accordionDetailGridOpen` 1fr). The title-shift was a separate, real border-box bug: the closed row had a `border` (1px) and the open row had none — adding/removing a border shifts the interior content box by the border's own width. Fixed with a shared `accordionItem` wrapper that keeps `border` (1px) present at both states, only ever toggling its *colour* (`accordionItemOpen`'s `border-transparent` vs `accordionItemClosed`'s `border-[#e8ecf1]`) — same box geometry, so the title never moves. `transition-colors duration-300` on that same wrapper smooths the bg/border swap too.

Heading/eyebrow gained a responsive split at the same time, matching `WhatWeCover`'s own established mobile/desktop pattern exactly: `eyebrow` `text-base` (16px) → `xl:text-[1.25rem]` (20px); `heading` `text-[1.875rem]`/`leading-[34px]`/`font-normal` (30px/34px) → `xl:text-[3.375rem]`/`xl:leading-[64px]`/`xl:font-medium` (54px/64px). The closing note's max-width also splits: `max-w-[320px]` mobile → `xl:max-w-[570px]` desktop.

**Note's own type size corrected the same day** (owner: "font for this should be 18px... follow the same as in other sections") — was a flat `text-[1.25rem]` (20px) at every breakpoint; now `text-[1.125rem]`/`leading-6` (18px) below `xl`, `xl:text-[1.25rem]`/`xl:leading-7` (20px) at `xl`+, matching the same responsive split this page's other body copy already uses (e.g. `whatWeCover.itemBody`).

**Real double-padding bug, found the same day** (owner report: "Container width is 320px... follow all design elements, fonts etc. spacing"): `accordionStack` originally carried its own `px-5`, on top of `fabricOptions.section`'s own `px-5` (its direct parent) — a real double inset, not a design choice, shrinking the mobile content column to `viewport − 80px` (measured `295px` at 375px) instead of the correct `viewport − 40px` (Figma's own `320px` at its `360px` frame). Fixed by dropping the redundant `px-5` from `accordionStack` — `section`'s own padding is enough. Also found `accordionStack` had **no** top margin at all (Figma's `Content` root: `gap-[32px]` between `Title` and `Accordions Stack`) — added `mt-8`. `accordionLabel` ("BEST FOR"/"PERFORMANCE") also had a `leading-none` that doesn't match Figma's `leading-[normal]` — removed. Confirmed live: accordion stack now measures exactly `335px` at a 375px viewport (`375 − 20×2`), heading-to-accordion gap exactly `32px`.

**A real spacing bug found wiring the page-level divider above this section**: the owner's own explicit numbers (56px gap above the divider, 120px below it) measured `120px`/`120px` at first — the filters+grid row already had 64px of its own bottom padding sitting directly against the divider, and adding a separate top margin on the divider doubled it. Fixed by folding the 56px into that row's own existing padding instead of stacking a second margin on the divider.

**Typography threshold moved max-xl:/xl: → max-md:/md: (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)**: `eyebrow`/`heading`/`note` all switch to their desktop size at 768px instead of 1280px now. `heading`'s own `headingBlock` width moved with it (`md:max-w-[750px]`, `xl:` restoring the original 579px→750px pair) — load-bearing together, since 750px is specifically what lets the heading's forced "The fabrics behind the" line-1 text fit on one line at its 54px size; widening the box without growing the heading at the same breakpoint (or vice versa) would reintroduce the exact 3-line wrap bug documented above, at tablet width instead of desktop. Confirmed live: heading still wraps to exactly 2 lines at 768px and 1024px. **The desktop `<table>`/mobile accordion structural split was deliberately left `xl:`-gated, not extended to `md:`** — its three fixed columns (`302px`/`293px`/`293px` + `132px` gaps ≈ 1152px) are already flagged as tight even at 1280px and would not fit a 768–1279px container at all; shrinking three fixed data columns needs its own dedicated pass, not a same-day judgment call bundled into this typography/layout review. The accordion stays the real mobile-and-tablet experience for now.

**That same divider's own width corrected to 1440px** (owner: "the divider under products is 1440px in width") — an intermediate pass (wrongly) matched it to this section's own 1164px content column, assuming it was the same kind of element; it's actually its own, wider divider, edge to edge of the site's real 1440px page cap, not inset to any section's content width.

**Gap to the next section (`TrustPoints`), corrected three times the same day: 120px → 80px → 102px → 104px** (owner: "120px gap make it 80px", then "make the 80px gaps... 102px", then "make it 104px not 102px" — all describing the gap "between the 3 new sections", `FabricOptions`/`TrustPoints`/`WhatWeCover`): each of the 3 sections had its own `pt`/`pb-[120px]` at the boundary it shares with a neighbour; since padding doesn't collapse like margin does, two adjacent 120px paddings summed to a real 240px gap, not 120px. Each correction halves the new target onto both sides of the shared boundary so two adjacent sides sum to the real value — final state: `section`'s own `pb` here is `52px` (net `104px` with `TrustPoints`' own `pt-[52px]`).

**Row spacing corrected 2026-08-30** (owner report: "the gaps under the content and in between is 32px"): the first build had a `gap-12` (48px) flex gap on the outer row list stacked on top of each row's own 32px padding, doubling real spacing in places. Re-pulled `get_metadata` with exact per-row y-coordinates: there's zero gap between one row's own box and the next — each row instead carries its own 32px padding on both sides (32px before its own trailing divider, then the next row's own 32px again before its content). Removed the outer `gap-12`; `headerRow`/`dataRow` now each own `pb-8` (`dataRow` also `pt-8`) instead. 138px side padding re-confirmed unaffected, still exact at both edges.

**A real `888px`-not-`1164px` border-box bug, found the same day** (owner: "separator width is 1164px"): `section` originally capped at `xl:max-w-[1164px]` with `xl:px-[138px]` — under Tailwind's `border-box` sizing, `max-width` caps the OUTER box including padding, so the 138px padding on each side ate into that 1164 cap instead of sitting outside it, netting an `888px` content box (`1164 − 138×2`), not the real 1164px Figma specifies. Affected the entire section (heading block, table, note), not just the page-level divider that sits above it (same bug, same fix, independently also hit and fixed on `TrustPoints` the same day). Fixed by capping at `max-w-[1440px]` (the site's own real page cap, matches `--container-page`) instead — 138px padding on a 1440-wide box is what correctly nets `1164px` of content. Confirmed live: section outer width now `1440px`, content `1164px`.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`. **Order swapped 2026-08-30** (owner: "make [WhatWeCover] 1st and [this] as 3rd") — now the last of the 3 new sections (was first, directly below the section divider). Its own top/bottom padding was reassigned to match: gained a `pt-[52px]` (pairs with `TrustPoints`' own `pb-[52px]` above it) and its `pb` became `120px` (the page-end value `WhatWeCover` used to carry when it was last).

**Gap to `TrustPoints` corrected again, 2026-09-01** (owner: "the spacing between the white 3 sections in 104px, make it 12px") — `pt-[52px]` → `pt-1.5` (6px), pairing with `TrustPoints`' own `pb-1.5` for a real 12px gap. Its own `pb-[120px]` (page-end spacing) is unrelated and untouched.

**Real `<table>` markup, not styled divs** (SEO/AEO/GEO finalization pass, 2026-08-30 — docs/06-seo.md: render the fabric table as real HTML so an answer engine can lift it, not a div grid). Column headers ("Fabric"/"Best for"/"Performance") stay grouped with row 1's own values inside the same `<th scope="col">`, matching this design's real, deliberate structure exactly (`get_metadata`: header labels share row 1's own layout group, not a separate header row) — `scope="col"` still tells a screen reader or crawler these are column headers for every value below them.

**A real, reproducible zero-width `<td>` bug found converting to a table**: the div version's standalone divider (a sibling element after each row) became a `<td colSpan={3}>` wrapping a divider `<div>` when first converted to `<tr>`/`<td>` — and resolved to a genuinely zero-width table cell in testing. Root cause: forcing the `<table>`'s own `display` to `flex` (needed to keep this section's existing custom layout) makes `<thead>`/`<tbody>` compute to `block`, but a bare `<tr>`/`<td>` with no sibling content inside that `block` context still tries to compute the table-specific `table-row`/`table-cell` displays independently, with no active table layout algorithm left to actually size it — an ambiguous partial-table state, not a one-off rendering glitch (confirmed reproducible via `getComputedStyle`). Fixed by dropping the standalone divider element entirely and moving to a `border-b border-[#e8ecf1]` directly on each already-explicitly-sized flex `<tr>` (`headerRow`/`dataRow`) instead — a border on an element with a well-defined width by construction has no such ambiguity. Same visual result (a hairline below every row, including the last), simpler DOM.

**A real overflow found by the viewport QA run, flagged then fixed the same day**: at exactly 1280×720 this table's fixed `302px`/`293px`/`293px` columns plus their `132px` gaps need `1152px` of content width, but the section's own `xl:px-[138px]` only leaves `1004px` available at that exact viewport (`1280 − 138×2`) — a `10px` overflow, pre-existing before the table-tag conversion above (confirmed: the same column/gap values were already there; swapping `div` for `table`/`tr`/`td` doesn't change any width). Root cause, once actually traced: the header row's own cells (`colFabric`/`col`) never had `shrink-0` and already shrank correctly at this width; `fabricCellCol`/`bodyCellCol` (the data rows' own cells) did, blocking them from narrowing to match. Removed `xl:shrink-0` from both — same standing rule already in this project's own decision log ("a fixed-width flex row that must fit multiple desktop viewports never uses `shrink-0` on its children"): default `flex-shrink: 1` lets all three columns narrow together, proportionally, so the row fits at every desktop width without losing its ratio. Confirmed live at 1440px that the table still measures the real `1164px`/`302`+`293`+`293px` once there's enough room.

### TrustPoints — Built (desktop-first, safe mobile fallback)
`components/sections/TrustPoints.tsx` · recipe: `trustPoints`

The PLP's quality/QC trust list (Figma node `579:5493`, 2026-08-30): H2 + subline (no eyebrow — this section's own pattern, same as `CategoryBanner`'s h1+subline, not the sitewide eyebrow+H2 `SectionHeading` rule), then a bordered list of 5 rows, each a diamond glyph plus one line of copy.

Props: `heading`, `subline`, `points: string[]`. Row glyph is lucide's `Sparkle` (`fill="currentColor"`, `text-accent`) — the same 4-point shape this project already reuses for `CategoryBanner`'s trust bullets, `Marquee`'s separator, and `Header`'s mega-menu promo bullets, not a new icon file. Row text is one shared `1.875rem` (30px) size across all 5 points — Figma's own file has 3 of the 5 rows at 30px and the other two at 26px/28px, read as inconsistent manual overrides in the file rather than 5 deliberately different sizes for otherwise identically-styled rows.

**Left-aligned, not the centred headingBlock its siblings use**: `FabricOptions`/`WhatWeCover` both centre their heading block; this one's own Figma export has it at a fixed 650px width flush with the section's own left edge — the same left-aligned convention as `CategoryBanner`/`CategoryMetaStrip`, not a centred one. Same bespoke 1164px column as those two siblings (`x=138` on a 1440 frame). A same-day pass briefly tried a responsive fallback below `xl` here (to avoid overflow under 1440px); reverted on explicit owner instruction ("The section should follow th gaps from right and left 138px") back to a flat `px-[138px]`. Row padding (`py-6`) is exactly the requested 24px top/bottom, confirmed live rather than changed.

**Real bug, found the same day**: `section`'s `max-w-[1164px]` capped the OUTER box (padding included) at 1164, so the `px-[138px]` was eating INTO that already-1164 box — every row's own width measured `888px`, not the real `1164px` (owner report: "sparator width is 1164px"). Fixed by capping the outer box at `1440px` instead (this section's real total width) so the 138px padding nets out to exactly `1164px` of content, matching what `FabricOptions`/`WhatWeCover` already get right.

**Reused on the PDP (2026-09-01), with a `sidePadding` variant (`"plp"` default / `"pdp"`)**: the PDP's own Figma frame (`634:5189`) wants 80px side padding and no title max-width cap, vs. the PLP's 138px/650px-cap. Added `trustPoints.sidePaddingPlp`/`sidePaddingPdp` and `headingMaxWidthPlp`/`headingMaxWidthPdp` tokens, selected via the new `sidePadding` prop, rather than a `className` override — two conflicting `px-*`/`max-w-*` utilities can't reliably override each other by class-string order, confirmed live when a first attempt at a plain `className` override silently lost the cascade.

**`section`'s own bottom padding moved into the two `sidePadding*` tokens too (2026-09-07, owner: "make it 48px for mobile")**: PDP's own mobile bottom gap needed to drop to 48px while PLP's stayed at 72px — the same "one token per variant, not two conflicting same-specificity utilities" reasoning as the `px-*`/`max-w-*` split above, since a bare `pb-*` on the shared `section` string and a new mobile override on `sidePaddingPdp` would both be unprefixed (mobile-default) utilities fighting for the same property. `section` no longer carries any `pb-*` of its own; `sidePaddingPlp` gained `pb-[72px]` (unprefixed, restoring the exact value/every-breakpoint behaviour that used to live on `section`, so the PLP is pixel-identical to before); `sidePaddingPdp` is now `pb-12 md:pb-[72px] xl:pb-[120px]` — 48px mobile (today's request), 72px tablet (fixed 2026-09-07 earlier the same day: "make it 72px only on both mobile and tablet"), 120px desktop (unchanged, 2026-09-02). `ProductRelatedStyles`' own `topRuleNone` (which relies on this section's bottom padding to supply its top gap, owning none itself) needed no change — it was already a passive "whatever the previous section's own bottom padding is" relationship, not a value it duplicates. Confirmed live at 375px (48px), 768px (72px) and 1440px (120px) on the PDP, and 375px/1440px (72px, unchanged) on the PLP.

**PDP tablet: gained its own real top gap, and its tablet bottom dropped to 0 (owner, same day, follow-up: "for PDP tablet, follow 120px from top of each section, 0px from the bottom" — for this section, `ProductSpecifications`, and `ProductCustomizeSteps`/"HOW WE CUSTOMIZE" specifically)** — before this, this section owned no top padding on the PDP at any width, real mobile included; the visible gap above it always came entirely from whatever `ProductCustomizeSteps`' own bottom padding happened to be (a deliberate past decision, "PDP wasn't part of that request, so its own gap stays whatever `ProductCustomizeSteps` above it already supplies"). `sidePaddingPdp` gained `md:pt-[120px]` (this section's first real top gap of its own, tablet-only) and its existing `md:pb-[72px]` became `md:pb-0`; `xl:pt-0` was added explicitly to cancel the `md:pt-[120px]` cascade at desktop, since desktop wasn't part of this request and still relies on `ProductCustomizeSteps`' own unchanged `xl:pb-[200px]` for its gap, exactly as before. Real mobile (`pb-12`) is untouched either way. A follow-up question surfaced that zeroing this section's tablet bottom would collapse its own gap to the next section (`ProductRelatedStyles`' mobile/tablet instance, which relied on this section's bottom the same passive way) down to 0px — see that entry's own note for the fix. Confirmed live on the PDP: 120px above, 0px below at 768–1279px tablet; unchanged (0px above, relying on `ProductCustomizeSteps`; 120px below) at 1440px `xl:`; unchanged (48px below, no top of its own) at real mobile.

**Mobile heading gained Tailwind's `text-wrap` utility (`text-wrap: wrap`), overriding the sitewide `h1/h2/h3 { text-wrap: balance }` default** (owner, 2026-09-01: "Built to pass the" / "squat test" was leaving visible room on line 1 that "squat" would fit in — `balance` deliberately evens the split over greedy-filling). A one-heading override, not a global change. First attempted as `[text-wrap:normal]`, which silently did nothing — `normal` isn't a valid `text-wrap` value, so the browser drops the whole declaration as invalid; `text-wrap` (→ `wrap`) is the correct utility.

**`subline` now uses the `text-subline` token (`#17191E`)**, not `text-ink` (2026-09-01, see `docs/02-design-system.md`'s Colour section and the decision log) — a sitewide title+subline colour rule on white/paper backgrounds, applied at the recipe level.

**Mobile-only artwork, two passes (owner, 2026-09-03 then 2026-09-04)**: `artworkWrap` is mobile-only (no desktop frame exists for this image at all) at a fixed `16:11` ratio, `w-full` — correct at real phone width (~320–390px, ~220–270px tall, matching node `590:1258`'s own 320×220 tile) but disproportionately tall once that same `w-full` stretched to the full 768–1279px tablet container width instead (up to ~880px tall at 1279px). First fix (2026-09-03) capped it at `md:max-w-[420px] md:mx-auto` instead — same real-phone-sized box, centered, rather than growing with the viewport. Corrected the next day (owner, reviewing PLP/PDP screenshots of this and two sibling sections: "does not need an image placeholder... follow the website style" — meaning follow *desktop*, which has no image here at all) — the cap was still visible-but-resized at tablet width; now hidden entirely from `md:` up (`xl:hidden` → `md:hidden`, the width-cap classes dropped as dead weight), matching desktop exactly. Real mobile (`<md:`) is unaffected — still shows the image at its original size. `WhatWeCover`'s own artwork (below) and `ProductSpecifications`' own mobile image share the identical history and got the identical fix — see those entries.

**`heading`/`subline`/`pointText` font-size threshold moved xl:/max-xl: → md:/max-md: (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — `heading` reuses `sectionHeading.heading`'s own `text-h1` scale (fluid, safe at 768px, confirmed on the homepage pass); `subline`/`pointText` are fixed literals, desktop always larger. `section`'s own flat `px-[138px]` (unconditional, no breakpoint split at all) is unaffected. Confirmed live on the PDP (`sidePadding="pdp"`): heading measures ~44.8px at 1024px.

### WhatWeCover — Built (desktop only)
`components/sections/WhatWeCover.tsx` · recipe: `whatWeCover`

The PLP's coverage grid (Figma node `579:5580`, 2026-08-29/30): a centred eyebrow + H1-style heading over a 6-item, 2-column grid (Fabric, Color and print, Style and fit, Branding, Labels, Packaging), each its own title + one-line body with a bottom border.

Props: `eyebrow`, `heading`, `items: CoverageItem[]` (`{title, body}`, `content/activewear/types.ts`) — real Figma-confirmed copy in `content/activewear/leggings.ts`, no `PLACEHOLDER:` prefix, same treatment as `h1`/`trustBullets`/`fabricOptions`/`qualityPoints`. Same bespoke 1164px column (`x=138` on a 1440 frame) as its siblings `FabricOptions`/`TrustPoints`, built the same session — see `FabricOptions`' own header comment for why this isn't `container-p`.

**Flat `px-[138px]`, not a responsive fallback** (owner correction, 2026-08-30: "exactly follow the design" — the same instruction that corrected `TrustPoints`' own attempt at a responsive version back to flat). Kept overflow-safe on the styleguide's own always-must-pass 15-viewport suite by wrapping that one demo in its own `overflow-x-auto` (`app/styleguide/page.tsx`) instead of making the component responsive — the real PLP route this section lives on isn't in that permanent suite while its sibling sections stay desktop-first (see `tests/screenshots.spec.ts`'s own comment), so there was no real page this fallback was protecting.

**Every grid cell owns its own `py-8` (32px top and bottom), not a shared row `gap-y`** (owner correction, 2026-08-30: "the spacing within the content is 32px from top and bottom" — corrects a same-day first pass that used `pb-8` plus a `gap-y-8` grid gap, a "close enough" approximation reasoned to look identical; it wasn't what the owner asked for, and stacking both would have doubled the gap to 64px had padding been added without removing the grid gap first). Matches Figma's own literal per-cell structure exactly, confirmed via `get_metadata`, rather than the simplified equivalent this shipped with first.

**The exact same `max-w`/padding bug as `TrustPoints`, caught by re-measuring rather than assuming it was fine**: `section` originally capped its outer box at `max-w-[1164px]` while also carrying `px-[138px]` — the padding ate into that already-1164px box, netting only `888px` of real grid content width, not the intended `1164px`. Fixed the same way as `TrustPoints`: `max-w-[1440px]` (this section's real total width) so the 138px side padding nets out to exactly 1164px of content.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`. **Order swapped 2026-08-30** — now the first of the 3 new sections (was last, order was `FabricOptions` → `TrustPoints` → `WhatWeCover`, confirmed at the time via `get_metadata` on all three nodes; now `WhatWeCover` → `TrustPoints` → `FabricOptions`). Its own `pt` was dropped entirely now that it's first — the page-level section divider above already supplies that gap — and its `pb` became `52px` (was `120px`, the old page-end value from when it was last).

**Mobile-only artwork, two passes (owner, 2026-09-03 then 2026-09-04)**: same fixed-`16:11`-ratio, `w-full` defect as `TrustPoints`' own artwork above, same two-pass fix history — capped at `md:max-w-[420px] md:mx-auto` first, then corrected to hide entirely from `md:` up (`md:hidden`, cap dropped) once the owner asked for tablet width to just follow desktop (no image at all), not a resized version of the mobile one. See that entry for the full reasoning; confirmed live at 1024px both sections' artwork are hidden (was 420×261px mid-fix); unchanged at 375px real mobile.

**`eyebrow`/`heading`/`itemBody` font-size threshold moved xl:/768px → md: (owner, 2026-09-04: apply the homepage's tablet-width treatment to PLP/PDP)** — fixed values, desktop always larger. `headingBlock`'s own centred column stays a flat 579px at every breakpoint here (unlike `FabricOptions`' own heading, which needed a paired width change too — this heading has no forced line break to protect), so no coupled width change was needed. `section`'s own flat `px-[138px]` is unaffected.

**Gap to `TrustPoints` corrected again, 2026-09-01** (owner: "the spacing between the white 3 sections in 104px, make it 12px") — `pb-[52px]` → `pb-1.5` (6px), pairing with `TrustPoints`' own `pt-1.5` for a real 12px gap. The page-level divider above this section (its own top boundary) was corrected the same day too — `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`'s divider `xl:mb-[72px]` → `xl:mb-[120px]` (owner: "from top of the customization section make it 120px").

**Grid is a real `<ul>`/`<li>`, not styled divs** (SEO/AEO/GEO finalization pass, 2026-08-30 — docs/06-seo.md: "the fact lists" should be real HTML lists an answer engine can lift, not divs). Tag swap only, no class changes — `<ul>`/`<li>` carry no default display that fights the existing CSS grid, so nothing visual moved (Tailwind's own preflight already zeroes list markers/padding sitewide, confirmed by the identical precedent already working on `CategoryBanner`'s trust bullets and `TrustPoints`' own list).

**Placement resolved** (was left open in this entry's first version): re-ran `get_metadata` on all three section nodes directly (`FabricOptions`, this section, `WhatWeCover`) rather than the single node originally checked — all three share the same `x=0`/`width=1440` coordinate space and tile with zero gap in exactly this order: `FabricOptions` ends at `y=1020`, this section spans `1020`–`1792`, `WhatWeCover` starts at `1792`. Moved from after `WhatWeCover` to between `FabricOptions` and `WhatWeCover`, matching that confirmed order.

**Used by:** `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`, seeded with `content/activewear/leggings.ts`. Stays the middle of the 3 new sections after the 2026-08-30 reorder — was `FabricOptions` → `TrustPoints` → `WhatWeCover`, now `WhatWeCover` → `TrustPoints` → `FabricOptions` — so its own `pt-[52px]`/`pb-[52px]` needed no change at the time.

**Both gaps corrected again, 2026-09-01** (owner: "the spacing between the white 3 sections in 104px, make it 12px") — `pt-[52px]`/`pb-[52px]` → `pt-1.5`/`pb-1.5` (6px each), pairing with `WhatWeCover`'s own `pb-1.5` above and `FabricOptions`' own `pt-1.5` below for a real 12px gap at both boundaries.
